# Agent Development Instructions

This file is the working contract for future agents and developers in this repository. Keep it updated whenever setup, product flow, architecture, or constraints change.

## Documentation Sync

When making changes that affect setup, product behavior, architecture, or development constraints, update all four files together:

- `README.md`
- `README_zh.md`
- `AGENTS.md`
- `CLAUDE.md`

Do not update only one language README when the same information should be available in both. `CLAUDE.md` belongs in this set because Claude Code loads it and not this file: leave it out of a constraint change and the standard that actually reaches the agent goes stale while these three look current.

Keep the root READMEs as concise project entry points. Detailed behavior belongs in the paired canonical references under `docs/`: `product-and-api`, `deployment-and-configuration`, `operations`, and `development`. A change must also update the owning English/Chinese detail pair; do not copy the full detail back into the root READMEs.

External Agent onboarding is documented as the paired operational runbook
`docs/agent-mcp-memory-sop.md` / `docs/agent-mcp-memory-sop_zh.md`. Keep its UI path,
Codex/Claude configuration, scope table, runnable official-client command, Memory review
gate, and revocation guidance synchronized with the product/API contract and
`scripts/example_mcp_memory_client.py`.

## Numeric Limits and Truncation

Do not introduce result-changing literal slices or limits (for example
`hits[:20]`) in production paths. Reuse an existing protocol constant when the
number is invariant; use a validated `Settings` field when deployments may
trade quality for cost. User-authored data must never be silently truncated:
surface the shared rail in the frontend and reject over-limit API input. Keep
embedding/model-input truncation on its existing single configuration source so
online, batch, and backfill paths cannot diverge. Tests may use explicit numeric
fixtures and are outside this production-code rule.

## Tracking Completed Spec Features

`silicon_notebook_fangan.md` is the product spec; `fangan_done.md` records what has actually been implemented against it.

**Whenever you complete a feature defined in `silicon_notebook_fangan.md`, you MUST append/update the corresponding entry in `fangan_done.md`** in the same change. Specifically:

- Add the now-working capability under the relevant section (cite the spec section, e.g. §6.7, §12, when applicable).
- Move any item you just finished out of `fangan_done.md`'s "当前边界 / 未完成" list.
- Keep it factual and in sync with the code — describe what is real and verified, note where deterministic/offline fallbacks apply, and don't mark something done until `scripts/check.sh` passes for it.

Treat `fangan_done.md` as a required deliverable of finishing spec work, not an afterthought.

## Deep Report Credibility Contract

Deep reports reuse the shared intent contract and persist `result_scope` / `completeness_required`; until report-side complete enumeration exists, they must not present relevance retrieval as an exhaustive result. Build the visible corpus basis from SQL aggregates plus bounded representatives, never by copying a complete source-family map into intent, outline, polling payloads, or prompts; resolve identities only for source ids actually touched by evidence. A profile is absent for one of two unrelated reasons and must say which: a scoped run deliberately skips the whole-collection aggregate (`scope_restricted`), aggregation can fail (`failed`). Persist the reason instead of a bare empty profile, keep both fail-open, emit `report_corpus_profile_failed` only for the failure, and never let an unavailable marker — a non-empty object — reach a consumer that would format it as measured statistics. The profile counts the current notebook while retrieval is federated, so the disclosure must separately state how many distinct reference-library sources the body cited, derived from assembled references at no query cost. Stage wall clock (retrieve/synthesis/draft) is recorded as `report_stage_timing` with indices and milliseconds only — never a section title or evidence text — because model latency is recoverable from the LLM log and retrieval is not. `_generation_started_at` belongs to the store: a terminal understanding write must preserve it rather than overwrite the column wholesale. At every depth, all section retrieval completes before at most one report-wide synthesis call and parallel drafting; the sole gate is section count, since cross-section consistency is undefined for one section, so only single-section reports retain retrieve→draft streaming. Depth selects retrieval budgets only. Section drafting, the report-wide blueprint, and the final editor use explicit, separate, deployment-configurable output budgets rather than silently inheriting the global chat cap; provider/model bindings own total-context compatibility. Whole-report admission and per-report section fan-out are database-protection gates independent of physical model-service capacity; waiting jobs hold no database connection. The claim ledger is a post-hoc audit overlay on already-written prose, never a drafting input, so grounding is enforced per row: a statement that is not verbatim prose, or an evidence key that is illegal or absent from that same statement, vetoes that row — never the section's whole ledger. Submissions over the row cap are truncated to the first 24 rows; status is four-valued missing/available/partial/invalid (kept-plus-dropped is partial; an empty submission audits nothing and is invalid), and the availability count includes partial with a separately disclosed partial count. The blueprint claim-id membership check is removed entirely: prose legitimately contains claims beyond the blueprint, nothing downstream joins ledger rows to blueprint claims by id, and that check once zeroed every ledger of a blueprint-bearing report. The optional `frame_assignments` tags still degrade per entry: one unknown facet key or off-vocabulary value costs that tag only. The synthesis blueprint's per-claim facet tag follows the same organizational-tag principle: deterministic repair first (composite prefix narrowing plus unambiguous case-insensitive reverse lookup of facet ids, names, and declared values, with declared ids winning and shared spellings never guessed), then per-claim clearing on failure — a facet tag never discards the blueprint, while evidence bindings, section ownership, and structure stay atomic; repair/clear counts surface as the counts-only `report_synthesis_facet_tags` event. The safety argument for row-level disposal is monotonicity, not independence — exclusive-facet conflict detection aggregates across rows, but the kept row set is always a superset of the old all-or-nothing behavior's, so the detection surface only grows. Meaningful synthesis skip/failure and claim-ledger availability are user-visible, and model/validation failures fail open and remain observable; suppress only pure no-op receipts (`not_requested` with `0/N` ledgers) from a single-section report or a legacy report generated under the old depth ≥ 8 gate, never a multi-section no-op, skip/failure, or usable ledger. A generation with zero successful section bodies is `failed`, never `done`; if that failed row retains a confirmed outline, generation retry reuses the frozen intent/outline and atomically clears stale generated artifacts. The high-risk citation audit always discloses its result but automatic evidence-level downgrade is controlled by a separate default-off flag. The final editor audits and summarizes but never rewrites body sections or adds facts. Keep numeric rails only in `docs/product-and-api.md` and `docs/product-and-api_zh.md`.

## Retrieval Run And Report Runtime

Ask executes inside one request-local retrieval run; Deep Report planning and generation each execute inside a fresh, independent run. A run shares a thread-safe, successful-result-only query-embedding single-flight and (for reports) one bounded leaf-I/O semaphore through copied `ContextVar` state. Only actual KG/chunk/element/PPR leaf calls acquire that semaphore: orchestrators, section workers, and code holding a database connection must never acquire it while waiting for child work. Waiting for a report leaf slot is cancellation-aware and must recheck cancellation after acquisition before starting I/O; a leaf already inside a backend/database call is allowed to finish safely rather than being abandoned in the background. State and cached values must not cross requests or report stages; failures and `None` results remain retryable. Report planning may run the independent KG and raw-element probes concurrently within that same gate. Sufficiency and reasoning-action heuristics live in `services/reports/policy.py`; deployment-tunable rails use validated `Settings`, while structural protocol bounds remain named constants. All retrieval-run summaries, planning/generation/final-editor stage timings, and section-attempt events are fail-open and content-free: allow only stable stage/run kinds, opaque report/run ids, indices, counts, booleans, statuses, and milliseconds—never notebook/source ids, questions, section titles, queries, prompts, evidence, or exception text. Exact rails remain documented only in `docs/product-and-api.md` and `docs/product-and-api_zh.md`.

## Source Evidence Diversity

New ingestion removes only high-confidence repeated textual page-boundary artifacts before elements, chunks, embeddings, and KG facts are published; parser-explicit header/footer blocks count as boundaries even when intervening layout blocks place them away from the page's first or last textual position. It must keep one representative and must not globally deduplicate authored prose, tables, formulas, images, code, or same-page repetitions. Retrieval is a second, independent defense for historical and external-parser data: same-source normalized-text duplicates collapse before candidate/result caps in chunk and element lanes, reasoning accumulation, reflection summaries, and final Ask/Report assembly. Different sources remain separate provenance even when their text matches. Reflection and final synthesis must see the same capped diverse element view, with valid outline bindings taking closed-cap priority rather than bypassing the cap. Keep the normalization and selection policy shared instead of adding per-consumer variants, and document exact structural thresholds only in the paired product/API references.

## Parser Capability Registry And Generated-Question Index

Parser routing has one backend registry. Upload validation, built-in parser dispatch, cloud-format admission, the authenticated system-configuration response, and the import UI's supported-format hints must derive from it rather than restating extension sets. The browser receives only ordered capabilities, execution boundary, availability, and fixed unavailable-reason enums—never credentials, endpoints, paths, or raw errors—and deliberately does not surface the routing pipeline to users: they only see which formats are supported. Routing is automatic; a configured self-hosted MinerU path must never silently fall through to public cloud. The built-in parser remains the guaranteed fallback surface. Production ingestion has exactly one parser route: the startup-frozen ProviderChain with declarative `after`/`before` DAG edges. Freeze every link core route decision before provider I/O so an earlier failure cannot open public cloud. A link probe may perform at most one remote parse and pure mapping but has no persistence port; workbook reconciliation and core admission precede materialization, and only the accepted materializer may replace image assets. URL self-hosted/builtin fallbacks share one request-local download. The same per-source lock covers asset replacement, parsing, element replacement, and chunk-marker publication so concurrent reparses cannot delete one another's accepted assets. Do not restore the legacy dispatcher, facade patch seam, or an optional dual route.

Generated-question retrieval is a deployment-only optional supplement, never a user scope control or evidence source. `off` is zero-cost and the default; `shadow` may perform the bounded comparison and emit content-free counts but must return the exact baseline; only explicit `on` may append original chunks after baseline hits, without evicting or reordering them. Build it only through the offline `question-index` phase using `chunk_question_generation` plus `chunk_embedding`. Each question row points to an original chunk; retrieval hydrates/cites only that chunk, applies the frozen source ceiling before the bounded read, and skips rather than scanning beyond the deployment cap. Successful empty generation is persistently complete and idempotent, failures remain retryable, and reparse/delete/copy/migration preserve the original-chunk ownership contract. Exact numeric rails live only in `docs/product-and-api.md` / `_zh.md`.

## Full-Stack Parity (Backend ⇄ Frontend)

### Frontend Workspace State Ownership

`frontend/app/use-source-library.ts` is the sole owner of source rows and local retrieval selection, paging/search, source detail elements, reparse/delete busy state, delete tombstones, and parse polling. `page.tsx` must preserve the paired stable notebook + first-source-page opening read and commit that snapshot explicitly; it must not add an effect-driven duplicate source read. Source writes already sent to the backend may finish, but their UI commit is admitted only while the exact user/notebook/workspace owner token is still current. Notebook/user transitions synchronously invalidate old list/detail/poll owners and timers. The hook exposes readonly views, named commands, and narrow refresh effects only—never raw setters, repository/service-locator context, or setters from Ask, Report, KG, collection, or modal owners. Keep current request counts, delete A→B→A convergence, tombstone suppression, proxy detail reads, and parse-poll cadence unchanged, and do not introduce a global state library.

`frontend/app/use-ask-session.ts` is the sole owner of the Ask draft/transcript, intent preview and confirmation, durable stream/reconnect state, conversation history and tombstones, rename/delete/bulk actions, and answer feedback. The shell still owns the paired notebook/source opening read and explicitly asks this hook to restore one conversation list and at most one latest detail; it also retains Memory answer-link batching and other cross-domain presentation effects. Exact actor/notebook/workspace owners reject stale visible commits, while durable Ask jobs are detached—not cancelled—by navigation and may reconcile history after returning to the same actor/notebook identity. Only explicit Stop cancels: before `started`, the transport must remain readable until the job id arrives, then issue exactly one cancel before aborting locally. Because the synchronous cancellation endpoint has no enforceable whole-request database deadline, the browser keeps that one cancellation request in flight until authority answers; it must not abort it on a client timer or release retry authority early. Intent preview and confirmed execution reuse one frozen source/base-scope snapshot. The hook exposes readonly views and named commands, imports only Ask/pure contracts, never exports raw setters or receives another workspace owner's setters, preserves existing request counts and polling cadence, and does not introduce a global state library.

`frontend/app/use-report-workspace.ts` is the sole owner of the report list/detail, lazy entry read, list/detail polling, intent and outline submissions, generation/cancel/retry actions, share state, export selection, deletion busy state, and report deletion tombstones. Opening a notebook or a non-report tab performs zero report I/O; entering Reports performs one list read, and a pending-center focus reads one detail only after that list settles. Navigation detaches background report work and never calls cancel. Exact actor/notebook/view owners reject stale visible commits, while successful deletes always record an actor+notebook tombstone so A→B→A responses cannot resurrect a report. Every write command rechecks the live manage policy, freezes source/base scopes at creation, and preserves existing request counts and the mutually exclusive six-second list/detail poll cadence. The hook imports only report/pure contracts, exposes readonly views and named commands, never exports raw setters or receives another workspace owner's setters, and does not introduce a global state library.

The KG workspace owner is a thin composition layer over three independently testable domain owners sharing one gate. `frontend/app/use-kg-workspace.ts` composes `use-kg-knowledge.ts` (Knowledge rows/types/filter/page/duplicates/context), `use-kg-schema.ts` (Schema views and mutations) and `use-kg-graph.ts` (unified-graph search/range/node state, pending-merge review and tombstones, and durable KG build/relink/rebuild tracking) over the single actor/notebook/generation gate in `use-kg-owner.ts`; together they remain the sole owner of all of it. The three domains never see each other, and the gate holds no domain state and imports no API module: everything that spans domains (clear on owner change, invalidate, adopt a freshly established owner) is the composition layer routing the gate's fan-out into a domain's own named command — never one domain receiving another's setter. Notebook opening performs no Knowledge, Schema, or graph-content read; those remain explicit lazy commands, while the existing rebuild/relink status recovery probes remain notebook-scoped. Exact actor/notebook/generation owners reject stale visible commits, and actor+notebook identity keys preserve durable maintenance claims and merge-decision tombstones across A→B→A navigation. Every write command rechecks the live Knowledge/Schema/KG policy, read-only members never receive review controls or review-job recovery I/O, and graph/review/build polls remain single-flight at their existing cadences. Every one of the four hooks exposes readonly views and named commands, imports only Knowledge/KG/pure contracts, never exports raw setters or receives another workspace owner's setters, and does not introduce a global state library. `page.tsx` still consumes exactly one namespaced view (`.knowledge` / `.schema` / `.graph`) plus the flat command surface; the domains' internal fan-out commands never leave the composition layer.

`frontend/app/use-notebook-collection.ts` is the sole owner of actor-scoped notebook rows, bounded collection search, filter/sort/view/menu state, issued/published list watermarks, focus/visibility access reconciliation, collection editor/delete state, default-notebook creation single-flight, and notebook deletion tombstones. The shell retains the existing composite health + notebook-list + system-configuration bundle and explicitly begins/commits an opaque list ticket only after its sidecars settle; notebook opening remains the paired notebook + first-source-page read and adds no collection effect read. Search keeps the 250 ms gate and one bounded point search per visible notebook. Actor changes synchronously hide stale rows/dialogs, writes recheck the live row authority between stages, and successful deletes record an actor tombstone before any derived refresh so A→B→A or an older list cannot resurrect the card. The hook exposes readonly views, named commands, and narrow shell effects only—never raw setters, modal/sharing/source/Ask/Report/KG owners, or a global state store.

`frontend/app/use-root-modal-coordinator.ts` is the sole owner of root-dialog presentation leases, typed conflict/layer policy, actor/workspace/source scope generations, topmost arbitration, and safe focus return. Workspace transitions synchronously invalidate workspace/source slots before asynchronous navigation; actor replacement and logout synchronously invalidate every old slot; A→B→A never revives a lease. Async domain openers must issue a frozen lease before their read and publish it only while that exact owner and issue remain current. The coordinator has no domain payload, permission vocabulary, busy state, HTTP/API import, timeout, interval, repository, or business callback; payload and cleanup remain in their existing domain owner and enter only through the exhaustive close sink. Do not replace the typed slots with one global `activeModal`: the info layer may legally sit above a primary dialog, and only the topmost eligible dialog may own Escape/focus trapping. New centered dialogs keep using `FloatingModalCard`; opening, closing, or transitioning the coordinator itself adds zero I/O and zero timers.

Opening a notebook is **one transition with rollback**, not a hand-written begin/commit/finish sequence. `frontend/app/notebook-transition.ts` is the pure orchestrator (no React, DOM, or network): it runs every step's `begin` in declaration order and only then decides refusal (a short-circuit would leave later owners un-begun); then `enter` → `load` → `isCurrent` → `apply` → each step's optional `commit` in declaration order → `conclude`; then settles every successfully begun step exactly once, in **reverse** begin order, with the committed outcome or `null` for a rollback. Refusal, a failed parallel read, supersession at either guard, and a thrown error all converge on that single rollback path (a thrown error is rethrown verbatim afterwards), and a superseded transition settles only the tickets it minted itself — never the owners a newer transition has already claimed. `page.tsx::notebookTransitionSteps` is the **single registration point**: each owner declares its `begin`, optional `commit`, and `settle` there and nowhere else, so adding an owner is one list entry instead of four scattered edits whose omissions all fail silently. The root-modal step must stay first because its close sink is the single cleanup path for staged intake. `openNotebook` keeps only its own prologue and the plan declaration, with the four phases in named helpers; request counts (one parallel notebook + first-source-page read), workspace-epoch semantics and the logical position of both guards, late-response rejection, tombstones, `#notebook=<id>` history handling, and the failure landing spot are unchanged. Gates: `frontend/tests/guards/notebook-transition-guard.test.mjs` (no owner lifecycle call may appear inside the orchestration functions themselves) and `frontend/tests/unit/notebook-transition.test.mjs`.

Hidden-state views and the owner fan-out have one shape each. Every value an owner hook returns while its owner is not visible must be a **stable reference** (a module-level `Object.freeze` constant or a per-instance ref), never a bare `[]`/`{}`/zero-argument `new Set()`: consumer effects depend on those fields, a fresh reference per render re-runs them every frame, and page.tsx's Memory-link effect calls setState inside that branch, which is an infinite render loop (a real PR #557 regression, `Maximum update depth exceeded`); that effect's reset also carries its own bail-out rather than relying on dependency stability alone. Actor activation and leave fan-out over all owner hooks lives only in page.tsx's `activateWorkspaceOwners` / `leaveWorkspaceOwners` / `leaveActorOwners` (the first is shared by both authentication sites; the latter two serve returning to the collection and signing out and deliberately differ in membership), so a new owner hook touches only those. An owner view must never be re-flattened into top-level local aliases inside `Home` — neither field-by-field nor a second flattening layered on top of a namespace alias; the only sanctioned new shape is the namespace-level destructure itself (regression gate `frontend/tests/guards/owner-view-no-reflatten-guard.test.mjs`). Regression gates: `frontend/tests/guards/hook-view-stable-empty-guard.test.mjs` (semantic AST, unwrapping `as`/parentheses/`??` and the local-variable form) and `frontend/tests/guards/workspace-owner-transition-guard.test.mjs` (those calls may only originate from the three entry points); each hook's component test pins `Object.is` stability across re-renders.

`frontend/features/extension-sdk` is the only build-time workspace UI registry and host contract. The only wire slots are `workspace.side_panel` and `source.detail_section`. The first production contribution is the existing Agent Profile entry: its plugin component is presentation-only, performs zero domain I/O before click, and delegates one exact-owner `openUnderstanding` action to the existing modal/data owner. `workspace.side_panel` renders as a compact entry row inside the sources panel's fixed area, above the scrolling source list, reusing existing button classes and `:root` tokens with no colour literals; it must never add a dedicated workspace column (that column held a single entry, left the rest blank, and squeezed the chat panel). Collapsing the sources panel hides the entry with it (visually hidden but still Tab-focusable, matching the sources panel's existing behaviour), which is accepted. A committed workspace may issue one shared `/system/extensions` GET per actor generation; a failed request is not cached and is re-issued on the next committed workspace (e.g. a notebook switch) rather than a timer/poll. Collection/signed-out/empty-registry paths remain zero-I/O, and notebook transitions reuse the actor projection while synchronously invalidating visible entries and stale actions by actor/notebook/workspace generation. Rendering remains exact local tuple ∧ live availability ∧ core permission ∧ normalized UI mode ∧ current workspace owner. UI code is static—no remote JavaScript, runtime scan, mutable registry, or global store. The backend projection exposes no capability name, internal reason, topology, endpoint, path, credential, or exception text. Props are readonly summaries plus reviewed narrow actions; never pass page setters, hook internals, repositories, raw fetch, or a service locator. Cross-stack parity must remain non-vacuous. After adding a contribution, run `scripts/generate_ui_extension_contract.py` to refresh `backend/tests/fixtures/ui_extension_contract.json`; the contracts lane reconciles it with `--check`. **Deployment-time private UI plugin packages** load through the injection chain `registry.ts` (built-in, `.ts`-only closure) → `workspace-registry.ts` (merge) → the generated, gitignored `registry.local.ts`, produced by `SILICON_NOTEBOOK_UI_PLUGINS` and `npm run sync:ui-plugins` (`postinstall` plus five `pre*` hooks). A plugin may import only `contracts.ts`/`ui.tsx`/`react`/`lucide-react` and same-package siblings, never `api.ts` — the port is injected per `pluginId` via `actions.api` by the host, so a plugin that cannot import `api.ts` cannot forge another plugin's port. A plugin ships no CSS and no inlined colour literal (visuals reuse existing classes and `:root` tokens) and never reads `error.message`/`.error` (`errors-guard`'s exact-count sweep has no allowlist entry for a package outside this repository); user-facing text goes through `api.userMessage`. `refreshSources` and `openUnderstanding` are both exact-owner narrow actions, called at most once after the plugin's own action completes with the rejection caught; remote JavaScript, runtime scanning/registration, a global store, and raw `fetch` remain forbidden. **Plugin dialogs join the core root-dialog arbitration through one generic `"extension"` slot** — core's `RootModalSlot` union names no plugin, because that would mean patching the public tree per install. The claim is recorded by the shell as a **contribution id** (not a plugin id: one plugin may register several contributions, and a plugin-level key would let them all open at once), and the host projects `context.dialog` (`{ open, topmost, zIndex }`) only onto the holding contribution; everyone else gets a closed view. A plugin therefore keeps no dialog `open` state: `actions.openDialog()` asks, `context.dialog.open` answers, `actions.closeDialog()` releases, and the coordinator closes it on a conflicting primary, a workspace transition, or actor replacement. `openDialog` publishes the holder only after `rootModals.open` grants the lease, the holder is released only through the coordinator's close sink, and `closeDialog` is refused for anything that is not the **live** holder (checked against a ref, not the render closure) so a stale callback cannot close another contribution's dialog. `ExtensionModal` renders nothing until `dialog.open`, applies `inert`/`aria-hidden` whenever `dialog.topmost` is false, and carries the coordinator's `zIndex` as its one permitted inline style; focus return stays with the coordinator. `source.detail_section` contributions cannot open dialogs — the host refuses this structurally, not by convention (codex #578 R4 P2): that slot's own host is itself a `FloatingModalCard` (so `position: fixed` would resolve against it) and holds the conflicting `source-detail` primary lease, so `actions.openDialog()`/`closeDialog()` in that slot are refusal functions that never reach the shell (a dev-mode `console.warn` fires) and `context.dialog` is unconditionally the closed view; `ExtensionModal` itself throws a `TypeError` when rendered in that slot. The new read-only admin page `/admin/extensions` is the frontend parity surface for `GET /admin/extensions`, listing the extension topology actually loaded for operational visibility.

Source-detail opens participate in the same frozen primary issue watermark as every other asynchronous root opener. The source catalog review is the only primary slot allowed to remain above its source-detail parent; every covered dialog must be `inert` and `aria-hidden`, including KG analysis and source detail while a compatible overlay is topmost. Focus return is a post-commit layout effect: verify the expected underlying lease is still topmost and no inert ancestor remains before focusing, without a timer.

**No half-features.** In this product, every user-facing backend capability MUST ship with a corresponding frontend UI in the same change. It is not allowed to implement only one side.

- If you add a backend endpoint or data type that produces something a user should see or act on (a new knowledge type, list, action, status, field, analytics view, etc.), you MUST also add the frontend surface to view/use it — and vice versa (no frontend control that calls a missing endpoint).
- A feature is "done" only when: backend endpoint exists, `frontend/app/page.tsx` (or its components) exposes it, `scripts/check.sh` is green, and `cd frontend && npm run build` passes.
- Concretely, do NOT leave any object type "approvable but not browsable", any endpoint with no UI entry point, or any UI button wired to a non-existent route.
- Purely internal/infrastructure endpoints (health checks, migrations, observability logs) are exempt — they are not user-facing. When in doubt, treat it as user-facing and build the UI.

## Product Name

Use `silicon-notebook` as both the product name and project name.

Do not introduce a separate Chinese product name unless the user explicitly changes this decision.

## Product Flow

The app uses a two-level notebook structure:

1. Outer page: notebook collection/library.
2. Inner page: individual notebook workspace.

The notebook collection page should keep the current NotebookLM-inspired structure while staying visually distinct:

- Tabs such as `全部`, `我的笔记本`, `精选笔记本`.
- Search, view switch, sort, and `＋ 新建` controls.
- Grid, compact, and table-like list preview modes.
- The first card is `新建笔记本` when not searching.
- Notebook card upper-right menus must open real edit/delete actions.
- Collection/notebook search must include notebook metadata, source metadata, parsed source elements, and knowledge objects.

The workspace has two interface modes, persisted per-user (`user_profiles.ui_mode`, `auto` default / `advanced`) and toggled from the avatar dropdown's `高级模式` switch. Advanced mode is the full interface described throughout this document. Auto mode is a deliberately reduced surface: it hides the engine sub-switch, the retrieval-effort picker, the Deep Report research-depth picker, and the source/reference-library scope checkboxes, and every control it hides forces its default value (unnarrowed scope, standard retrieval effort, standard report depth) on the request side rather than silently reusing whatever an earlier advanced-mode session left narrowed.

Group management is a collection-level independent page opened from the avatar menu. Its left rail selects a group and its workspace exposes Knowledge libraries, Members, Share requests, and Settings. A group has one transferable owner: the creator starts as owner/admin, transfer requires an existing member and keeps the former owner as admin, and the owner cannot be demoted, removed, or leave before transfer. Every member may inspect only the notebooks their current group role actually grants them; a `group_admins`-only notebook must not be disclosed to a plain member. Owner/admin users choose which notebooks they manage should be visible to the group. They may also issue, reopen, rotate, and revoke one reusable bearer invitation link; an authenticated visitor joins atomically as a plain member, repeated visits preserve any existing role, and invalid/revoked/rotated/deleted-group tokens all fail as the same 404. The browser preserves the token only through login, removes it from URL history before redemption, and never presents the link as expiring automatically. The page must reuse the existing collection shell, typography, controls, spacing, color tokens, and responsive breakpoints; do not reintroduce group management as a centered modal or invent a separate visual system.

When the user creates a notebook:

- Do not ask for title, purpose, or description up front.
- Immediately create an `Untitled notebook`.
- Open the notebook.
- Show the source selection/import UI immediately.
- Later ingestion and LLM analysis should infer title, description, domain, summary, and knowledge objects from imported sources.

Inside a notebook:

- Model evidence markers use one syntax contract across Ask and Deep Report: ASCII `[k1]` and localized `【k1】`, including grouped markers with ASCII or Chinese commas, resolve to the same clickable numbered citations. Ordinary grouped-marker binding remains all-or-nothing when any key is unknown. Section-by-section Ask keeps its existing isolation rule: before binding, it removes keys outside that section's server-assigned range while preserving valid keys from the current section.

- Hide the global collection top bar.
- Keep visible `silicon-notebook` differences with a more engineering-console feel rather than a direct NotebookLM copy.
- Reset scroll to the top when switching into or back from the notebook workspace.
- The upper-left notebook title should be editable in place and save through the notebook update API.
- Keep the notebook header compact: do not render the notebook description under the title; show that description in the Ask welcome state when no conversation is active. Top toolbar actions must keep their labels intact across desktop widths.
- Centered floating dialogs are draggable by their header via the shared `useFloatingWindow` hook — inline `page.tsx` modals go through the `FloatingModalCard` wrapper, which owns only the card (drag offset + card ref) and hands the header its `dragHandleProps`. Dragging is disabled at the narrow-screen breakpoint (`isFloatingDisabledWidth`), where modals keep their fixed layout, and a press on a header control (close ×, buttons) never starts a drag. New centered modals adopt this rather than reinventing drag; side-anchored drawers, anchored popovers, and full-screen views stay put. The model-service status modal additionally re-polls its live 并发/排队/最久等待 every ~2s while open (an in-memory scheduler read, no model calls); its probe-driven 延迟/上次检查 stay event-driven.
- Left column: user-imported source files only. User-facing source counts exclude hidden `source_type` `memory` / `knowhow` projection sources; physical `size.sources`, copy/storage thresholds, and scheduler accounting keep physical-row semantics. `has_unindexed_content` must preserve a derived-content scale-index update decision when the visible imported-source delta is zero.
  - Show how many sources are in the current notebook.
  - Every visible imported-source row has a retrieval checkbox shared by Ask intent preview/execution and new Deep Reports. Default to all visible sources selected; `include` / `exclude` source scopes may name only visible sources owned by the active notebook, while mounted base libraries remain participants. Freeze exclusions to an explicit include-list at the API boundary and push a genuinely narrowed ceiling below bounded candidate limits. Compute `narrowed` on the server from the validated selection and current visible-source count, overwriting any client value: a frozen all-selected include list, including a one-source notebook, is the whole-scope path and must not clear history or disable graph channels. That path snapshots the current hidden Memory/Knowhow participant ids without exposing or persisting them in the public scope object; a genuinely narrowed scope excludes them. The hidden half is scoped by kind: **Knowhow projections are notebook-wide** (every member reads the table itself), while a **Memory projection is private to its creator** and only that user's ceiling may admit it — the `memory_items.created_by` predicate belongs in the SQL of that one read, never on its result, so another member's Memory source ids never enter the process and a shared notebook's default full-select request cannot reach another member's private Memory through candidate retrieval or through the whole-graph/PPR channels a non-narrowed run re-enables. That hidden read is deliberately its own bounded query (`hidden_source_ids(notebook_id, owner_id)`), fired only when the dimension was not narrowed: the compact `include` freeze reads just the requested ids plus a count, and folding the two together would put every source row back on that hot path. An orphaned Memory source fails the `EXISTS` and drops out (fail closed). If either the visible-source or hidden-participant universe later differs from the all-selected snapshot, disable unsafe whole-graph/PPR/relation/exact-section/whole-corpus-profile channels before I/O rather than letting concurrent additions become hidden premises — and take that live re-read with the identity the freeze used (carried on the frozen scope, never re-derived in the worker), because comparing an owner-scoped snapshot against an unfiltered or differently-scoped read reports drift on every request in a shared notebook and disables those channels permanently, without turning any behavior assertion red. The checkbox selection is the sole active-notebook retrieval scope; the model never narrows or widens it. Source-bounded base KG seeds may still map directly to base chunks without combined-graph traversal. When the effective local scope is empty and no base is mounted, disable Ask input and new-report creation in the UI and enforce the same rejection in the API. Persist and revalidate a report's resolved scope through intent confirmation, planning, and generation; omitted scope remains the backward-compatible whole-scope path.
  - Keep source cards compact and readable for long mixed Chinese/English titles and summaries.
  - Source cards should open a source detail preview with element-level parsed text and expose a delete action. The interactive detail reader must fetch and render bounded element pages (40 by default, 100 maximum per request), support loading earlier/later pages, and resolve a requested citation element to the page that contains it; the legacy full-element reader remains for internal/backward-compatible callers, not the browser detail path.
  - The element -> chunk reverse index (`chunk_elements`, SQLite v46 / PostgreSQL v24) forks the per-query evidence lookup on the persisted `unified_kg_state.chunk_elements_indexed` marker: a backfilled notebook does a bounded point lookup on the `(notebook_id, element_id, chunk_id)` primary key, an unbackfilled one keeps the legacy version-cached whole-notebook chunk scan byte-for-byte. Every chunk write path that a live notebook can reach — `replace_source_chunks`, the knowhow projector's `insert_rows`/`delete_by_ids`, and the knowhow single-table transfer that copies chunk rows straight into an EXISTING notebook — maintains the reverse rows inside the same write transaction as the chunk rows, and the `chunks` foreign-key cascade removes them on source delete/reparse/cell rewrite. One registered exemption: whole-notebook deep copy does not copy `unified_kg_state`, so a copy's marker is always absent and its read path is the legacy scan; adding `unified_kg_state` to the deep-copy table set would therefore require copying `chunk_elements` with it. Historical rows are projected only by the explicit offline `scripts/batch_ingest.py backfill-chunk-elements` phase — never from a request — with one `chunk_element_backfills` row per notebook: bounded keyset page and cursor commit atomically, restart resumes the last committed page, a completed current marker skips, and `kg_mutation_seq` drift records only the stable `kg_generation_changed` code before the next run resets from the new generation. The point lookup orders by SQLite `rowid` / PostgreSQL `ordinal` (chunk insertion order) so the order-sensitive BFS fallback stays deterministic; that order was never a contract, the determinism is. The whole-notebook PPR membership map (`_ent_chunk_map`) deliberately keeps the legacy path — it is inherently whole-notebook.
  - Source delete/reparse must never rebuild a whole notebook's reverse index inside the interactive request. A notebook whose reverse index has been backfilled resolves affected KG objects through `knowledge_object_sources`; an unbackfilled notebook uses keyset-paged database-native evidence filters for the current source and leaves the index marker untouched. That legacy fallback may still scan the notebook's KG rows, so large imported libraries should run `scripts/batch_ingest.py backfill-source-index` as the explicit offline prebuild/repair path. The offline rebuild persists one `source_index_backfills` row per notebook: cursor and index rows commit in the same bounded transaction, restart resumes the last committed cursor without clearing prior pages, a completed current marker skips rebuilding, and `kg_mutation_seq` drift records only a stable failure code before the next run resets from the new generation. Take the source aggregate row lock before projection cleanup so concurrent extraction cannot republish children after the cleanup cursor. Cap affected-object delete statements at 500 ids (this is a parameter/statement bound, not a constant-time or background-delete promise), fetch referenced image assets in one database round trip before deleting them, and make the frontend show a source-specific deleting state that disables duplicate deletion; notebook-scoped deletion tombstones must also prevent stale navigation/list responses from resurrecting a deleted row.
  - The add-source dialog must keep staged rows stable: compact overlong filenames in the middle while preserving the ending/extension and expose the full name as a tooltip. Drag-drop and the file picker share one staging path: drops are handled explicitly (never left to the native file input, whose `accept` attribute silently filters unsupported files), and every skipped file — unsupported type, legacy Office format, over the size cap, or over the batch cap — is listed persistently inside the dialog with its filename and a per-file reason; a transient toast alone is not acceptable feedback. Its confirmation button must include the staged batch in the effective document-limit check, become visibly disabled when the batch cannot fit, and show an actionable reason; keep comfortable space below the last action row. Per-file source-upload size is deployment-owned by `SOURCE_UPLOAD_MAX_MB` (integer 1–1024, default 50; 1 MB = 1024 × 1024 bytes): Settings computes the byte value, the authenticated lightweight system-config response carries that parsed byte cap to the browser for immediate picker rejection, and the upload route retains the authoritative streaming 413 check with the active cap in its message. Next.js 15's external rewrite clones only 10 MiB by default, so `next.config.mjs` must derive `experimental.middlewareClientMaxBodySize` as a whole-multipart transport envelope from that same per-file setting, the fixed 20-file batch rail, and only the bounded multipart overhead — never introduce a second user-visible cap. Source/dev builds read the root env exported by the launcher; the offline standalone bundle builds this transport envelope at the protocol maximum because the target's actual `.env` is runtime-owned. Both sides also enforce a fixed 20-file maximum per multipart request; the backend applies that limit during multipart parsing so the per-file allowance cannot accumulate into an unbounded spool. Multipart metadata is limited to the two expected per-file fields, at most 40 scalar parts of at most 4 KiB each; unknown or excess metadata is rejected.
  - Source detail element text should wrap within the modal width, including long Markdown paths, LaTeX fragments, and mixed Chinese/English text; keep horizontal scrolling local to tables/formulas rather than the entire detail panel. Direct KaTeX inputs must strip full-value Markdown math delimiters (`$...$`, `$$...$$`, `\\(...\\)`, `\\[...\\]`) first, and malformed input must fall back to visible raw text rather than an empty/error-only visualization.
  - On desktop, source detail uses a conventional close control and supports dragging by the header; keep narrow-screen geometry fixed and the detail body independently scrollable.
  - Do not enable web/network source search yet; keep it as a disabled future affordance only.
- Main column: source-grounded knowhow tools, exposed as four tabs: **问答** (Ask), **知识库** (Knowledge), **记忆** (Memory), and **深度报告** (Deep Report).
  - Ask citation images are body content, not an answer-end gallery: insert each persisted image after the smallest complete Markdown block containing its bound citation (paragraph/heading sibling; inside the relevant list item or blockquote; after the whole table), merge composite citations in body order, and show a repeated asset only at its first cited position. Ask inline/popover and public-conversation surfaces display the image plus the `本段附图 [n] / 模型未直接读取图片` trust label only; caption/description stays searchable and supplies accessible `alt`, but is not repeated visibly. If the cited element is itself the attached image, suppress its parser-generated snippet/quoted-span; a text evidence row with a nearby image keeps its real excerpt. Clicking opens the coordinated in-page preview (button/backdrop/Escape/focus return), never source navigation, and a citation popover must stay mounted while that preview owns focus. `PublicImage.reference_keys` may repeat only already-public citation keys and must never expose asset/element ids; legacy public payloads without position keys use an explicitly unpositioned image-only fallback rather than dropping images or guessing a binding. MinerU and Markdown use the same rule once bytes plus `asset_id` exist; missing bytes remain text-only. Standalone Markdown staging must persistently warn about local/relative targets that need ZIP/folder intake and remote targets the server will not fetch, without blocking text upload; scan batch files sequentially and admit a late warning only while that exact name+size file remains staged. Deep Report body-inline placement is phase two; its existing reference detail remains unchanged.
  - Ask-born Memory keeps its persisted citation provenance visible as source title/original file, location, and quoted span; live citations in the same notebook participant scope can reopen the exact source element, while transferred citations remain explicitly archival.
  - Ask provides KG-grounded Q&A with per-sentence `[k_i]` citations and multi-turn conversation. A citation/anchor whose bound evidence includes a captioned image (captions are the sole entry point into retrieval — an uncaptioned image is never chunked and gets no "nearby image" fallback) may additionally carry a bounded `images` list (`CitationImage`, shared `evidence_context.py` enrichment, zero extra model/embedding calls); the field lives on both `AnswerAnchor` (reasoning's authoritative `[k]` display path) and the `Citation` fallback list, is rendered in a panel visually distinct from the cited text since the model never viewed the image, and is deliberately absent from the public report-share allowlist. Deep Report references share the same enrichment through one fail-open batch call per finished report. Knowledge browses/governs extracted objects by type. Deep Report exposes the current two-stage report workflow, outline review, progress, export, cancellation, deletion, and in-place retry for failed generation that still has a confirmed outline. Retry never reruns intent understanding or planning. Completed-report list and detail metadata use `updated_at` for the exact browser-local generation timestamp, retain its relative age, and show the wall-clock duration from the atomically persisted `generation_started_at` (the successful `outline_ready`/`failed` → `generating` claim) through that terminal write. Intent/outline confirmation waits are excluded; legacy completed reports without a generation-start stamp show no invented duration. Non-completed reports show only their creation time and never claim a final duration. The earlier Scenario query / Case search / Checklist tools are retired.
  - Deep Report planning is intent-first and confirmation-gated. Creating a report first performs corpus-blind question understanding and persists an `understanding` contract (original objective, editable resolved question, mandatory topics, entities/comparison axes/constraints/exclusions/output, assumptions, confidence, and bounded blocking ambiguities), then pauses at `status=intent_ready` for the report **creator's** confirmation unless the create request has `auto_generate=true` and the question is clear (`needs_clarification=false`), in which case the server auto-confirms through the same deterministic freeze the manual endpoint uses (`backend/app/services/reports/intent_confirmation.py`, no second LLM call) and proceeds directly into planning; a question with blocking ambiguities always pauses regardless of `auto_generate`, and required ambiguities must be answered before a human or the server can confirm. Reports are per-creator (P1 group sharing): any user with read access — owner, read-only member, or group-granted reader — creates, confirms, and manages their **own** reports behind row-level `created_by` checks (see the report-endpoint entry in the Architecture Baseline), so a read-only member confirms their own report rather than waiting for the owner, and the owner neither sees nor confirms anyone else's. Only `POST /notebooks/{id}/reports/{report_id}/intent` (or, under `auto_generate`, the equivalent server-side auto-confirmation sharing that same code path) may confirm the resolved question and resume corpus access: it must atomically claim `intent_ready → planning`, deterministically preserve every already-reviewed contract field, and add the confirmed wording/answers without a hidden second LLM reinterpretation. That frozen input is authoritative for all later retrieval, outline, and generation; `auto_generate` also auto-accepts the default outline and proceeds directly to generation once intent is confirmed, provided no blocking ambiguity intervened. A scoped report (either persisted scope dimension) is auto-confirmed only after the same scope revalidation the manual endpoint runs — injected into the engine as a callback from the api layer, since the validators live there; a failed, raising, or absent recheck leaves the report at the manual gate with reason `scope_invalid`, so sources deleted or libraries unmounted during intent understanding never carry a stale scope into planning; a passing recheck's refreshed freeze is ADOPTED — the claimed understanding and the planning/generation scope context both use it, so a re-freeze that flips `narrowed` (sources or libraries added meanwhile) matches what manual confirmation would persist. A failure or race while auto-advancing intent or outline fails open — the report is left at its prior status with the manual confirmation gate still usable — and emits only the body-free `report_intent_auto_confirm_skipped` event (kind/notebook_id/report_id/a stable reason, no question or answer text). Confirmation answers may enrich the internal retrieval/drafting question but must never leak into the visible report title. After confirmation, probe KG and direct `SourceElement` coverage per mandatory topic, then let corpus evidence refine vocabulary/order without replacing or narrowing a required topic. Every mandatory topic keeps at least one section binding through outline review (the UI locks its last binding and the PATCH API rejects bypasses), and every approved retrieval direction must execute even if the reasoning planner replans. Outline cards expose mandatory questions, editable retrieval directions, and KG/raw-element coverage. Direct `SourceElement` hits are first-class `[k]` evidence in both reasoning Ask and reports; selecting a report citation reveals its bound source/location excerpt. Small libraries may score their element rows directly; a non-copyable large library must never full-scan element text/vectors, but must recover a bounded exact-element candidate set from already bounded chunk ANN/FTS hits and hydrate only their `element_ids`. Citation display in Ask and reports prefers the grounded `source_paper_meta.paper_title` only when `is_paper=true` and the parsed title is nonblank, otherwise it retains the ordinary source title/file name. Citation payloads also preserve `sources.file_name` separately as `source_file_name`; Ask/report citation cards show it as `原始文件` when it differs from the display title, including for mounted public-library evidence. It must come from the persisted upload row, never a MinerU temporary/output Markdown name. The title rule has one implementation, `app/domain/source_display.py::source_display_title` (`app/services/source_display.py` keeps a re-export for existing importers), which every path naming a source for the user imports — citation titles, retrieved element cards, and the typed-collection list all label the same sources in the same session, so a second copy is visible to the user as one paper carrying two names; `backend/tests/test_source_display_title.py` fails on a second implementation. A report section is `grounded` only after its emitted anchors bind to sufficiently relevant evidence; never trust the model's boolean alone or collapse two concrete chunks/elements merely because they share a source. The final editor may summarize and flag missing intent/contradictions, but must not rewrite sections or add facts.
  - Deep Report credibility is a report-wide contract: reuse shared intent and persist `result_scope` / `completeness_required`; ranked retrieval must disclose any unfulfilled complete, aggregate, or hybrid scope; assumptions are never retrieval text or evidence. Planning, body, and UI share one bounded corpus profile and conservative distinguishable-document grouping. Identity-unknown sources remain separate in the bibliography and never increase independent-source counts; Top-1 concentration assigns all unknown support to the largest verified family as a conservative upper bound. At depth 8 or above, finish every section's retrieval before at most one global synthesis blueprint, then draft sections; optional frame, blueprint, and claim-ledger data fail open. High-risk citation audit measures anchor coverage rather than semantic entailment, trend confidence is capped by distinguishable cited sources, and the final editor receives syntactically valid bounded context and audits without rewriting. Numeric rails live only in `docs/product-and-api*.md`.
  - Ask federation is mode-specific. Baseline `chunk` retrieval is active-notebook-only; optional KG overlay/PPR can add federated KG context and base-backed chunks. `graph` and `reasoning` use federated KG paths. Citations retain their tier, and answer synthesis defers to base on contradiction. The `mode` field selects `chunk` (default), `reasoning`, or the opt-in/experimental `graph`; retired `fast`/`global` ids map to `chunk` only for persisted-session compatibility.
  - Retrieval scope has TWO independent dimensions, both user-owned, both defaulting to fully selected: a checkbox per visible imported source (`SourceScope`) and a checkbox per WHOLE mounted reference library (`BaseNotebookScope`: `include`/`exclude` plus `notebook_ids`, never expanded into the sources inside a library). `base_scope` ids may name only libraries currently mounted on the active notebook (422 otherwise); the API boundary freezes every submission — `exclude` + [] included — into an explicit include snapshot and recomputes `narrowed` server-side, which is what stops a library mounted after a report was created from silently joining it at confirm/generate. The dimensions are ORTHOGONAL and must never be folded: `source_scope_restricted()` gates the ACTIVE notebook's own channels (PPR, private Memory, community reports, weak-support relations, exact-section lookup, the report corpus profile) and unchecking a borrowed library must not switch any of them off. Cross-library channels each consult the library dimension for themselves — typed collection enumeration and the collection map, federated candidate retrieval, community expansion, graph traversal, `follow_chain`, evidence assembly, and the KG availability gate (a JUDGEMENT, not a result filter: with no local graph and the only graph-bearing library unchecked it must answer "no graph", or `kg_required` never flips; it reads the frozen selection through `scoped_participants`, not whether that selection narrowed anything). Conversation history is the only gate that consults both, because a prior answer can quote a library the user just unchecked. Library-level narrowing is applied at ONE boundary — the resolved participant list, filtered once and read by the plan, the walk, the denominator and the closing fingerprint — because enumeration requires rows and counts to come from a single predicate; `enumeration_active()` is unaffected (the tool stays available, its scope shrinks). The leak surface includes the query terms themselves, so community expansion narrows where it READS sibling entity names, not where hits come back. `resolve_participants` / `mount_sql.py` stay untouched: that predicate is shared with permission checks, and a per-request retrieval checkbox may not narrow an authorization set. Two second-order costs are registered and accepted: graph-walk and PPR filtering happens after traversal/truncation (the federated graph is memoised process-wide under a scope-blind key), and the whole-graph size guard stays scope-blind. The 409 "empty scope" judgement is the conjunction of both dimensions — which libraries this request CHECKED, not which are mounted — at all three Ask entry points and at report create/confirm/generate; a dimension the request did not narrow answers from the notebook's real evidence universe, and the local half of that universe is `NotebookSummary.local_evidence_available` (chunks incl. Knowhow cells, local usable KG, that user's confirmed Memory — computed by the catalog at zero extra queries, defaulting to False and ORed with the visible-source count so it can only widen), never the visible-source count alone. `AskResponse.retrieval_scope` is a display-only receipt (`local {selected,total}` + per-library `{notebook_id,name,included}`); library names are an authorization-time snapshot, retrieval never reads it back, and it is absent when neither dimension narrowed. The browser's strict-reasoning gate is per-SELECTION too: `NotebookSummary.base_kg_notebook_ids` decomposes `base_kg_available` into which mounted libraries actually have a graph (zero extra queries — `mounted_bases_row` already returns `has_kg` per row and the aggregate was just `any(...)` over it; same fill paths as the boolean, and the two must stay self-consistent, non-empty ⟺ true, being two projections of one read). 深入分析 / 知识图谱 are admitted on "local graph OR a CHECKED library has one", and the `将借用参考库「…」推理` hint names only checked graph-bearing libraries — reading the aggregate admits a mode that cannot reach a graph this round and names a library that is not participating. The field belongs on `NotebookSummary`, never on `NotebookRef` (that model also serves `/notebooks/{id}/mountable` and backs `MountedBase`, neither of which computes the flag).
  - Retrieval source scope is decided by the user alone; the model never infers which sources a question names. The visible-source checkboxes (`SourceScope`: `include`/`exclude` plus `source_ids`) are the hard active-notebook ceiling, pushed through the request-scoped `source_scope_context` into bounded candidate generation and rechecked at evidence trimming, synthesis, anchors, and citations. The server computes `narrowed` from the validated current visible universe: an all-selected snapshot (including a one-source notebook) keeps normal graph channels, privately snapshots current hidden Memory/Knowhow participants, and freezes source-partitioned candidates/results; a genuinely narrowed scope excludes hidden projections. Visible or hidden participant drift after validation disables unsafe graph channels before I/O. Narrowed chunk ANN applies a compact row-aligned source-code sidecar in the HNSW filter before Top-K and rechecks after hydration; older indexes without it use bounded source-filtered FTS until rebuild/fold. After KG, PPR, and exact deterministic seeds, a completely empty evidence state performs one bounded raw-element search before the first reflect decision. Channels that cannot prove safe per-source isolation are skipped with a visible reason rather than run library-wide and filtered afterwards. The corpus-blind intent planner must not emit source identities: the former `source_refs` / `QueryIntentSourceScope` / `search_evidence(source_refs)` contract is removed. Exact-equality resolution could not honor an abbreviation such as "pdagent" for a source titled "PDAGENT-BENCH: …", so an ordinary question failed closed with a deterministic 422; with user-owned checkboxes already available, a second model guess only adds failure modes.
  - KG extraction publishes immutable source-local facts and normalized evidence-element bindings in the same transaction as global objects. Facts are keyed to the source extraction generation, do not depend on the lifetime of the fused global object, fail closed on stale generation or cross-source evidence, and follow source delete/reparse/notebook-copy lifecycles. A deep copy remaps facts, bindings, and terminal backfill ledgers through one source-generation map and creates a copy-local completed KG run; copied audit/repair must never depend on the original notebook's operational extraction history. The internal `SourceSubgraphSnapshot` is generation-keyed and backend-neutral: every adapter applies the selected-source predicate, relation endpoint authorization, and status checks before `LIMIT`, while SQLite object/chunk/cluster reads are source-first rather than notebook-first. Cache identity uses O(1) KG/cluster mutation sequences plus bounded source/run/backfill state; never add COUNT/MAX scans to the cache-hit path. Live completeness is checked inside the bounded fact window. Object/chunk/fact/evidence/cluster overflow disables only dependent capabilities, and missing source-index state, generation drift, or an unsupported fact projection version fails closed before dependent graph use. Snapshot payload/evidence trees are recursively immutable. Source-bounded `SourceGraphPrimitives` revalidate source ownership, endpoints, evidence, review status, and per-channel capability for neighbors/expansion, two-hop chains, relation lexical/endpoint recall, exact-section hydration, and cursor enumeration. No primitive may substitute a whole-notebook graph/map or admit an excluded source as an intermediate node; exact and enumeration operate only over the already bounded snapshot. An incomplete live or backfilled projection may label nodes only from its source-local fact rows and must disable exhaustive fact/membership consumers. Scoped PPR is independently rollback-gated by `SOURCE_SUBGRAPH_PPR_ENABLED`; its invisible shadow consumer is enabled by default and can be disabled independently. It must build a sparse transition from the snapshot itself, never slice a whole-notebook CSR and filter the ranking afterwards. Filter node/chunk ownership, relation source/endpoints/evidence/review state, object↔chunk memberships, and cluster members before degree normalization or any limit; reset accepts only object/chunk ids present in that filtered graph, and min-max, Top-K, and hydration consider only its selected-source chunks. Cache by frozen scope and KG/cluster/source generations with a bounded LRU; cold transition construction is single-flight so concurrent requests cannot multiply peak CSR memory. Node/edge overflow and build/run failure return an explicit local capability reason and no PPR hits. PPR only ranks `G`; it never rewrites baseline relevance or order. In a large notebook, the source-partitioned scale companion keeps selected-source graph work independent of whole-library size: full build/fold reads one visible source at a time through source-first LIMIT+1 rails, publishes one hash-addressable CSR partition bound to the main scale manifest identity, and invalidates a dedicated single-flight LRU. Every partition file is digest-verified. Runtime reads all selected small manifests first and rejects cumulative node/nnz/cross-edge overflow before opening any payload; it reuses a single partition's CSR without rebuilding it, and combines several partitions only through sparse arrays with one bounded cross-edge allocation. Result selection must use bounded partial Top-K rather than sorting every source chunk. Cross-partition relations require selected ownership, source-local evidence, both endpoints in the selected object union, and the central edge registry. Legacy, missing, corrupt, over-limit, or identity-mismatched companions return capability unavailable and must never fall back to a whole-graph traversal. Publication and runtime consumption retain separate default-on rollback switches; Ask/Report consume G only through the shared activation service. Selected-source graph enrichment is a separate protected lane: freeze the historical `B_final` first, never pass it through a shared reserve/token selector, and spend only an independent enrichment token budget and reasoning action/step ledger on `G`. A duplicate chunk may merge producer provenance only in an isolated copy; it must preserve baseline text, source/citation handles, score, relevance, and position. `baseline_evicted_count != 0`, graph failure, or timeout discards all of `G` and retains the identical baseline manifest. With enrichment disabled or its budget zero, do not call the provider or change the historical call/prompt/result sequence. Ask/Report consume this lane only through the shared quality-gated activation service; off and shadow preserve the historical retrieval scores, channels, evidence, prompts, and answers.
  - User-visible selected-source graph activation requires a verified paired quality attestation. `app/services/source_graph_quality.py` is the production contract; production services must never import `app.eval`. The golden suite binds source aliases to actual source ids and covers one source, a few sources, a large single source, mounted base evidence, and same-name objects across sources. Baseline and shadow observations must share the exact model/sampling, corpus signature, frozen local/base scope, and source-alias map. Unauthorized evidence/citations, changed baseline manifest/candidates/citation keys, any baseline eviction, missing required graph actions, or insufficient scenario scale are hard failures. Quality and cost comparisons apply per case as well as in aggregate so one improvement cannot mask another regression. The emitted attestation contains no questions, answer text, evidence ids, citations, or source ids. Its digest detects accidental mutation but is not a signature: deployment must read it only from a trusted artifact path and pin the expected corpus/model before allowlist, stable-hash, or on activation. Shadow mode needs no attestation because it cannot alter user output. Ask/Report now consume G only through the shared host's built-in contributor and core activation bridge; off and shadow always return B.
  - Indexed KG retrieval must keep every post-ANN database hydration step bounded by the current candidate window. Isolated-node ranking probes return only candidate ids through indexed per-candidate `EXISTS` checks; they must never materialize complete adjacency rows. Canonical folding uses `cluster_fold_rows` for the scored ids rather than the full notebook cluster map, and lazy ANN handle opening is single-flight per scale-index instance and artifact kind. These are result-preserving constraints: do not change scores, thresholds, PPR, or recall to recover latency.
  - The twelve built-in KG edge ids and their directed endpoint pairs have one authoritative registry in `app/domain/kg/edge_schema.py` (`app/services/kg/edge_schema.py` is a re-export shim; new `EdgeSpec` entries must not be declared there); extraction prompts, output validation, edge trust, graph/PPR/canonical hydration, relation retrieval, query-time composition, and persisted-artifact versioning must derive from it rather than repeat local matrices. Producers are fail-closed for the four core types. Query consumers reject illegal core-to-core pairs and unknown edge ids while retaining known edges that touch administrator-defined extension node types. Historical invalid rows remain stored for audit but are excluded from graph consumers. `scripts/audit_kg_edge_contract.py` is read-only and reports grouped counts plus ids only, never evidence text.
  - Optional post-extraction relation completion is bounded and rollout-gated. `KG_RELATION_COMPLETION_MODE=off` is the default; `shadow` runs proposal/verification without writes and `write` inserts only pending relations. Both active modes additionally require an explicit notebook allowlist or stable hash rollout. Completion pages a source through an indexed `(source_id,id)` keyset and a mode-specific persistent source-generation watermark; each job hydrates only its bounded object/evidence-id window, uses indexed per-candidate relation `EXISTS` plus bounded same-source FTS/ANN overfetch, section quotas, pair/batch/character rails, server-issued ids, and an independent verifier. Unfinished states re-enqueue and startup recovers current pending generations; if effective mode changes, one generation-CAS transaction publishes the newly active mode's recoverable cursor before marking the old pending cursor `stale`. It never full-scans a notebook/book or falls back to brute force when indexes are unavailable. The final short write transaction rechecks source/run generation and object/element ownership, persists the exact server excerpt seen by the verifier, and uses stable relation ids for retry idempotence; reparse/delete races write zero stale rows, while invalid zero rails fail closed without advancing.
  - Retrieval provenance is producer-level and accumulative: semantic, lexical, PPR, KG-source, and community producers append typed support records when candidates merge. Never infer origin from a final score. Chunk/graph mixed selection may reserve `CHUNK_GRAPH_RESERVE` graph-only candidates that already clear the existing relevance floor, but it must not enlarge token/item budgets or change the oversized-first-chunk exception. Additional reserves go through the same `select_with_reserves` rule list rather than a parallel selector: reserves share one budget, a reserve set to `0` must stay completely inert (so the shipped `CHUNK_GRAPH_RESERVE=0` default keeps behaving exactly as before), and no reserve may evict a chunk another reserve is holding.
  - Questions naming an exactly lookup-able identifier additionally run the zero-model exact-lookup channel (`EXACT_LOOKUP_ENABLED`, on by default): exact substring match locates the section, `chunks_by_section` fetches that node plus its subtree, and `EXACT_SECTION_RESERVE` protects those chunks during mix selection. The gate is `exact_probe_terms`, a deliberately NARROWER view of `identifier_terms`: `_`/`.`-joined names always qualify, hyphen-only forms must carry a digit (`GPT-4`, `v1-2` in; `state-of-the-art`, `real-time`, `end-to-end`, `high-level` out). Recall and probing are different contracts — one extra OR-ed lexical term is free, one extra probe is not — so the wide definition must stay wide on the recall side; the narrow one exists because those hyphenated words occur in a large fraction of analytical questions (a deep report's per-section question reliably contains one, i.e. one wasted probe per section, measured at 16 ms / 50 hits on a 20k-chunk library) and, when one matched a chapter heading, put that entire 12-chunk chapter into the evidence set at relevance 1.0. That gate is the cost contract — a question with no probe-worthy name must issue zero additional queries — and every probe stays bounded by the `EXACT_LOOKUP_*` settings. Hits fold onto their nearest identifier-named breadcrumb prefix BEFORE section slots are allocated and are ranked per group: ranking raw breadcrumbs let one command's sub-sections take every slot (`set_db > Arguments` 4 hits / `> Examples` 3 / `set_db` 2 / `report_timing` 2 with three slots dropped `report_timing` entirely), and "a command's sub-sections out-number its main section" is the normal shape of a reference manual. A group whose key is identifier-named is fetched as a subtree; anything else is hydrated **by matched chunk id**, never re-queried by `(source, section_path)` under a LIMIT — heading breadcrumbs are a Markdown-parsing property, so MinerU-parsed PDF/DOCX sources leave dozens of bare `Arguments` headings sharing one section key, and the path re-query returned the first 12 of them while discarding the hit the probe had actually found. Both adapters must escape `%`/`_`/`\` in LIKE patterns (command names contain `_`, LIKE's single-character wildcard, so an unescaped needle silently loses precision rather than failing), and the exact probe must never decompose its needle through `lexical_recall_terms`. Channel chunks score keyword-only against the probed NAMES rather than the caller's query — one producer, one meaning; scoring the caller's query made mix report 0.2857 (below `tau_high`) and reasoning 1.0 for the same evidence — and deliberately skip `RELEVANCE_FLOOR`, because the admission criterion is the exact section anchor, not fuzzy relevance; membership reaches the selector as an explicit chunk-id set rather than a new `RetrievalSupport` origin. Active notebook only — mounted base libraries are out of scope.
  - **Quoted spans are the one user-facing search syntax, and `app/core/query_syntax.py` is its single definition point.** Text inside ASCII double quotes is matched WHOLE and never tokenized. It lives in `core` because both sides of the retrieval stack need it and they cannot import each other: `app.repositories` (candidate generation) and `app.services.retrieval` (scoring) are joined through the ports module. Three deliberate boundaries: ASCII `"` only (typographic `“…”` is ordinary Chinese quotation — honouring it would silently constrain a large share of existing questions), a MIN_LEXICAL_TERM_CHARS floor (SQLite's trigram index cannot match anything shorter, so a shorter span would be a promise the backend cannot keep), and MORE than MAX_QUOTED_PHRASES DISTINCT spans in one text disables the syntax for that text entirely — distinct, because the internal research query repeats one phrase across the objective, the normalized question and every mandatory topic, and counting occurrences silently switched the syntax off exactly where it matters most. That last one is a shape test, not a budget: knowhow completion's "query" is a JSON envelope where every key and cell value is a quoted span, so honouring a PREFIX of a dozen spans would both invent constraints the user never stated and strip the CJK cell values of their tri-gram recall path. A span the parser REJECTS must stay in the remainder — masking a span the caller then discards deletes those words from the query entirely, turning a precision request into a recall hole. Consumers: `lexical_recall_terms` puts phrases at the head of the term list (ahead of the quote-stripped whole-sentence term) and decomposes only the remainder, `probe_keyword_basis` scores a channel against the names IT probed with every name atomic — atomicity is a property of the probe, never inferred from whether the string contains a space (that demotes `config.yaml` and every CJK phrase back to tokens) — `retrieval.KeywordBasis` counts each phrase as ONE indivisible unit satisfied by whole-phrase substring presence (`honor_quotes=False` for the callers comparing two stored TEXTS, e.g. governance merge similarity — a document that quotes someone is not stating a search constraint), Memory's candidate probe (one whole-string phrase) additionally OR-s each accepted span in as an independent probe through `phrase_queries`, or a memory holding the phrase but not the surrounding sentence could never become a candidate; `exact_probe_terms` admits phrases unconditionally because a user's quotes are never incidental (the cost argument that rejects `state-of-the-art` is about INCIDENTAL names) while `honor_quotes=False` keeps the model's own `exact_term` on the narrow gate, and the four planning/reflect prompts append one line about preserving quoted spans — CONDITIONALLY, unlike `SCOPE_DEIXIS_GROUNDING`, since a quote-free question gains nothing and a deep report would pay for it once per section per step. Terms already selected by a caller reach the exact channel through `exact_probe_query`, which quotes every one of them: the channel re-derives its own terms from the string it is handed, so a bare space-join loses multi-word phrases. A query with no quoted span must produce byte-identical output at every one of those points. `frontend/app/query-syntax.ts` mirrors the parse so the composer can echo which phrases were recognised before the user submits — without it, a rejected span is a silent failure — and the two are pinned to one shared case table (`test_query_syntax.py` PARITY_CASES / `query-syntax.test.mjs`).
  - Lexical candidate generation is part of the indexed retrieval contract. SQLite FTS5 must quote every user-derived clause as data, keep an optional exact-query phrase bonus, and OR independent Latin/number terms plus overlapping CJK trigrams; never quote a multi-term query only as one mandatory contiguous phrase.
