Separator-joined identifiers (`_`/`-`/`.` runs such as `set_db` or `config.yaml`) are additionally recalled as whole quoted terms — trigram phrases give exact substring matching — bounded by an ASCII-letter requirement, a 4-char floor, the exact-phrase length cap, and a 16-term quota; when identifier terms overflow the shared term budget, a bounded CJK tail quota is reserved so the CJK half of a mixed query keeps lexical recall. Queries with no such identifier keep the historical term set bit-for-bit. PostgreSQL must decompose the same bounded term set before native trigram candidate generation, and must escape LIKE metacharacters in its `ILIKE` arm so a term such as `set_db` stays literal instead of widening into `setXdb`/`set db`; the trigram operator and `similarity()` keep the raw term, because escaping is a LIKE-only concern. The term set that is finally PROBED is additionally filtered by the notebook's corpus languages (`LEXICAL_LANGUAGE_GATE_ENABLED`, default true): when the sampled corpus (`_notebook_langs`) holds no CJK character, terms whose characters are all CJK are dropped, because every trigram of such a term still contains a CJK character and can therefore match nothing. The filter belongs to the consuming layer — the caller that knows the `notebook_id` passes `corpus_langs` into `fts_search`/`chunk_fts_search`, always for the notebook the adapter is about to query — and never rewrites `lexical_recall_terms` itself. Three asymmetries are mandatory: the CJK direction only (Latin identifiers are ordinary content inside Chinese documents, and an empty corpus already answers `["en"]`, so the mirror rule would fire on a guess); the tail only, so the user's quoted spans and the whole-sentence term are never filtered; and only all-CJK terms, never merely CJK-containing ones, which retain a shared Latin trigram. Both backends must filter identically, or one notebook's recall would depend on which adapter served it. Source-scoped lexical probes are never language-gated because a misjudged notebook-level sample could otherwise erase their fallback; every scoped call site passes `source_scoped=` explicitly. Indexed Chunk retrieval unions bounded ANN and lexical windows; when narrowed, its persisted row-to-source sidecar applies the source predicate inside HNSW before Top-K, while missing/legacy sidecars use bounded source-filtered FTS. Indexed KG retrieval retains its bounded source-key lexical path. Indexed Relation retrieval unions ANN hits with a bounded set of live relations adjacent to lexically matched KG endpoints through the source/target indexes; preserve FTS endpoint rank and reserve budget for both relation directions so a high-degree source cannot starve target-only hits. Keep candidate identity separate from semantic-score maps so lexical-only candidates remain keyword-only instead of receiving a synthetic zero semantic score.
  - Ask collection-enumeration result cards keep the coverage badge, list title, and optional single-source scope visible, but their item content is collapsed by default. The user explicitly opens a card to render its grouped elements or KG objects; after opening, retain the existing first-20 preview and separate “show all loaded items” control. Keep partial/synthesis disclosure inside the collapsible content, and do not eagerly mount image rows while the card is closed.
  - Large-library cold loading is a readiness prerequisite. With `STARTUP_PRELOAD_SCALE_INDEXES=true` (the default), startup strictly loads every live published scale index, every currently enabled KG/chunk/relation ANN handle, and the safely reusable single-index PPR transition/chunk-id core before `/api/ready` admits traffic. A required artifact failure or a live-index count above `SCALE_IDX_CACHE_MAX` keeps the service not-ready; never silently make the first user pay that artifact load. A direct single self-index graph with the delta gate off reuses the ScaleIndex node list/map/idf and prepared PPR core instead of copying tens of millions of entries; this core lives with the dedicated ScaleIndex LRU, not the evictable general VectorCache. Do not eagerly materialize every cross-notebook mounted combined graph at startup: the current composition path would multiply full node maps/CSR edges and can OOM, so those combinations remain lazy until they get a bounded/shared representation. The strict guarantee is for the published artifact set present during startup: a runtime build/fold publishes a new generation through the existing online path, and adding a new indexed notebook can exceed the configured LRU; stable deployments should size `SCALE_IDX_CACHE_MAX` before that change and restart to re-establish the readiness guarantee. `STARTUP_PRELOAD_SCALE_INDEXES=false` is only an explicit recovery escape hatch for repairing a bad index; production remains single-worker so multi-GB caches are not duplicated per worker.
  - Scale/viz performance helpers are artifact data, not request-time work. New scale and standalone viz artifacts persist the stable degree order plus the by-source edge permutation/indptr; old compact or JSON artifacts remain readable and may derive those helpers once. A bounded core graph must enumerate only the kept sources' adjacency segments and then restore original edge order, never allocate a graph-wide membership mask or scan every edge. Multi-participant PPR composition must preserve the historical ordered left-fold semantics: earlier duplicate coordinates collapse to structural presence before the last participant, while overlap with the last participant retains its pre-normalization double weight; build the final combined CSR once and keep differential PPR score/rank tests. Heavy and light maintenance categories use separate fixed worker queues. Submission captures ContextVars and returns a joinable compatibility handle, but queued maintenance work must not create one blocked thread per item. Conflict resolution has independent object and relation admission, repeats the relation sentinel read inside the worker to close the preflight race, and skips the whole pass on overflow rather than treating a prefix as complete. Exact rails remain only in `docs/product-and-api.md` / `_zh.md`.
  - Scale-index scheduling treats a manual `when=now` request as an atomic upgrade of an older idle request for the same notebook: only a newly claimed immediate build removes that queued entry, a concurrent already-running build preserves its follow-up, a later idle request remains queued, and daemon-start failure restores the displaced request. The idle scheduler must claim entries independently instead of clearing the batch: busy entries stay queued and one launch failure cannot drop or starve the rest. Persisted `AskResponse.index_required` records answer-time degradation only; the answer panel must combine it with live `ScaleIndexStatus.exists`, and `index_done` must refresh the active notebook after bounded foreground polling has ended so the historical warning disappears as soon as an index is published. Queued status now discloses queue length / queued_at / next off-peak window start (UTC) / last total build duration, and the pending-actions bell reports `queued` verbatim instead of masquerading it as `building`.
  - Ask mode ids are protocol; their display names are UI. `chunk` / `reasoning` / `graph` and the group ids `general` / `strict` are what `POST /ask` accepts and what persisted sessions and bookmarks store — treat them as stable and never rename one to improve wording. The user-visible names are owned solely by the front-end registry `frontend/app/ask-modes.ts`: `chunk` → 通用问答, group `strict` (the picker entry, default engine `reasoning`) → 深入分析, `reasoning` → 逐步推理, `graph` → 关联追溯. Read them through `groupLabel()` / `modeLabel()`; no other front-end file may hardcode a display name, and prose mentioning one interpolates it. `ask-modes.test.mjs` recursively scans `frontend/app` and fails both on a current display name appearing outside the registry and on a retired name (严格推理 / 深挖推理 / 图谱多跳) reappearing, so a rename is a one-line registry edit. `scripts/check_ask_modes_contract.py` separately pins the id set against the backend registry.
  - Ask conversation history should use a single-row `历史 N` header entry and an expandable session manager; the adjacent `+` starts a new session directly. Do not restore a separate current-session context row or permanently split the constrained center panel.
  - Ask question timestamps use the browser-captured submission instant, persisted with the answer rather than substituting server answer-completion time. Hover reveals the browser-local label below the question; click pins it until an outside click. Today shows time only; another day in the current Monday-based week shows weekday plus time; outside the week shows date plus time, never date and weekday together. The current year is omitted and other years are included. A visible pinned label must refresh when the local calendar day changes.
  - Ask answer timestamps use the authoritative answer-write instant (`answers.created_at`), exposed as `AskResponse.answered_at`; reopening legacy payloads backfills it from the answer row without a migration. Answer cards use the same browser-local formatting, hover reveal, click-to-pin, outside-click dismissal, and local-midnight refresh rules as question cards.
  - Conversation history is ordered and timestamped by latest activity, not creation time; the activity clock must retain sub-second precision and SQLite ordering must compare absolute instants across UTC-offset changes. Once `/ask/stream` emits `started`, the durable conversation must appear in history immediately (including a new first turn with no model answer yet), become the current `conversation_id`, and remain reopenable after switching sessions—even if that switch wins the race before `started`. History publication, list-request generation, and terminal-summary refresh are notebook-scoped rather than visible-run/session-epoch-scoped; older orchestration callers follow the newest same-notebook list request (including through a failed intermediate request), while a delayed request from a different notebook must not invalidate the active notebook. The authoritative refresh must not be deferred until `final`, and the Ask composer/mode controls stay disabled while a selected session/notebook's latest detail is still loading so a late detail response cannot take over a newly submitted run.
  - Reasoning mode should surface backend agent progress in the Ask panel while the request is running. Use `POST /notebooks/{id}/ask/stream` for live NDJSON `progress` events, render the trace as a streaming one-line summary by default, provide click-to-expand details, and keep the final `reasoning_trace` visible on the answer; do not regress to a bare static "thinking" placeholder for reasoning requests.
  - The reasoning trace must cover the whole run, not just retrieval. The question-understanding preflight runs before any durable job exists, so the UI synthesizes its own leading trace steps (understanding in flight → understood / awaiting clarification → confirmed) and the backend steps stream in after them, rather than the phase getting its own separate status line outside the trace. That phase's client-measured wall clock is reported back as `intent.understanding_ms` (bounded, optional, never an input to retrieval) and becomes the persisted `intent` step's `duration_ms`, so a replayed trace does not silently drop it. The engine streams its `intent` step before Memory retrieval and records a `memory` step when private memories were recalled. That step reports RECALL, not attribution: memory routinely reaches the prompt without being cited, so gating it on bound anchors would under-report a lookup that did happen, and deferring it until synthesis would move it away from where it occurred; attribution is carried by the answer's `[k]` citations, and its wording must never claim the answer used those memories. A zero-hit lookup is recorded as a timed `skip` instead — the candidate query and embedding call still ran, and dropping their duration would contradict whole-run timing. The engine also records a `synthesis` step after answer generation — that generation call is usually the longest single slice of a run and must be neither invisible nor excluded from the trace total; its citation count comes from bound anchors, never from the retrieved-evidence cards. Live trace panels are gated on whether the engine actually streams a trace (`AskMode.streaming`, mirrored as the frontend's `streamsTrace` and locked by `scripts/check_ask_modes_contract.py`), never on its display group: a non-streaming engine in a strict group would otherwise show an empty "waiting for backend events" panel for the whole request.
  - Reasoning mode is intent-first. The official UI calls `POST /notebooks/{id}/ask/intent` before creating a durable Ask job; this preview may use the latest prior user questions, but not corpus-derived assistant answers, and must not read notebook/base corpus or create a conversation/job. Clear intent auto-confirms, while direction-changing ambiguity pauses for user answers. Because clear intent has no human review, its original user wording remains the authoritative first retrieval seed and any model-normalized wording is supplemental; after blocking ambiguity is answered, the reviewed resolved question becomes authoritative. Freeze mandatory topics/retrieval directions, entities, comparison axes, constraints, exclusions, assumptions, expected output, and answers without a second interpretation pass. Use that contract for Memory, PPR, initial subqueries, evidence retrieval, and answer synthesis; retain the original user question as the visible/saved turn. The whole authoritative question runs first, then reviewed directions are round-robin seeded so each mandatory topic gets coverage before extra aliases. An effort level's initial-subquery ceiling bounds first-round concurrency only; it must never silently discard the reviewed directions that do not fit. Directions past that width go into a deferred coverage ledger and are executed in contract order after the deterministic PPR/exact seed passes and before the reflect loop, sharing the same `max_steps` budget (the coverage pass may consume at most half of it, so the reflect loop always keeps a working share). A per-run registry maps every reviewed direction to a unique short label — widening the truncation window and, failing that, appending a deterministic numeric suffix, so two directions whose default labels collide still get independent identities — and back again, since the model only ever sees and resubmits the label, never the seed's full contract text. `add_subquery` submissions are resolved through that registry: one matching an uncovered direction executes the seed's own contract text (not the bare label) and is recorded under the seed's identity, and one matching a direction already covered — whether by the coverage pass or an earlier `add_subquery` — is deduplicated rather than re-run. Reflection sees the current uncovered set every round, and a single `skip` trace step naming whatever remains uncovered is emitted once the reflect loop ends (naming nothing, and emitting no step, if reflection covered them all) rather than freezing the state at the moment the coverage pass ran out of budget. The reasoning planner must not silently replace these seeds; later reflection may only add evidence-driven queries. Invalid confirmation returns 422 before a durable job exists, and aborting the preflight propagates a cancellation event to the intent model call. That whole **first round** — run-ledger setup, understanding/experience/collection-map prompt scaffolding, planning, initial retrieval, the PPR seed pass, the exact-lookup seed pass, the empty-evidence element fallback, and the confirmed-direction coverage pass — is carried by `reasoning_retrieval.ReasoningRetriever._run_first_round`, whose stages are the `_first_round_*` methods; run-level state crosses those stages through the explicit `_ReasoningRunState` dataclass. That object is a one-time handoff into the reflect loop, not a running authority: `run` unpacks it into locals exactly once, so its mutable containers stay the same objects while its scalar fields go stale the moment the loop starts writing the locals. Stage order is itself contract — in particular the exact-lookup seed must stay after the PPR seed so PPR's dedup and counts remain bit-identical. Trace-step kinds/order/`detail` key sets, per-step request counts, and embedding calls are pinned byte-for-byte by `backend/tests/fixtures/reasoning_first_round_golden.json` (`backend/tests/test_reasoning_first_round_golden.py`; regenerate only with `REGEN_FIRST_ROUND_GOLDEN=1` and only against the revision you intend as the baseline).
  - Reasoning retrieval effort and collection completeness are separate protocol contracts. `retrieval_effort` has stable ids `overview` / `standard` (default) / `deep` / `thorough` / `exhaustive`; their per-query ranked take is `4/8/8/12/16`, final `floor/aspect/cap` is `8/2/12`, `20/3/36`, `24/4/48`, `32/5/64`, `40/6/96`, maximum `steps/initial-subqueries` is `4/2`, `8/5`, `16/6`, `32/8`, `50/10`, KG/chunk prompt characters are `4000/12000`, `6000/30000`, `8000/50000`, `12000/80000`, `16000/120000`, and the direct source elements (formulas/tables/images/…) admitted into the final synthesis prompt (`answer_element_items`) are `4/6/8/12/16` — chosen by retrieval relevance descending (tie-break element id), not insertion order, and still consuming the shared chunk-context budget. The same five profiles also carry the collection-enumeration tools' budget: `enum_page_size` is `50` at every effort, `enum_pages_per_run` is `2/4/6/8/12`, and `enum_rows_per_run` is `100/200/300/400/600`. Final ranked selection is `min(cap, max(floor, aspect × executed-query-count))`; a model may stop early but never raise a ceiling. Candidate recall never scales with effort but is independently deployment-configurable: `CHUNK_RECALL` defaults to 200 and separately bounds indexed Chunk/KG ANN and lexical windows; `RELATION_RECALL` defaults to 200 and separately bounds Relation ANN and the total lexical relation-id window, whose source/target directions retain reserved shares. These defaults are not request-level hard ceilings. Intent records `ranked` / `complete` / `aggregate` / `hybrid`; explicit completeness for Knowhow must use stable cursor enumeration, not a larger Top-N. All five efforts share 25 rows/page, 50 pages, 1,250 physical rows/request, 8 tables/request, 8 selected columns/table, 1,000 characters/cell model excerpt, 256,000 characters/structured payload, 100 rows inline in answer prose, and 20 rows initially visible per result card. Cursor exhaustion plus stable table catalog, row counts, column metadata, and selected table scope is required for `complete=true`; touching any row/page/table/column/payload rail or observing concurrent mutation returns `complete=false` with `explicit_partial` and must never claim “all”. A 100-row Knowhow table therefore returns `100/100`; a lower effort may reduce analysis but never lowers explicit-completeness enumeration limits. Exact-completeness enumeration of physical rows currently covers Knowhow only; the model-invoked `enumerate_elements`/`enumerate_kg_objects` reflect tools — plus their `enumerate.collection="sources"` parameter value, which kept the model-facing action space at ten ids for this feature (the outline scratchpad below separately added a genuine eleventh action, `update_outline`) — separately cover the whitelisted source-element kinds, KG object types and the library's user-visible document roster (see the Architecture Baseline entry below); Memory and any other collection remain relevance-ranked and must disclose that boundary. That disclosure is suppressed only when FOUR deterministic conditions hold together: `result_scope != "aggregate"`, the intent contract's `constraints`/`excluded_topics`/`assumptions` are all empty, at least one collection result card returned rows, and that card's coverage is `complete`. The bias is deliberately toward over-warning: a card's coverage proves that one physical collection was walked end to end, never that the physical collection is the subset the question asked for, and no deterministic test exists for the latter.
  - The exact-completeness executor is deliberately narrower than the intent labels: confirmation reclassifies scope from the final edited wording and authoritative clarification answers, and only direct whole-table physical-row/method lists, direct row/record counts, and their hybrid analysis may enumerate. Conditional subsets, distinct/type counts such as “how many kinds”, and group-by requests without a deterministic plan must fall back and disclose that exact completeness is unsupported. The lightweight catalog returns at most 8 table descriptors and no cells, code attachments, or health payloads; an explicitly named table is prioritized before applying that window, while aggregate counts/sequences still cover the whole notebook. Responses separate per-table, batch, and synthesis coverage: a 200/200 enumeration with a 100/200 model preview is “enumeration complete, analysis partial”, while an 8-table batch truncation does not make an individually exhausted selected table partial. KG objects/relations, confirmed Memory, and query-time chains share the KG character budget; structured preview, chunks, and direct elements share the source budget; the final evidence block may not exceed their sum.
  - Reasoning mode wires the exact-identifier channel in twice, and both are zero-model. This wiring reaches the `reasoning` ask mode and, because the report engine reuses `ReasoningRetriever` verbatim, every deep-report section's retrieval; `graph` mode does not wire it in. (1) A deterministic seed pass runs once immediately after the initial retrieval whenever `exact_probe_terms(question)` is non-empty — mirroring the PPR seed pass, it does not bet on whether the agent picks the action. It scores its hits by the joined identifier names it actually probed, not by the raw question — producer-native scoring is this retriever's existing convention (a PPR hit is not scored "relevance to the raw question" either), and scoring the exact match against every unrelated token in a long question sank a genuine hit's relevance low enough to lose the character-budget cut and, as the sole anchor, the grounded/overview threshold (a measured regression). It records an `exact_lookup` trace step whose detail carries the names actually probed (already truncated to `EXACT_LOOKUP_MAX_IDENTIFIERS`), the yield, and `phase`. It is placed after the PPR seed pass so PPR's dedup and counts stay bit-identical. A question with no identifier issues zero calls and adds zero trace steps. (2) The reflect model may choose the `exact_lookup` action with an `exact_term`, scored the same producer-native way. The action shares the seed pass's `exact_probe_terms` gate — an arbitrary low-selectivity string, and equally an ordinary hyphenated English compound, is refused with a skip rather than becoming a library-wide substring scan, because that gate is a measured cost contract and must not be delegated to the model — shares one per-run ledger keyed by name so the seed pass's names cannot be re-requested, executes only the names new this round, and is capped at `_MAX_EXACT_LOOKUPS = 3` agent invocations (the seed pass does not consume that quota). Every skip — channel disabled, missing name, non-identifier shape, duplicate, or over the per-run cap — feeds a teaching note back into that same ledger (not just a TraceStep), deduplicated by reason/name so a model repeating the same invalid input does not grow the ledger without bound; e.g. a non-identifier term teaches back "「2.1」不是可精确查找的名称(要像 set_db、config.yaml 这样带下划线或点;只用连字符连接的词还需带数字,如 GPT-4)" — phrased as what to supply next, because a note that only says "invalid" gets the model to retry with another equally invalid ordinary word — so the next reflection can act on the reason instead of silently retrying. `exact_term` is cleaned (unwrapped, not truncated) at parse time so the `reflect()` fail-closed 2000-character field cap can actually reject an oversized value; truncation to the lexical layer's phrase cap happens only at the point of use. Both paths go through `_filter_candidates` and an `allow_exact_lookup` instance policy flag mirroring `allow_ppr`: knowhow completion sets it `False` for the same reason it disables PPR/community below — its JSON-envelope query would otherwise make the seed pass unconditionally (and uselessly) probe the envelope's own field names on every completion request. The action name and prompt line are static rather than flag-dependent, following the precedent set by `ppr_retrieve`: with `EXACT_LOOKUP_ENABLED=false` or the policy flag off, the action is skipped at execution with zero I/O instead of being removed from the prompt/whitelist, so the action contract does not drift with deployment configuration. The trace step renders with the concise `精查` label.
  - Reasoning mode exposes `follow_chain` as an internal agent action, not a separate Ask mode or button. It may compose only evidence-backed, same-type two-hop paths from the explicit transitive whitelist (`derived_from/kind_of/prerequisite_of/precedes/part_of`). The two stored relations are separately citable; the composed conclusion must be labelled as an inference and must never be persisted as a KG edge. Its live/final trace step must render with the concise `推导` label and bounded detail rather than raw evidence text.
  - Reasoning mode exposes `consult_memory` (Agentic Memory P4) as a twelfth, zero-parameter reflect action the model may spend one turn on, not a separate Ask mode or a channel that reads any user content: it recalls entries from the deployment-global retrieval-strategy experience library plus the caller's own notebook-understanding overlay note, never evidence, never citable with `[k]`, and never a source of retrieval scope — the same "how to search, never what to read" rule the passive experience block already keeps. It exists in the schema only when `retrieval_effort` is `deep`/`thorough`/`exhaustive` and the experience library's own injection flag is on; the tier gate is folded into that same flag rather than being an independent switch. Its trace step renders with the concise `回想` label (never `记忆`, which the Memory-recall step already owns) and a call that returns nothing new records a `skip` step instead. Full contract: `docs/product-and-api.md`/`_zh.md`, "Retrieval strategy experience" / 「检索策略经验」.
  - Answers must stay evidence-grounded, render Markdown/code/formula/table content cleanly, use compact numbered citations (`[1]`, `[2]`, ...), including model-emitted numeric citation groups such as `[1, 2, 3]` when every number maps to a known answer reference, expand citation details inside the answer panel (not in an overflowing floating popover), and support lightweight 👍/👎/copy actions. A whole-line single-line `$$...$$` emitted next to prose is still display math, and wide display formulas scroll locally rather than widening/clipping the Ask, report, Memory, or Knowhow panel. Do not flatten all related knowledge under each answer; route deeper exploration from the citation area into the Knowledge Graph.
  - Ask input ergonomics: `Enter` submits, `Shift+Enter` inserts a newline. While a model response is running, lock the input and mode controls, prevent duplicate sends, and turn the send button into an interrupt control that restores the draft question for editing. Explicit interruption must call `POST /notebooks/{id}/ask/jobs/{job_id}/cancel`; that endpoint sets the backend cancellation event so the in-flight Ask worker / LLM path stops and does not save a cancelled final answer. A transport disconnect only stops delivery to that client; navigation, refresh, or loss of the `/ask/stream` connection leaves the detached worker running to completion.
  - Prompt chips should run useful first-version questions derived from the notebook's imported source titles/summaries when available; the menu should expose a real clear/reset action.
- Knowledge Graph opens as a full-screen workspace overlay.
  - On open: fetch the current graph data and `GET /unified-kg/status`; show a refresh button when the graph is dirty. Do not trigger an automatic rebuild on open.
  - Opening the graph from an Ask knowledge-object citation must select and center the cited node, even when that node is outside the bounded high-degree core. Fetch its bounded one-hop neighborhood from the citation's real source notebook (including mounted base notebooks) and merge it into the view; graph-BFS anchors retain their owning base notebook, but every browser request stays authorized by the active notebook. The backend validates/resolves the object only inside that active notebook's participant set and proxies the read internally — mounting a public base does not grant direct notebook membership. A raw cited Concept id must be resolved through bounded `cluster_fold_rows` to the canonical graph id returned as `focus_id`, while the response also preserves the raw object id used for context so selecting the folded node does not query a synthetic `K-*` id from `knowledge_objects`. When a large notebook's viz artifact is unavailable, return an explicit temporarily-unlocatable result instead of entering the full cluster-map DB fallback; the UI keeps the graph open, shows a retryable notice, and never queues an impossible raw-id focus.
  - Use the object-level unified graph so Concept / Claim / Formula / Procedure nodes can appear together; do not fall back to a concept-only graph when object-level relationships exist.
  - The main canvas should show node names, type-specific node marks, and relationship labels on edges.
  - Provide multi-select type filters for dense graphs. Selecting a node from either the canvas or the overview should focus/highlight that node and update the selected-node relation/source details.
  - Keep node type color/shape marks consistent between the canvas, node overview, selected-node detail, and related-node sections. In the detail panel, label evidence/source excerpts as `出处`, render them as structured evidence cards with separate source metadata and wrapped excerpt text, hide raw `section_path`, and show concept-mounted objects as `相关节点` grouped by type.
  - Knowledge Graph evidence and occurrence cards must render `element_type=formula` excerpts through the shared block KaTeX view. Keep wide equations locally scrollable, malformed LaTeX visibly readable as raw text, and non-formula excerpts as ordinary text.
  - The side panel should provide a type-grouped node overview (Concept, Claim, Formula, Procedure, plus future types) and selected-node relation/evidence details.
- Do not show a fixed right-column Studio sidebar in the primary notebook workspace.
  - Keep the source column + four-tab (`问答 (Ask) | 知识库 (Knowledge) | 记忆 (Memory) | 深度报告 (Deep Report)`) main column as the two-column workspace.
  - The Analysis menu itself contains only the promotion queue (admin), tier toggle (admin), and edge-review queue. Dashboard and the full-screen Knowledge Graph are separate top-toolbar actions; 「图谱 Schema」 (object-type/field management; see 界面词汇表) is reached from the Knowledge Graph view header, not a separate top-toolbar action. Administrators own the global baseline. Every notebook reads that baseline by default, while its owner may copy-on-write an inherited type, disable it locally, or create a notebook-only type; deleting an override restores inheritance, and deleting a notebook-only type is rejected while objects of that type still exist. Notebook readers can inspect the effective definitions but cannot write. The notebook-scoped effective registry drives Ask rendering, knowledge browsing/type labels, and induction; the canonical source-extraction graph remains the separately governed four-type edge contract, so changing a registry definition does not rewrite already-extracted objects or widen that extractor. Global routes remain admin-only, notebook routes retain notebook read/write guards, and proposals are isolated to their notebook and creator. Historical global custom rows with no notebook owner remain global and are never guessed into a user's ownership. The existing analytics view has separate Memory and Knowhow content-asset cards served by `GET /api/notebooks/{id}/analytics/content-overview`: Memory totals/statuses/recent rows are scoped to the authenticated viewer and requested notebook (never admin cross-user analytics); Knowhow table/row, projection pending/failed, stale-code, and recent-table metrics follow notebook read access. Cards navigate only to the existing Memory and Knowhow pages/editors, whose write restrictions remain authoritative; do not add another browser/editor.
  - The dashboard also surfaces a read-only pipeline health check (`GET /api/notebooks/{id}/checkup`, aggregating H2–H8 damage/pending signals: empty source, missing chunks, missing chunk/element vectors, KG-pending, stale/corrupt scale index). It upgrades the existing source-status and index-and-build blocks plus the bell rather than adding a panel; a healthy notebook stays neutral. Repair actions reuse existing CTAs except two new ones: `POST /sources/reparse` (batch) and `POST /backfill-vectors`. All model-calling repairs (reparse, backfill, analyze) are user-triggered, never automatic. H checks are read-only and cheap per-notebook: H6 pending-count and H7 index-state are memoized; H4/H5 are two anti-join COUNTs behind a **single-slot** per-notebook memo keyed on `(this notebook's active-lease snapshot, ≤30s TTL)` — the exclude-active-leases predicate handed to the counts is unchanged (the process-global snapshot, verbatim), and only the *key* is narrowed to the leases that belong to this notebook, so another notebook's upload no longer evicts this one's entry. The key cannot be a monotonic sequence (embedding success does not bump `kg_mutation_seq`, and `sources.updated_at` only moves on element replacement — keying on either would leave the counts *permanently* stale after a repair, which is worse), so a TTL is the fallback: **H4/H5 may be up to 30s stale**. Single-slot matters: a multi-slot cache would still hold the pre-repair `active=∅` entry and serve it back the moment the backfill job releases its lease. H8 (disk integrity) is cached on the on-disk manifest identity — never on `version_signal`, which is decoupled from the index artifacts. **H6 "pending analysis" is not `NOT EXISTS(knowledge_objects)`.** A document with very little text, or one that is entirely uncaptioned images, completes extraction with zero knowledge objects; counting that as pending made the signal un-clearable — the per-source badge stayed 待分析 forever, the dashboard kept offering 「继续分析」, and every incremental analysis re-ran those sources at full model cost only to produce zero again (that is what a user reads as "analysis keeps failing"). A source is analysed-and-empty when its latest `kg` extraction run is `completed`, its message starts with the zero-object marker the success path writes, it has no failed windows, and it is not a partial retry; `no-llm` runs deliberately do NOT qualify, because those really have not been analysed and must be picked up once a model is configured. The predicate has one authoritative statement, `app/models/sources.py::kg_analyzed_without_objects`, consumed by four sites: the two pending-count queries mirror it in SQL (that position must be a single `COUNT`), while the source projection and `_kg_target_batches` call the Python function directly. `backend/tests/test_kg_empty_extraction_marker.py` reconciles the SQLite mirror against the Python predicate case by case on a real database and pins both failure directions (under-excluding leaves the count stuck; over-excluding silently strips sources that were never analysed). `SourceSummary` carries it as an additive `kg_analyzed_empty` boolean beside `kg_extracted` — the two are mutually exclusive and give the badge three states. Incremental analysis skips these sources; `mode="rebuild"` still selects them, which is the path back after switching extraction models or re-parsing with OCR.
  - A **KG quality analysis** panel (batch 1 of 4; `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-25-kg-analysis-view-design.md`) opens from a `图谱分析` button inside the Knowledge Graph view header, next to `图谱 Schema`. It must lead with actionable interpretations (report reliability, merge signal, topic-structure signal, and source-review candidate), visibly explain red/yellow/gray/current states, and say that convergence/connectivity are diagnostic signals rather than monotonic quality scores. The five artifact rows each explain what question they answer; largest concept groups and relation provenance are rendered as diagnostic sections rather than ledger-only receipts. `GET /notebooks/{id}/kg-analysis` and `GET /notebooks/{id}/kg-analysis/sources` require only notebook read access. Editors additionally receive an in-panel generate/update action that reuses the confirmed `POST /notebooks/{id}/unified-kg/rebuild` background workflow and its completion poll, does not re-extract sources, and refreshes the report automatically; read-only members retain report access without the action. Both GETs only read three precompute product tables (`kg_community_edges`, `kg_source_profiles`, `kg_analysis_artifacts`; schema v34) that `rebuild_communities` writes wholesale — the online path never scans the full object/edge/cluster tables. Publication is **atomic**: the board partition (`communities` + `community_members`), its `community_seq` stamp and all three product tables land in **one** write transaction, so no concurrent reader can ever observe recast boards paired with the previous ledger (that window used to be minutes long on the production library). Every full-table read that feeds the products therefore runs **before** that transaction opens and must stay there — `SqliteDatabase.write()` holds a process-wide lock, so a full scan inside it queues every write in the library. Because the board partition is not in the database yet at that point, the source-profile scan (`source_canonical_rows`) joins only `concept_clusters` and returns one `(source_id, canonical_id)` row per usable object; canonical→board mapping and the per-board counts happen in memory (`SourceBoardCounter`, two int32 columns per row plus one in-place sort), which is byte-for-byte equivalent because `community_members` is a partition written from the very membership held in memory. Each endpoint runs **all** of its reads inside **one** shared read snapshot from the store's backend-neutral `read_snapshot()` (empty parameter list; SQLite `BEGIN DEFERRED` WAL snapshot deliberately without `PRAGMA query_only`, PostgreSQL `BEGIN … READ ONLY` at REPEATABLE READ): the overview's state row, ledger, board list (`community_overview_on` — the connection-taking half of `community_overview`, which keeps a self-opening entry for callers holding no read connection) and cross-board edges; `/sources`' state row, ledger, count and page. Both backends otherwise take a snapshot **per statement** (SQLite autocommit / PostgreSQL READ COMMITTED), so a concurrent precompute or community rebuild committing mid-request would staple one generation's freshness stamps onto another generation's board ids, or pair old boards with new edges and draw dangling links in the board map; opening a separate snapshot per read is exactly as broken as opening none. Merge-convergence rate is reported **per object type** (concept/claim/formula/procedure computed separately; mixing them dilutes the true concept convergence rate roughly 3x). The precompute has its **own freshness gate**, independent of the community layer's `community_seq` gate: the judgment is "ledger missing, or ledger seq != current `community_seq`", not "does the graph need rebuilding" — sharing the community gate would mean an already-rebuilt production base library (where `community_seq == kg_mutation_seq` predates this feature) never backfills the ledger without a forced rebuild. `kg_source_profiles` is the only product row allowed to be legitimately absent (a zero-community library has no meaningful mainstream share, so writing an all-zero table would misreport every source as an outlier); the other four artifact kinds must all be written together or the write fails closed. `kg_community_edges` carries a hard row cap (`MAX_PERSISTED_COMMUNITY_EDGES`, kept by weight) with explicit `truncated`/`edges_total` accounting rather than a silent cutoff; that cap bounds what is **persisted**, not what the fold **allocates**, so the fold also drains its input edge map incrementally (`popitem`) instead of iterating it — otherwise the aggregate and the 8.36M-entry input would both be resident at the same peak. Every reported number is stamped with the generations it was built at and how far behind the live graph it is, per metric rather than one page-level staleness banner: `kg_mutation_seq` for every artifact, plus `built_at_cluster_seq` (`unified_kg_state.cluster_mutation_seq`) for the four computed from `concept_clusters`, because cluster writes deliberately do not bump `kg_mutation_seq`. The two board-dependent artifacts stamp the merge generation their **board partition** was built on, which is an explicit `null` whenever the round only backfilled the ledger (the partition is then pre-existing and nothing records its merge generation); `stale` is correspondingly tri-state and `null` must never be rendered as "up to date". The action refreshes derived analysis state only; this view still performs no delete/quarantine governance.
  - Do not document or reintroduce retired content-generation, article, or derived-rule controls as current UI.
- The candidate Review Queue was removed: the KG extractor writes approved knowledge objects directly (no candidate staging). Knowledge governance is dedupe/merge over the objects in the Knowledge browser.

## MVP Scope

The MVP is a real-team local beta, not a throwaway mockup.

Confirmed scope:

- Mixed Chinese/English source material.
- First supported file types: PDF, Markdown, DOCX, PPTX.
- Real multipart upload and local storage of original files.
- Parser-generated `SourceElement` records with element-level citation granularity.
- Source summary after parsing. Use the OpenAI-compatible client when configured; otherwise use deterministic fallback.
- Notebook-internal search over notebook metadata, source metadata, source element text, and knowledge-object payloads.
- LLM-backed KG extraction for Concept / Claim / Formula / Procedure objects with evidence binding is implemented. Legacy candidate tables/endpoints still exist for governance compatibility, but the current no-LLM offline extraction path records `error_message='no-llm'` and does not synthesize rule/method/risk candidates.
- Current user-facing knowledge capabilities are Ask (`chunk` / `graph` / `reasoning`), the Knowledge browser, creator-private Memory, and Deep Reports. The current report contract is the `reports` table and `/reports` APIs; retired Scenario/Case/Checklist and content-studio endpoints are not current capabilities.
- Knowledge governance: `knowledge_objects` has a status lifecycle (`reviewed/approved/deprecated/conflict/project_specific`) plus `owner`/`last_reviewed`. Only USABLE statuses (approved/reviewed/project_specific/conflict) feed answers; `deprecated` is excluded, including during Ask's one-hop KG neighbour expansion. Browse dynamically via `GET /notebooks/{id}/knowledge-types` + `GET /notebooks/{id}/knowledge?type=...`, edit via `PATCH /notebooks/{id}/knowledge/{knowledge_id}`, dedupe via `GET .../duplicates` + `POST /notebooks/{id}/knowledge/{knowledge_id}/merge`.
- Two-tier KB & tier-aware retrieval (Wave 1+2): `notebooks.tier` is `base` | `personal` (default `personal`), set through the tier repository methods and `POST /notebooks/{id}/tier`. Baseline chunk retrieval remains active-only; optional KG overlay/PPR may add federated KG context and base-backed chunks, while graph/reasoning use federated KG paths. The exact-score `base` tie-break applies only to knowledge-object hits returned by `federated_retrieve()`; `federated_retrieve_relations()` sorts relation hits by score only. This is separate from the answer-prompt rule that defers to base and discloses a contradiction.
- Graph-reasoning Ask mode (`mode="graph"`, opt-in/experimental — default stays `chunk`): `ask_graph` uses PPR when available and otherwise builds a rustworkx in-memory graph from `knowledge_relations` (`app/services/kg/graph_reason.py`), traverses bounded multi-hop chains (`max_depth=3`, `max_fan_out=8` defaults), runs answer-time adversarial chain verification, and reports a weakest-link, authority-weighted `chain_trust` (personal hop factor `0.85`). The graph is cached with the same VectorCache version-key pattern as the vector matrix.
- Edge trust & curation (Track E): per-edge trust signals (evidence/corroboration/type-validity) plus a curator review queue (`review_queue`/`set_edge_review`); reviewer-rejected edges are excluded from graph reasoning. Endpoints: `GET /notebooks/{id}/edge-review-queue`, `POST /notebooks/{id}/relations/{rel_id}/review`. Front-end exposes an edge-review queue modal (from the analysis actions menu) that lists relations ranked by review priority with confirm / reject actions, mirroring the promotion-queue modal pattern.
- Governance / promotion (Track F): owner-triggered personal→base node promotion state machine (`propose_promotion`/`list_promotion_queue`/`approve_promotion`/`reject_promotion`), dedup-on-approve reusing the merge clustering. Backend `POST /notebooks/{id}/knowledge/{knowledge_id}/promote`, `GET /promotion-queue`, `POST /promotion-queue/{id}/approve|reject`; front-end exposes a promotion queue modal + per-object "propose promotion" affordance (personal-tier objects only).
- User-created notebooks, imported sources, and deep reports expose delete actions. Reparse preserves the source row and original file: it replaces source elements/chunks and their embeddings, and removes extraction runs plus source-derived knowledge before rebuilding. Delete performs the same cleanup, then deletes the source row (cascading source-owned records) and local file.
- User accounts: self-service registration (username rule: exactly one ASCII letter + literal `00` + 6 digits, e.g. `a00123456`; stored lower-cased) + password login (PBKDF2-SHA256). Auth uses opaque DB session tokens (`auth_sessions` table) passed as `Authorization: Bearer`; resolution is read-mostly and sliding-expiry writes are throttled by `AUTH_SESSION_TOUCH_INTERVAL_SECONDS` (default 300). Each notebook is owned by its creator. The built-in `admin` owns pre-existing notebooks. Administrators may grant or revoke the `admin` role from the user-usage page through `PATCH /admin/users/{user_id}/role`; the built-in administrator and the active administrator's own role are immutable, authorization must be revalidated inside the role-change write transaction, and existing sessions observe the new role on their next request. The user-usage table sorts the complete fetched user set before paginating it, defaults to 20 rows per page, offers 20/50/100-row page sizes, and exposes sortable headers for user identity, role, timestamps, usage counts, and document limit. Its Ask usage column and expanded notebook rows expose `questions`, the count of durable `ask_jobs` submissions owned by that user (including failed or cancelled jobs), rather than the number of `conversations` containers. The user total includes submissions made in joined read-only notebooks; the expanded inventory intentionally remains owner-only, so its question cells are a breakdown over owned notebooks rather than a sum that must equal the user total; the per-user report count has the same shape (the overview total counts reports by `reports.created_by`, including ones created in shared notebooks, while the breakdown stays owner-only and need not sum to it). The legacy `conversations` response field remains during the compatibility window but is deprecated and is not displayed as question usage. Each notebook caps user-uploaded documents at the owner's effective limit (per-user override `user_profiles.upload_document_limit`, else the global default in `app_settings` key `upload_document_limit_default`, else `USER_UPLOAD_DOCUMENT_LIMIT`=20); administrator-owned notebooks are exempt. The cap counts only user-visible documents (`source_type NOT IN ('memory','knowhow')`, matching the source list) and is enforced before source creation in the upload/import/link endpoints (409 via `user_error`); admins set it from the user-usage page via `PATCH /admin/settings/upload-limit-default` (global) and `PATCH /admin/users/{user_id}/upload-limit` (per-user; `null` clears). Every administrator may publish a notebook as a public knowledge base (`tier='base'`). Base notebooks are excluded from regular users' own-library lists but are discoverable through each notebook's reference-library picker (`notebook_bases`), and participate in retrieval only for notebooks that explicitly mount them. Auth is enforced through `get_current_user`, and synchronous SQLite authorization work must stay off the event loop. Share links are implemented: small notebooks can be copied; large notebooks can be joined read-only. Owners retain write authority. There is no live collaborative editing. Password management is self-service plus admin reset: `PATCH /me/password` verifies the caller's current password, rewrites the PBKDF2 triple, and revokes every other `auth_sessions` row for that user while keeping the requesting session; `POST /admin/users/{user_id}/reset-password` (admin-only, and it must carry a real bearer session — the auth-optional anonymous fallback is refused) revokes all of the target's browser sessions so they must sign in with the new password. Agent long-lived credentials (`agent_access_tokens`) are deliberately outside both revocation scopes. `/auth/login` verifies the password and issues the session inside one write transaction (`login_with_password`; SQLite write lock / PostgreSQL `FOR UPDATE` on the users row), so a login racing a rotation is serialized against it — it either lands before the rotation (and its session is swept by the rotation's revocation DELETE) or after it (and the superseded password fails verification); splitting authenticate-then-create-session would let an old-password session survive rotation. Both paths reject the built-in `user-local` administrator with 409 (`BuiltinAdminPasswordError`) because seeding rewrites its password from `SILICON_NOTEBOOK_ADMIN_PASSWORD` on every startup, so an online change would be silently rolled back; the UI hides its change-password entry and marks its user-usage row as protected. A finished deep report can additionally be published as a sign-in-free read-only page; issuing is idempotent, revocation is indistinguishable from an unknown token, and unfinished reports are refused. The anonymous route must live on a router without the blanket `Depends(get_current_user)` and must only call repository methods that do not consult the current user, since an unset ContextVar falls back to the seeded admin. Its payload is an allowlist — body, question, timing, and per-citation title/filename/location/excerpt — never `source_id`/`element_id`/`object_id`/`notebook_id` or the `understanding` contract. What that page isolates is the product layer, not the rendering pipeline: it must reuse `remarkCitations` (same `[k]`/【k】 syntax as Ask and reports, rendered as clickable numbers taken from the number inside the key — the projection drops entries with neither title nor excerpt, so positional numbering would disagree with the body) and the shared `.answer-table-wrap` / `.answer-code` wrappers so wide content scrolls inside its own block, and it must import `katex/dist/katex.min.css` itself because it is the one surface that never loads `app/page.tsx` — omitting it raises no error and silently renders every formula twice, once as character-by-character MathML.
- A finished multi-turn Ask conversation can equally be published as a sign-in-free read-only page at `/c/{token}`, mirroring the report mechanism end to end — a separate anonymous router, a row-level creator gate, an allowlist payload, and the same shared `remarkCitations`/`.answer-table-wrap`/`.answer-code`/`katex.min.css` rendering pipeline — with only the conversation-specific additions registered here. Issuing (`POST /notebooks/{nb}/conversations/{cid}/share`) is idempotent and simultaneously advances the `shared_through_at`/`shared_through_id` watermark; the request body's `expected_through_id` (the newest answer the client saw in the turns it disclosed) pins the watermark to exactly that answer — a newer answer that landed between the client's disclosure read and this POST is not published, closing the disclosure TOCTOU — while a deleted disclosed boundary is refused (409, reload) and an empty body falls back to the current latest (sharing and "update to latest" are the same call). The public page renders only turns written before that watermark (an in-flight turn — a job with no answer row yet — is excluded by construction); the boundary is a keyset on the watermark answer's `(created_at, rowid/ordinal)` tuple (exact tie-break, so a same-instant answer sorting after the watermark is not pulled in), falling back to the pure `created_at` interval only when `shared_through_id` no longer resolves. Sharing is refused for a blank-`created_by` conversation and for one with zero answers (409 either way, the latter rolling back the token it just issued). The payload additionally excludes `reasoning_trace`, `intent`, `retrieval_scope`/`retrieval_query`, and every addressable id including `memory_id`; a cited Memory excerpt is still disclosed — a sharer can only ever cite their own Memory — but the share dialog must state the excerpt count before the link goes out. A cited image resolves through a per-token HMAC-derived alias (`conversation_asset_alias`, never the real `asset_id`) served by `GET /public/conversations/{token}/assets/{alias}` with `Cache-Control: no-store`, scoped to only the assets referenced in that frozen snapshot. Result-set cards are not projected in v1, but their count is disclosed (`PublicTurn.omitted_result_sets`) rather than silently dropped. Each turn's question **and the conversation title** are served whole, and that is only a *bounded* promise because the write side refuses over-length text — one rail each. `AskRequest.question` carries `max_length=ASK_QUESTION_MAX_CHARS` (4,000, the same value the report side uses and for the same reason — codex #525 R1 P2), so `/ask`, `/ask/stream` and `/ask/intent` all answer 422 above it, and the MCP tool `ask_notebook` refuses it too with its own actionable message rather than a raw validation error — a deliberate behaviour change for long-lived Agent tokens, which could previously submit a question of any length. `ConversationRenameRequest.title` carries `max_length=CONVERSATION_TITLE_MAX_CHARS` (200 — a title is a one-line label, not question-length prose), so `PATCH /conversations/{id}` answers 422 above it; renaming is the only way a title grows past the 60 characters the server slices off the first question, so that one endpoint is the whole write side, and the MCP surface has no rename tool to mirror. On both rails the client blocks submission at the same limit counted in Unicode code points, never clipping the user's text. One leftover is recorded rather than hidden: rows written before these rails (an older turn's question, a title renamed earlier) can still be longer, and bounding one in the projection would need a disclosure field plus a public-page change. Two entry points share that one call: the button on a session card in the history popover publishes the **whole conversation** (`expected_through_id` = its current last answer, i.e. the existing "update to latest" semantics), while the button in each answer's action row — **after** the copy button — publishes **through that answer**, by sending that answer's id as `expected_through_id`. No new endpoint and no new server behaviour; an in-flight answer with a blank `answer_id` gets no button. Three UI contracts hold only in that bounded mode. (1) `expected_through_id` is the clicked id itself and must never fall back to the empty string when the conversation detail fails to load — an empty body publishes the current latest, so a dialog reading "share up to this one" would hand out the whole conversation. (2) The watermark is **advance-only** (the regression check in `share_conversation`, codex #522 R3), so "already shared further along, now clicking an earlier answer" is something the server cannot do; that branch offers **no publish action at all** and instead states that the link already contains this answer plus N later turns, pointing at revoke-then-reshare as the only way to narrow it — offering a button would only return a 409 saying the conversation changed, when nothing changed. That branch's intro copy must drop the narrowing promise too: the copy control sits between it and the detail line, so a dialog that opens with "contains only this answer and earlier turns" gets the link sent before the correction below is ever read (codex #530 R1 P1). Success notices follow the same rule — a bounded update reports the selected answer, never "updated to latest". More precisely, the watermark's position relative to the boundary is a five-state value, not a boolean (`shareScopeState`: `unshared`/`at`/`behind`/`ahead`/`unknown`), and the intro copy needs a distinct sentence per state: it renders above the link box and the copy button, so it must describe what the link contains **now** rather than what pressing the button would publish — only the `unshared` sentence is a promise, because no link exists yet. All three review findings came from collapsing those five into one: `behind` (the link stops at an earlier turn, so the clicked answer is not in it yet) lets a user send a snapshot missing that answer, and `unknown` (the watermark names an answer absent from the loaded turns — another tab advanced the share after the detail read) was being treated as "not ahead" and thus asserted a narrower range. The range claim must also be revalidated before the link is copied (codex #530 R3 P1): the token is stable, so another tab advancing the same share *after* this modal loaded makes the link expose later turns while the copy above it still describes the old range. `doCopy` re-reads the share state first and, on any difference (including a revocation elsewhere), refreshes the dialog and refuses that copy; window focus re-checks too (event-driven, never polled). This closes the button path only — the link sits in a selectable readonly input, so a manual copy cannot be intercepted; an unreadable state counts as changed. It deliberately leaves `shareStateError` alone, since publishing is safe on its own (an explicit `expected_through_id` is pinned or 409'd by the advance-only store) and only the stale-claim copy is dangerous. The check is three-valued (`same`/`changed`/`unavailable`), not boolean: both non-`same` outcomes refuse the copy, but an unreadable state changed nothing on screen, so reporting it as "the range changed and the text was refreshed" would invent a change that never happened. (3) That branch's disclosure must be computed over the **full** turns rather than the truncated batch, or it under-reports the images and Memory excerpts the live link actually exposes (the disclosure describes what is in the link, not which answer was clicked). An unresolvable boundary degrades to the no-numbers fallback that still names both images and Memory, and stays publishable. The pure logic lives in `conversation-share-disclosure.ts::resolveShareBoundary`.
- Public URL source import is an outbound-network security boundary: only public `http/https` targets are allowed; validate DNS addresses on the initial request and every redirect, reject userinfo, localhost, private/link-local/reserved ranges, and keep response-size/time limits. Internal documents use file upload.
- The beta is multi-account with owner isolation and link sharing, but it does not provide live co-editing.
- User Memory is manual opt-in and creator-private. Every Memory is bound to exactly one notebook; there is no orphan/global row (the global Memory page is only an owner-scoped aggregate). Ask answers use preview → user edit → confirm; an unconfigured/failing preview model must use the deterministic question-title/cleaned-answer fallback. The notebook workspace tab order is `问答 (Ask) | 知识库 (Knowledge) | 记忆 (Memory) | 深度报告 (Deep Report)`, notebook summaries batch-count the current user's Memory, and the collection-level Memory page provides the cross-notebook view.
- Memory has `candidate/confirmed/rejected/deprecated` lifecycle states. External Agents may create only `candidate`. Same-user, same-notebook authorized Agent profiles may share candidate recall when their token has `memory:read_candidates`; other users and notebooks must never see it. Candidate is excluded from formal notebook Ask, notebook search, Deep Report, and `search_notebook_context` until the user confirms it; rejected/deprecated are excluded from both retrieval planes. Apply relevance before authority, then use `candidate < personal source < confirmed Memory < base KG/base source` only for exact-score/conflict handling.
- Memory inputs share one fail-closed validation policy across Pydantic/API, service/internal, and MCP paths. Trim title/content and reject blank values. Enforce title 80 chars, content 40,000 chars, at most 20 raw tags of 80 chars each, reason 1,000 chars, task-context serialized UTF-8 size 8,192 bytes, at most 50 evidence refs with a 32,768-byte serialized UTF-8 cap, and client request id 200 chars. Tag count is checked before trim/dedup, and any blank tag is rejected. Reject nested NaN/positive or negative infinity before persistence, serialize Memory-owned JSON with `allow_nan=False`, and preserve legitimate JSON null. API violations return 422; service callers must not bypass the caps, and the MCP proposal envelope must not narrow them with adapter-only limits. Keep frontend constraints and messages aligned with these constants. JSON safety/serialization helpers belong in neutral core/domain code; stores must not import service-layer input modules.
- Candidate provenance snapshots the creating Agent profile id/name and every submitted evidence ref, never the bearer token. Validate each ref against the candidate owner/notebook and persist a per-ref validated/invalid result with a bounded reason. Legacy unverified and invalid refs remain owner-visible but are never trusted or promotion-eligible; candidate detail, review, and provenance API/UI remain owner-only.
- The global Memory page's owner total and pending counts are independent of status/search/notebook filters, and it provides an owner-scoped notebook filter. Produce these aggregates with a bounded constant-query store method; do not add per-notebook/N+1 queries.
- Agent access is managed from the global Memory page through owner-private profiles and opaque, one-time plaintext tokens with scopes, expiry, default notebook, notebook allowlist, and immediate revocation. The only scopes are `knowledge:read`, `memory:read`, `memory:read_candidates`, `memory:propose`, `ask:execute`, `knowhow:code`, `sources:write`, `sources:delete`, `maintenance:execute`, `agent_profile:read`, and `agent_observation:write`. The scope vocabulary is mirrored in `AGENT_SCOPES` (backend `memory_service.py`) and `AGENT_SCOPE_OPTIONS` (frontend `agent-token-model.ts`); keep the two in sync when adding a scope. MCP is Streamable HTTP at `/mcp`: loopback HTTP is local-only, remote transport defaults to allowing plain HTTP (trusted-intranet default: relaxed Host/Origin checks and a startup warning) and can be hardened to enforce HTTPS plus DNS-rebinding protection by setting `MCP_REQUIRE_HTTPS=1`, every new session must call `select_notebook`, and every data tool revalidates the live token, scope, allowlist, selected notebook, and notebook access.
- The issued-token receipt exposes anonymous machine-readable `GET /api/agent-mcp/onboarding`. Its Markdown prints `MCP_PUBLIC_URL` verbatim as the endpoint to configure — never a rewritten variant, because that value is the deployment's published contract and a proxy may route only that exact path — while stating that a backend-direct `POST /mcp` is a 307 to `/mcp/` so a client that cannot follow it has the remedy, derives the tool list from that server instance's frozen catalog (`mcp_server.PUBLIC_TOOLS` is the same default frozen combined catalog), remains reachable during startup warm-up, and must never contain, accept, or reflect a bearer token. Reject any query string or Authorization header on this route. Because `MCP_PUBLIC_URL` now crosses into an anonymous Agent instruction plane, startup accepts only an absolute `http(s)` URL with path exactly `/mcp`, no whitespace/control/backtick, userinfo, query, or fragment. The user gives this link and the one-time token to the Agent separately. Codex `bearer_token_env_var` persists only the variable name: an Agent subprocess cannot export into its running parent's environment or hot-load tools, `codex mcp list` proves configuration presence only, and onboarding must report the remaining secure credential/restart action instead of claiming success until a new session completes `list_notebooks` plus `select_notebook`. The same credential rule binds the Claude Code instructions: the rendered `claude mcp add` command carries a single-quoted `${SILICON_NOTEBOOK_AGENT_TOKEN}` header, which Claude Code resolves at connect time from the launching process's environment, so the raw `<AGENT_TOKEN>` form is offered only as the fallback for clients that cannot interpolate. An undefined variable is sent verbatim and fails as a bad token with no configuration-time error, which is another reason success may not be claimed before a restarted session actually completes those two calls.
- Agent token expiry must include an explicit timezone offset. The browser converts `datetime-local` to an offset-aware UTC ISO instant; the backend rejects naive datetimes and normalizes aware values to UTC. Never attach the server timezone to a naive value.
- Saving an Ask answer to Memory must revalidate live notebook owner/member access inside the same `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction as the answer snapshot, Memory row, initial revision, and provenance write. Preserve owner and read-only-member save semantics and leave no partial row if access is concurrently revoked.
- The core prefix of the default MCP tool contract is these 23 tools; `mcp_server.CORE_TOOLS` derives that prefix, while `mcp_server.PUBLIC_TOOLS` derives the full default frozen catalog: the seven Memory/context tools `list_notebooks`, `select_notebook`, `search_agent_memory`, `search_notebook_context`, `get_memory`, `ask_notebook`, and `propose_memory`, plus the four knowhow tools `list_knowhow_tables`, `get_knowhow_discrimination`, `get_knowhow_row`, and `put_knowhow_cell_code`, the citation point-read `get_cited_element`, the six source-management tools `add_source_text`, `add_source_file`, `add_source_url`, `get_source_status`, `reparse_source`, and `delete_source`, the three build tools `build_kg`, `build_retrieval_index`, and `get_build_status`, and the two notebook-understanding tools `get_notebook_profile` and `add_observation` (Agentic Memory P3). `add_source_file` sends exact file bytes as strict standard base64 and derives format admission from the backend parser registry, so PDF/PPTX/DOCX/workbook/Markdown-ZIP support cannot drift from the browser upload surface. The deployed server-local catalog may append only explicitly trusted provider tools and drives live discovery/onboarding. Do not add candidate-confirm/reject/deprecate/promote tools. Treat all returned source/KG/Memory text as untrusted evidence, never Agent instructions. Keep the official-client offline smoke `scripts/smoke_memory_mcp.py` in `scripts/check.sh`, honoring `PYTHON_BIN`.
- **Long-running MCP tools must heartbeat, and the transport must stay SSE.** An MCP client does not wait indefinitely for a tool call — Claude Code applies an *idle* timeout (no response and no progress notification for N seconds) and other clients a flat per-call ceiling — while `ask_notebook` in `reasoning` mode routinely runs for minutes, so the client used to abandon calls the server was still completing successfully. Every one of the 23 tools therefore runs its one principal blocking call through `mcp_server._run_with_progress`, which emits an MCP progress notification every `PROGRESS_HEARTBEAT_SECONDS` carrying **only** the tool name and elapsed wall-clock seconds — never the question, a notebook/source name, or any notebook content, the same rule the telemetry events follow. One wrapped call per tool is the invariant: it keeps progress values monotonic within a request without any per-request counter, and `report_progress` is a no-op unless the client sent a `progressToken`, so a client that does not want progress pays nothing. Two traps are load-bearing and both have mutation-verified guards in `backend/tests/test_memory_mcp.py`: (1) FastMCP must be constructed with `json_response=False` — in JSON mode the SDK drains the per-request stream looking for the response and **discards every notification on the way**, so `report_progress` becomes an expensive no-op and the heartbeat is silently useless (measured: four beats reach the official client in SSE mode, zero in JSON mode); (2) the work must be captured in a child task and re-raised after the task group closes, because anyio 4 wraps a body exception in an `ExceptionGroup` and FastMCP turns whatever leaves a tool into the Agent-visible error text — the naive form reports every tool failure as `unhandled errors in a TaskGroup` instead of the sentence the tool wrote. The one user-visible cost of SSE mode is that a `POST /mcp/` must `Accept: application/json, text/event-stream` or get a 406; the Streamable HTTP spec requires clients to send both, and the SDK client, the tests and the SOP's curl all do. The heartbeat is not a timeout of ours — nothing here gives up on running work; the client's ceiling stays the client's to configure and the SOP documents the knobs. The interval value and the client/proxy guidance live in `docs/product-and-api.md` / `_zh.md` and `docs/agent-mcp-memory-sop.md` / `_zh.md`.
- The Agent source-management surface is deliberately narrower than the browser's. Writes (`add_source_text`, `add_source_url`, `reparse_source` on `sources:write`; `build_kg`, `build_retrieval_index` on `maintenance:execute`) are **owner-only**: a token's allowlist may name a notebook whose owner merely joined it as a read-only member, and those calls must be refused there. **As of P2 this owner gate deliberately diverges from the browser's HTTP write surface**, which flipped its six content-write capabilities (sources/kg/knowhow/knowledge/catalog writes and `notebook:manage`) to `user_can_admin_notebook` (owner ∪ an effective `role='admin'` grant edge) so a group admin now writes through the browser — the Agent token, a separate longer-lived credential whose owner may have been granted admin long after issuance, does **not** follow, and `notebook:delete` stayed owner-only on both surfaces (`deps._CAPABILITY_LEVELS` and `mcp_server._writable_notebook` both record this split). `delete_source` additionally requires `sources:delete` — which `sources:write` never implies — **and** that an Agent added the row: the criterion is the v48 `sources.agent_profile_id` provenance column being non-NULL ("some Agent added this", not "this profile did", so a rotated-out profile cannot strand its uploads), evaluated on the same row that proved notebook membership. Provenance is written on the INSERT branch only, so content-hash dedupe reusing a person's row leaves it NULL and a notebook deep copy clears it outright — re-uploading a user's bytes can never launder them into an Agent-deletable source. The projection defaults to false when the column is absent, so the gate fails closed. Reads (`get_source_status`, `get_build_status`, `get_cited_element`) stay on `knowledge:read` and member-readable access. This owner gate deliberately does **not** extend to the `knowhow:code` write surface (`put_knowhow_cell_code` and the mirrored HTTP `PUT`/`DELETE .../code`): design doc §⑥-4 keeps an Agent's authority there entirely scope-driven with no second owner axis — a cell code attachment is inert (never executed, indexed, embedded, or projected), while deleting or re-parsing a document reaches every member's retrieval. Two authority models coexisting is a recorded decision, not drift; `backend/tests/test_memory_mcp.py` pins both sides (`test_source_writes_are_refused_in_a_read_only_shared_notebook` / `test_knowhow_code_write_is_allowed_in_a_read_only_shared_notebook`). The user-facing contract, response shapes, and numeric ceilings live in `docs/product-and-api.md` / `_zh.md`.
- Only confirmed Memory can enter creator-proposed Memory→KG promotion. Each proposal pins its exact source revision, sanitized extraction candidates and server-validated evidence; the admin queue renders every typed candidate field and all pinned evidence, never raw Memory provenance/task context. Editing or deprecating a proposed Memory atomically supersedes the active queue item and resets `promotion_state` to `none`; edits can then be proposed again. Superseded current provenance must be pointer-free (no active `proposal_id`); the pinned proposal id/revision remains only in `kg_promotion_snapshots` and queue history. Approval revalidates current confirmed status and creator access, and must also validate the pinned revision/snapshot, notebook binding, and current proposed state inside the same write transaction before any Base mutation, then reuse existing dedupe/merge to create or merge one or more Base KG objects. Approve/reject routes record the authenticated admin reviewer, and the API/audit result must retain the complete `base_object_ids`. It does not change the private Memory's owner/tier. Notebook deletion cascades all members' bound private Memory, and the UI warning must state that fact without revealing member identities, content, or counts.
- Knowhow tables are structured domain-experience grids: free-form column names, rich-Markdown cells, at most one table-level row-title column. Their projection is the only zero-LLM KG writer: with a row-title column set, every non-empty cell becomes a knowledge object whose `object_type` is its column name, linked by the existing `about` relation to the row-title node, and identical short values in the same column merge into one node; without a row-title column the table is retrieval-only (cells become chunks, no graph nodes). Column content kinds (procedure / entity / attribute) are deterministic parsing hints, never an LLM call. New-table import explicitly accepts `orientation=columns|rows` (`columns` default); `rows` input is transposed after raw xlsx/csv/Markdown extraction and before preview/validation, while persisted grids, append import, retrieval, and projection stay column-oriented. Preview and commit must use the same request orientation, and row-oriented input defaults the normalized first column as the row-title suggestion without making that choice mandatory. Import validation errors that reach the UI must come only from typed safe exceptions and `user_error`: merged record-group headings in an otherwise row-oriented workbook should suggest `属性按行`, while duplicate/blank headers, unsupported files/encodings, stale mappings, and other correctable input errors must state an actionable fix; unrelated `ValueError` text remains diagnostic-only. Every mutation path (cell edit, import, append, reproject, deep-copy publish) converges on the per-table debounced single-flight `ProjectionScheduler` running through `background_jobs`. Projection status is a table-completion contract: rows stay `pending`/`syncing` until chunks, embeddings, the terminal table-wide knowledge-object/relation transaction, mutation sequence, and graph-cache notifications finish; publish `synced` only afterward, never before the KG is readable. Every status publication must conditionally match the table mutation sequence captured by that pass, so a stale pass cannot overwrite a newer edit's `pending` marker while the scheduler rerun is queued. Cell code attachments are stored per cell but never executed, indexed, embedded, FTS'd, projected into the KG, or included in Ask context (an isolation invariant pinned by tests); their `implemented`/`stale` freshness derives from a hash of the cell's image-stripped text. The LLM wording optimizer is an explicit per-cell button with a side-by-side confirm; it must never run automatically. The cell editor opens single-column by default with an edit / side-by-side / preview view toggle remembered per session and orthogonal to fullscreen (neither is ever auto-entered); every path that leaves a cell holding unsaved edits — close, Esc, backdrop, Cancel, switching to a sibling cell, and the exit a completed save performs itself — synchronously persists a restorable local draft first and refuses to leave if that write fails (a second attempt forces through, so a browser with unusable storage cannot trap the user), which matters because leaving unmounts the editor and cancels its debounced autosave, while a save or upload in flight blocks starting another save and any of save/upload/optimize blocks a sibling switch — an optimize deliberately does not block saving, since that request is uncapped and must never lock the user out of persisting text — and a completed request that outlived its cell must not act on whatever cell was opened next. The external-Agent surface (REST `/api/agent/knowhow/*` plus the four knowhow MCP tools) shares one service core with the session routes behind dual auth — reads need `knowledge:read`, code writes need `knowhow:code`, and cross-owner probes get a uniform 404 with no existence oracle. See `README.md` § Knowhow tables and `architecture.md` § 3.7 for the full contract.
- `KNOWHOW_KG_NODE_RETRIEVAL_ENABLED` defaults true: projected cell objects join reasoning/graph KG-node retrieval and preserve row-drawer citation refs; setting it false rolls back only that direct node path, while cell chunks remain searchable. Default-on widening must be scoped through `knowhow_tables.hidden_source_id`, never every custom Schema type, and its type tuple plus chunk-vector→KO bridge stay version-cached/single-flight and are evicted with KG mutations.
- **Knowhow row completion is suggestion-only and uses two evidence channels.** The explicit physical-row action is available from a record row's detail drawer and from each branch in an anchor-group concept matrix. Only a missing cell or exact empty string is eligible; whitespace-only stored content is not silently treated as empty. One request covers every target column and combines at most eight same-table reference rows (same anchor/row-title group first, then known-column similarity and coverage) with one bounded `ReasoningRetriever` run over the active notebook plus its currently valid explicit base mounts.
