This uses the Ask `reasoning` plan → federated retrieve → reflect/expand/follow-chain family, but the completion profile filters private Memory and the current table's own projection before candidates reach model reflection, and disables provenance-opaque PPR/community expansion as well as the exact-lookup identifier channel (same `allow_*` policy-flag mechanism — the completion query is a JSON envelope, not natural language, and would otherwise make the exact-lookup seed pass unconditionally probe the envelope's own field names); it must never call `ask_reasoning`/`ask_answer`, create a conversation/job, or persist an Ask answer. The completion response carries the final reasoning trace and server-issued library-evidence keys; the synthesis model may cite only those keys or the allowed table-row ids, and a suggestion without any valid citation must become an abstention. The current table's projected KG/chunk/element evidence must be filtered so the same-table channel cannot count twice or circularly support itself, and base/personal contradictions defer to base while disclosing the difference. `reasoning_agent` performs retrieval and `knowhow_complete` performs the final structured synthesis; both use system-level untrusted-evidence instructions. Invalid reasoning responses, unavailable providers, retrieval failures, and unparseable or unusable top-level synthesis responses fail explicitly; malformed individual suggestions are filtered, downgraded, or converted to abstentions. No failure may silently revert to table-only or fabricated offline output. The draggable review dialog shows table references and inert library-evidence Markdown (no links/images) separately and requires per-item human acceptance. Acceptance must use the ordinary cell PATCH with `expected_before=""` and `origin="llm_complete"`, so concurrent fills return a conflict rather than being overwritten and normal history/projection semantics remain intact.
- Every knowhow table carries a full change history (`knowhow_changes` + `knowhow_milestones`, schema v26). Every write-transaction method appends one change row via the module-level `record_change` as the **last** step of its write transaction, storing the affected entities' before/after plus the **post-change whole-table fingerprint** (reusing the transfer guard's `_FINGERPRINT_SQL`, covering table meta / columns / rows / cells / cell code and deliberately excluding timestamps); an architecture guard (`backend/tests/test_knowhow_history_coverage_guard.py`) parses **both** the SQLite and the PostgreSQL `KnowhowStore` and fails any un-allowlisted write transaction, so a future write path cannot silently skip hooking on either backend, and a store file that moves out from under the guard fails loudly instead of shrinking coverage to nothing. The guard requires the `record_change` call to sit **inside** the write transaction's `with` body, not merely somewhere in the method, so moving the call out past the commit — which silently breaks the atomicity of "data change and history row land together" — fails it. What the guard deliberately does not pin is the call's position *within* that body: the "last step" half of the contract stays a code-review obligation, because an AST position check can only approximate it and would red-flag legitimate trailing statements. Revert is pure delta reverse-replay inside one write transaction: verify the current fingerprint equals the head change's fingerprint (else abort), replay each change's `before` from head down to the target (rows/columns **reuse their original id** so citation jumps and code attachments survive), verify the resulting fingerprint equals the target's (else roll back the whole transaction), then append a `revert` change — history is append-only, so "revert of a revert" is inherent. Single-cell restore is an ordinary save (`origin="revert"`) that keeps the merged-cell fan-out invariant, not the whole-table revert path. Milestones are zero-snapshot names on a seq; pruning history deletes only the oldest contiguous prefix (by seq, not `created_at`, so clock skew cannot punch holes) and always keeps the head row. The orphan-image sweeper's live-reference set extends to change-log payloads, so an image that ever entered a cell is essentially never auto-collected — the cost being that "prune history" only frees it once the last reference no longer sits on the retained head row. Revert triggers a full reprojection through the same `ProjectionScheduler`. See `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-22-knowhow-table-version-control-design.md`.
- Knowhow cells normalize Excel-style formatting (Tab-indented `•` bullets, `A.`/`a.` section/sub markers, soft line breaks) into clean CommonMark. New imports and appends run the deterministic, zero-LLM `rule_normalize` inline behind a conservative allow-list gate: the whole cell is left byte-identical unless *every* line is plain prose or a list marker, so fenced code, tables, hard line breaks, and any inline construct spanning a soft newline (math `$…$`, inline code, `*`/`_` emphasis, `[` link/image text) pass through untouched (fail-closed on unknown structure). The anchor (row-title) column is exempt on every bulk path (import / append / backfill) because it is a grouping key that must stay byte-stable. The per-cell editor "reformat" button (`POST …/cells/{col}/reformat`, `reformat_cell`) and the batch "reformat row/table" action layer an optional per-user LLM rewrite on top, gated by a zero-LLM `content_invariant` check that rejects any candidate changing content rather than only formatting — alphanumerics/CJK and every Unicode Symbol category (`S*`) count as content, only whitespace and Punctuation (`P*`) are relaxed — and falls back to the rules; reformat never runs automatically and always presents a before/after confirm. Existing (存量) cells are backfilled by `scripts/backfill_knowhow_md.py`, dry-run by default and fully read-only (opens the DB `mode=ro`, constructs no write-capable repository, safe on a live backend) and following a plan handshake: the dry-run always writes a reviewed plan file and every `--apply` **requires** `--plan` (it re-applies that reviewed file verbatim, skipping any cell edited since review). See `README.md` § Backfilling knowhow-cell Markdown formatting.
- This guarded concurrency contract applies only to an interactive row/table reformat batch's save unit, never to ordinary shared-cell edits or ordinary APIs. The batch freezes one complete-table snapshot, including an exact member set only for each non-empty anchor group covered by a complete anchor-group save unit (a merged shared-column fan-out or a singleton complete group). In one SQLite write transaction, it revalidates every write target's expected content baseline, the current anchor designation, and exact membership only for those covered frozen groups. Membership uses the v21 `(column_id, JS-trim(content_md), row_id)` expression index with an equality query built from the same ECMAScript trim code points as the frontend, so it remains fail-closed without an O(R) anchor-column scan per save unit. A non-shared column in a multi-row anchor group is a valid subset write: it checks only its write-target baselines, not the whole-group membership guard. Any applicable content, anchor, or membership drift rejects the entire save unit with HTTP 409 and zero partial writes. The UI retains generated reformat candidates as stale, requires a rerun, and defers the parent-table reload until the batch modal closes.
- Interactive row/table reformat batches bound only candidate generation: use at most `min(3, live knowhow_reformat service capacity)` concurrent physical requests (fallback 2), single-flight equal `(column_id, trimmed original Markdown)` inputs, cache/reuse only successful still-fresh results, propagate cancellation, ignore late epochs, and count progress by physical cell. Saving remains serial and preserves the frozen snapshot and every guard above. Changed/saved entries expose a bounded raw-Markdown line/token diff inside the same dialog, with an oversized-content fallback and optional rendered preview; if stale was observed, opening a saved entry must close the dialog, await the guarded parent-detail reload, then recompute and open the stable physical representative (smallest row position then id)—never flash an old target. Reload/epoch failure or a missing row/column must open nothing and surface the existing recoverable table action error. The overall confirm never becomes per-item accept/reject. The fixed-layout grid derives `colgroup` widths from a memoized bounded visible-row sample, applies a fixed code-unit prefix before any Markdown regex/split or grapheme work, retains horizontal scroll/sticky first column, and does not add resize persistence. Stable user ids remain ownership/permission identities; ordinary session audit snapshots use trimmed username, then display name, then id, while Agent snapshots use profile name. Copy/import/transfer APIs must pass identity ids and audit labels separately. Resolve recognizable legacy user-id audit labels in bounded bulk reads with no N+1 or destructive rewrite; synchronous identity projection from async routes must stay in a threadpool. For an `origin=agent` change, resolve legacy human updater ids only inside semantic `payload.before`; preserve the Agent actor and `after`/`current` updater as free text. `knowhow_cell_code.updated_by` is fingerprinted and must never be rewritten only for display, and single-cell code GET/PUT responses must carry its readable label so the UI keeps attribution immediately after save.
- No demo/seed notebook is created. A fresh database seeds only the built-in account; the notebook collection starts empty and is populated entirely by real imported sources. Do not reintroduce synthetic notebooks or sources. New-notebook prompts/placeholders must derive from the notebook's actual domain and sources.
- PDF parsing is decoupled from the GPU via a MinerU adapter (`mineru_client.py`): `MINERU_MODE=http` calls a remote `mineru-api` service, `cli` runs MinerU's Python API (`do_parse/read_fn`) in an isolated subprocess, and `off` (default) uses the local PyMuPDF4LLM layout/Markdown fallback; pypdf is only the last-resort dependency/parser-error fallback. The FastAPI backend process must never import torch/MinerU directly. The self-hosted HTTP adapter and mineru.net cloud adapter share `MINERU_MAX_RETRIES` (extra attempts, `0..5`, default `2`, hence three total attempts) and retry only transient transport/timeout failures, HTTP 408/425/429/5xx, and empty/non-JSON responses with bounded backoff; explicit 4xx responses and terminal parse/business failures remain fail-fast inside the adapter, and local CLI parsing is not retried. After a cloud URL/file call finally fails or maps to zero elements, ingestion must use the local Python fallback for that format, persist a stable safe warning bit while retaining raw diagnostics only in pipeline logs/source `error_message`, and let a later successful reparse clear both. The frontend must show that fallback-quality warning with owner reparse/delete actions. `parsers.MINERU_CAPABLE_SUFFIXES` (`.pdf`, `.docx`, `.pptx`, `.xlsx`, `.xlsm`) is the single source of truth for "MinerU is worth trying": each of those suffixes has a MinerU-first branch that degrades through its own staged local chain (PDF: PyMuPDF4LLM → pypdf; DOCX: mammoth semantic HTML → python-docx; PPTX: python-pptx → raw slide XML; XLSX/XLSM: openpyxl), and the cloud-upload branch only fires for that set — uploading `.md`/`.csv`/text to mineru.net exposes user content to a third party that cannot parse it and burns a retry budget for nothing. The degradation warning is gated on the deliberately narrower `parsers.MINERU_FALLBACK_WARNING_SUFFIXES` (`.pdf`, `.docx`, `.pptx`): docx/pptx degradations used to be silent and must not be, but the openpyxl workbook fallback is cell-value faithful, so warning there is noise — and on a deployment whose MinerU cannot parse workbooks it would be a permanently lit warning no reparse can clear. Every MinerU element label/`source_format` prefix comes from `parsers.mineru_label_prefix(file_name)`, including the cloud branches; the old hardcoded default made a cloud-only `.xlsx` upload report `PDF p.1 …`. A non-empty MinerU workbook result is accepted only after a deterministic, model-free, network-free **row *and cell*** coverage reconciliation (`MINERU_WORKBOOK_MIN_ROW_COVERAGE`, protocol boundary, one threshold shared by both dimensions): MinerU renders workbooks to pages, so print areas/column widths can drop whole columns or sheets without raising, and accepting a truncated result is silent data loss that not even the fallback warning would reveal. Both dimensions are required — counting `<tr>` alone is blind to the common wide-table failure where every row survives but the columns past the rendered page width do not; non-table text elements contribute at most one row and one cell each (bounded inflation for MinerU's sheet titles/stray cells), and image blocks contribute to neither numerator because they correspond to no physical row or cell. Under-covering results on either dimension are discarded in full for openpyxl (cell-value faithful, so rejecting MinerU loses table HTML structure, never data); an unreadable/absent openpyxl is fail-open. The reconciliation is not local-parser-only: the cloud-upload **file** branch maps and reconciles through the same public `parsers.mineru_workbook_output_accepted` entry point for `parsers.MINERU_WORKBOOK_SUFFIXES`, since cloud-only deployments never reach `parse_xlsx` and would otherwise bypass the guardrail entirely (the URL branch stays PDF-semantic and is untouched). Mapping runs in two stages on both paths — a probe mapping **without** `persist_image` feeds the reconciliation, and only an accepted result is re-mapped with images — because `mineru_content_list_to_elements` persists image assets as a side effect, so a single-stage mapping would leave asset rows and files on disk for a result whose elements are then discarded. The stable warning prefix constant `PDF_PYTHON_FALLBACK_WARNING_PREFIX` keeps its name and value regardless: four storage layers, shadow parity, and already-persisted rows all prefix-match on it. Formulas are preserved as LaTeX (`formula` elements), tables as HTML in metadata (`table` elements). Legacy binary `.xls` is parsed locally via `xlrd` (no MinerU branch, MinerU cannot read that container); `.doc`/`.ppt` remain rejected with a save-as prompt.
- Markdown sources ingest embedded images through the same guardrails as MinerU-parsed documents, not a separate code path. `![alt](src)` with alt text writes the alt string into the element's `metadata.caption` — the same field MinerU-parsed image captions populate — so it is chunked and searchable on equal footing; an alt-less plain-path image still yields no element **unless** an image-description block follows it (see below). When `src` is a `data:image/{png,jpeg,gif,webp};base64,...` URI (`app/services/knowhow/assets.py::ALLOWED_MIME_EXTENSIONS`, the same mime allowlist MinerU image assets use), the bytes are decoded and persisted through the same per-source `persist_image` closure (`app/services/source_image_persist.py`) MinerU/DOCX/PPTX parsing already uses, so `MINERU_RETURN_IMAGES`/`MINERU_MAX_IMAGE_BYTES`/`MINERU_MAX_IMAGES_PER_SOURCE` gate it identically — that switch and those ceilings now cover image persistence for every source type, not only MinerU output. The data-URI payload must never reach element `text`/`metadata` or a KG extraction window: `parse_elements` (`app/services/kg/parsing.py`) uses the caption text, not the raw markdown slice, for an image block, and shrinks the block's char span to the caption's length before windowing, so a large embedded image cannot get sliced into repeated extraction windows over its own base64. A data-URI image with no alt that fails to persist (missing/oversized/disallowed mime/decode failure) produces no element unless an image-description block follows it, mirroring the alt-less plain-path case. A blockquote that immediately follows an image line (blank line optional — a blockquote interrupts a paragraph) and whose first line is nothing but the `**图片描述**` marker is that image's **description block**: every quoted line in it folds into the image element's `metadata.description` and joins the caption to form the element text, so a described image is retrievable even with no alt caption (`chunking.py` skips an image/figure only when caption **and** description are both empty; both chunk-store adapters project the new key, and `_citation_image` falls back to the description for the attached-image caption). The blockquote no longer also becomes its own paragraph element — `parse_blocks` emits a dedicated `image_description` block that `parsers.py` skips and `kg/parsing.py` maps to `paragraph`, so KG windows still see its verbatim source slice exactly as before. Fold only happens for a blockquote free of opaque blocks (fence/indented code/HTML — their content hangs off `token.content` with no inline children, so folding them would silently drop it; lists/headings/tables/nested quotes are folded, since the convention is "**every** quoted line") that holds text still rendering non-blank after the marker, with nothing between it and the image **in the source** (a link reference definition emits no token at all, so a token-only adjacency test would mint an empty orphan image block); the client mirror is a receipt-only heuristic with no markdown parser of its own, so it is pinned by **direction** rather than byte-equality: it must scan the **whole** blockquote — stopping at the first content line calls "lead-in sentence, then a fence" described while the server refuses the block, leaving an image that truly cannot be retrieved and a receipt that says nothing — and must allow 0–3 leading spaces before a fence or HTML block, which CommonMark still recognizes. A multi-paragraph description folds into **one** element and is therefore no longer split by the 600-char chunker: a very long description is embedded only up to `embed_truncate_chars` (registered trade-off); the marker line is judged against the **raw source line** (markdown-it strips a `- `/`# ` prefix off `inline.content`, so `> - 图片描述` would otherwise read as the marker and swallow an ordinary quoted list) and must hold nothing but the marker (with its `\r` stripped, so a CRLF document folds exactly like an LF one — the client's `scanLines` already drops CR, and not matching it server-side would make the client the *wider* of the two) (bold/trailing colon optional; trailing text requires a colon), so an ordinary quote opening with 「图片描述如下：……」 is untouched. Known boundary: only an image occupying an entire paragraph is recognized this way; one embedded inside a list item or table cell keeps only its alt text and is never persisted as an asset. `scripts/embed_md_images.py` pre-converts a markdown file's local image references into self-contained data URIs for upload.
- `.zip` is a first-class backend parser-registry format for Markdown bundles, not a browser-only interchange format. The raw archive is stored as one source and the background built-in `markdown_bundle` parser reads every `.md`/`.markdown` member in stable path order, records `bundle_path`, resolves local image references relative to each owning Markdown member (including `./`, `../`, root-relative archive paths, URL encoding, and query/fragment suffixes), magic-sniffs png/jpeg/gif/webp, and persists accepted bytes through the ordinary source-asset port. It rejects unsafe/duplicate paths, encrypted or unsupported compression, missing Markdown, excess retained entries, and an uncompressed aggregate over the ordinary per-source byte ceiling; it never extracts files to the host filesystem. A missing, remote, corrupt, or unsupported image fails open to the existing caption/description-only image element. Reparse remains under the same per-source lock and accepted asset-generation replacement path. A dropped folder still uses the dependency-free browser pipeline (`frontend/app/md-bundle.ts` / `bundle-intake.ts`) to pair paths and inline data URIs before upload, with its existing persistent receipt and deliberately narrower whole-paragraph/image-description mirror. `GET /system/config` continues to provide the image and upload rails for that folder preflight. Exact ZIP and folder guardrails live only in `docs/product-and-api.md` / `_zh.md`.
- On Apple Silicon (no NVIDIA, but MLX-capable) you can get high-fidelity parsing locally/offline: `pip install "mineru[core]"`, `mineru-models-download -m vlm`, then set `MINERU_MODE=cli` + `MINERU_BACKEND=vlm-auto-engine` in the local `.env` (gitignored). Use `MINERU_PARSE_METHOD=txt|ocr|auto` and `MINERU_LANG=en|ch|...` when you need to match a manual MinerU run; use a longer `MINERU_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` such as `1800` for full papers because local VLM can exceed 10 minutes. Keep `.env.example` default `off`. `check.sh`/smoke must stay offline and never require MinerU or model weights.

## Architecture Baseline

- Core tool exceptions must be mapped to stable public codes before FastMCP sees them; no internal exception text may enter the Agent response. Result admission is bounded while recursively copying, so an oversized string/list/map is rejected before an unbounded second object graph can be built.

The external-Agent MCP has one API-owned registration host and one startup-frozen catalog: the seven fixed core registrars captured as the exact 22-tool surface. Core handlers keep their existing validation/auth/I/O order and one progress-wrapped main body. Scopes must exist in the canonical backend/frontend vocabulary, and the core scope-policy table forces every write scope through the owner-only notebook gate. Registration/listing is zero repository/model work and every invocation revalidates live token/scope/allowlist/membership. `mcp_server.PUBLIC_TOOLS`, static documentation/smoke guards, production discovery, and onboarding all derive from that one live catalog; `CORE_TOOLS` is the same list under its structural name. There is no second, hand-maintained copy. The former external `agent.tool_provider` descriptor-append half had no consumer and has been removed.

Deep Report's authenticated backend batch export has one startup-frozen `report.exporter` Provider. Repository SQL first applies notebook, creator, completed-status, and non-empty-content authority and releases its connection; only then may the selected provider receive immutable opaque export views. The default topology contains exactly the built-in Markdown provider, with no host fallback or second backend formatter. Provider output is admitted as one exact ordered batch; core owns filename sanitization, duplicate suffixes, MIME/format policy, and the ZIP envelope. The browser's single-report local Markdown Blob is an already-authorized presentation path and remains outside this backend seat. A new user-visible export format must add frontend/API parity in the same change.

- The modular extension foundation and Phase-1 retrieval host contract are active. Stable cross-layer values belong in `backend/app/domain`; that package must not import services, repositories, API, infrastructure, extensions, or feature implementations. `backend/app/repositories/ports.py` must not import `app.services.*`, and the backend static import graph must remain acyclic. All existing repository-to-service imports are explicit debt ceilings, partitioned as SQLite/PostgreSQL/other, that may fall but not grow. `backend/app/extension_sdk` owns dependency-light manifest, contribution-kind (Provider / ProviderChain / Contributor / Observer), point-specific retrieval context/result/budget/cancellation/provenance, and sanitized failure contracts; capability `requires` are distinct from plugin `depends_on`, and contributions default to stable ID order rather than integer priority. `backend/app/extensions` owns the startup-frozen registry, frozen capability-decision catalog, and shared `RetrievalContributorHost`; `app.bootstrap` is the sole outer composition root that joins them to repository adapters. Workflows receive the stable `app.domain.extensions.RetrievalContributorHostPort`, never a registry/SDK service locator. Required capability names must have a decision entry at freeze, but availability is evaluated live on every consumption and never frozen. Plugin availability receives only I/O-free metadata and cancellation; execution context is built only after at least one contribution is available, and each plugin receives only the ports both declared in its manifest and currently available. One unavailable capability disables only its contribution: workflows must still enter the shared extension point so independent contributors keep their output. Invocation routing and core-owned admission policy are snapshotted at startup. With no applicable contributor the host returns the exact baseline object before context, cancellation/availability, clocks, events, fan-out slots, or I/O. Candidate proposals are capped before one core-owned batch hydrate; plugin values are never authoritative, malformed fields fail open, and no per-hit N+1 is allowed. Point-specific proposal sources and the generic authority reader are separate domain ports: built-in authority resolves from request-local maps, while unresolved proposals use one notebook/source-filtered SQL batch. Atomic admission is mandatory for contributions whose stronger contract requires the whole lane to pass together. Selected-source graph and generated-question recall are the first production built-in contributors on the separate `selected_evidence` and `chunk_candidates` invocations. Each plugin sees only a request-bound access port, never the baseline, query, graph service, settings, model client, repository, matrix, or connection. The graph bridge retains the original activation result so attestation, rollout, scope drift, baseline duplicate-support overlay, independent token budget, baseline-manifest/eviction guard, status, and whole-lane failure stay byte-equivalent; Ask and Report must not call the activation service directly. The generated-question bridge remains before MMR/fusion, stages collision-support overlays on a defensive baseline copy, uses request-memory authority without an extra SQL read, and commits only after host admission. `off`, a satisfied trigger, an empty/overflowing index, or failure returns the exact baseline; `shadow` evaluates but returns the exact baseline; only `on` may append original chunks without eviction or reordering. Its bounded scan applies notebook/source predicates and the retrieval-run actor's private-Memory predicate before `LIMIT`; actor identity is core-only run state and never enters telemetry. Both backends expose an I/O-free request-context connection probe; Host must block before contributor fan-out while a SQLite transaction boundary or PostgreSQL pooled lease is held, and pool-size-1 conformance is mandatory. Core request cancellation propagates through single- and multi-query paths; optional contributor failure/timeout remains fail-open. The architecture guard also forbids plugin implementations from importing concrete repository/facade/runtime implementations. `scripts/check_architecture_boundaries.py` runs in G1; the public `RepositoryFacade`/`SQLiteRepository` surface may shrink but must not gain plugin seats. The same guard also checks `app.core`/`app.models` reverse imports into `app.services`: the allowlist is now empty, so any core/models→services edge is a violation (the two historical edges were each fixed by moving the shared definition down a layer — `app.core.llm`'s cancellation primitives now live in `app.domain.cancellation`, and `app.models.agent_profile`'s `PROFILE_LABEL_ORDER` constant now lives in `app.models.agent_profile` itself, with `app.services.agent_profile_block` importing it forward). The guard also holds a fixed set of hot functions to a zero-slack line-count ceiling (a function may only shrink below its recorded ceiling if the baseline is lowered alongside it), and holds `backend/app/repositories/ports.py`'s total Protocol method count to the same kind of zero-slack, shrink-only ceiling. New extension work follows `docs/modular-plugin-architecture-design-2026-08-21.md`; no migration may begin while an SCC or repository-port reverse dependency exists. Every modular-architecture PR follows the delivery plan: two independent subagent reviews and green CI before squash merge. When a workflow already knows this call cannot supply a point-specific access capability (the feature is unconfigured), it may ask the host's `has_contributions` query -- a read of the startup-frozen snapshot only, touching no request state, clock, I/O, or capability decision -- whether the point is dormant, and declare that capability as `disabled_capabilities` to `run`; a dormant point returns the baseline before any call/context is constructed. Both production lanes (`selected_evidence` and `chunk_candidates`) now do this in their own disabled branch — `selected_evidence_lane_is_dormant` (`source_graph_activation.py`) and its mirror `generated_question_lane_is_dormant` (`generated_question_contribution.py`) are each called from `_activate_selected_source_graph`/`_run_chunk_candidate_contributors` before their respective `*ContributionCall`/`*_call_context` are constructed. Contributions filtered by `disabled_capabilities` emit no unavailable event — an unconfigured feature is a caller fact, not a live decision failure.
- Deployment-owned plugins (`EXTENSIONS_CONFIG`) load only the named, not-`enabled=false` entries of one TOML file — nothing scans a directory, reads entry points, or consults a second environment variable. `trust` is a three-value type (`builtin`/`deployment`/`isolated`), but the startup registry only ever admits `builtin` and `deployment`; `isolated` is reserved for a future process-isolated tier and is rejected today. A bundle may only supply an availability probe for the capability names its own manifest declares in `provides`, and a name colliding with a core `point:name` capability is a startup failure. A plugin's own HTTP routes — its API extensions — mount only under `/api/extensions/{plugin_id}` behind router-level session authentication; **every core port a plugin reaches authorizes the calling user itself** (the mount's own `{notebook_id}` path-shape guard is defence in depth, not the authorization boundary), and a plugin never receives the repository, global `Settings`, a model client, the FastMCP host, or a raw bearer token. Rejection logs carry only a plugin id and a stable reason code, never a settings value, module path, file path, or exception text.
- Generic Report admission reads acquire the shared retrieval leaf gate. Their SQL enforces the actor-owned private-Memory predicate even when a compatibility call has no frozen source scope; visible imported sources and notebook-wide Knowhow remain eligible. Graph request-memory authority never acquires that extra slot.
- `frontend/` is the only frontend path. (The former static `web/` fallback has been removed.)
- `backend/app/api/routes.py` composes the domain FastAPI routers and owns their order, not product endpoint bodies or compatibility exports; `routes.py` is composition-only. New backend endpoints go to the owning domain router, never `routes.py`. Boundary contracts inspect endpoint ownership on each domain router and verify the aggregate composition declaration semantically; never assume `include_router()` flattens child routes, because current FastAPI may retain lazy included-router nodes.
- Domain Pydantic models live in `backend/app/models/`; `backend/app/models/schemas.py` is a legacy compatibility facade. New Pydantic models go to the owning domain module; `schemas.py` is legacy compatibility only.
- `frontend/app/page.tsx` is the workspace orchestrator. Keep shared workspace API/view models in `frontend/app/workspace-model.ts`, answer/citation/reasoning-trace UI in `frontend/app/answer-panel.tsx`, built-in KG labels/styles in `frontend/app/kg-type-model.ts`, and shared KG rendering in `frontend/app/kg-type-mark.tsx`; do not copy those implementations back into `page.tsx`.
- HTTP endpoint ownership is in the six `frontend/app/*-api.ts` domain clients (`system`, `notebook`, `source`, `ask`, `knowledge`, `report`) plus `frontend/features/kg-maintenance/kg-api.ts` for KG maintenance, all using `api-client.ts`. Frontend HTTP mechanics go through `api-client.ts`; product policy remains in domain API modules. Keep page-owned state/effects/epochs/stale guards/polling and component-owned Blob URLs there; `api-boundary.test.mjs` semantically rejects direct production `fetch` outside `api-client.ts`.
- `backend/app/application` owns dependency-light, immutable application-stage envelopes. Its current explicit import allowlist is only `app.application`, `app.core.ask_retrieval_policy`, `app.domain.cancellation`, and `app.models.ask`; new contract modules require an equally explicit guard update rather than opening an entire package whose other modules may depend on services. Bare `import app` and any unaliased `import app.<allowed-module>` are forbidden because both bind the root package and can reach already-loaded implementation attributes; an explicitly aliased allowed submodule remains legal. It must not import services, repositories, SDK/extensions/features, API, bootstrap/main, migration, eval, or scripts. Ask reasoning crosses explicit prepared-input, retrieval-evidence, response-draft, and committed-answer boundaries. **The response draft is produced by an injectable `ResponseDraftStage`** entered through `execute_response_draft_stage`, which rejects any other output type as a `StageBoundaryError`; the shipped `DefaultResponseDraftStage` is the previously inline synthesis/binding logic moved verbatim. It receives one frozen `ResponseDraftInput` -- evidence *after* selected-source-graph activation and after the retriever's own fail-open degradation, plus the pre-retrieval disclosure facts (memory hits, the structured Knowhow batch, the completeness/KG flags) -- and owes exactly one `ReasoningResponseDraft`; it holds no repository, model client, settings, or persistence seat *through that envelope* -- the shipped default still uses `AskService`'s own seats via `self`, since it is a method of the service, while an injected implementation receives only the envelope and the runtime, and both are admitted to the seam only after the orchestrator re-verifies authority immediately beforehand. Cancellation is checked once immediately before the seam, ahead of assembly's first schema read, and once more at the commit boundary. The commit boundary re-verifies `mode` and every identity field the draft envelope carries (`notebook_id`, `question`, `conversation_id`, `user_id`, `job_id`, `asked_at`) against the same frozen `PreparedReasoningAsk` the stage was handed, so an injected stage cannot silently persist the answer into a different job, conversation, or notebook than the one it was asked to serve; `model_errors` is filled by core from the request-local model-error sink right after the stage returns and before the retrieval ContextVars reset, so a stage does not need to (and should not) set it itself. The frozen response envelopes transfer exclusive ownership of the typed `AskResponse` graph without serializing or copying it, because collection cards and the citation list intentionally share citation instances. Its point-specific runtime authority carries the exact source scope, one `ask_reasoning` request-local retrieval run (which owns the shared leaf-I/O semaphore), cancellation token, trace sink, and the exact I/O-free connection probe injected into `AskService`. Both AskService and the typed retrieval seam independently enforce the run kind and non-empty actor; scope notebook, run cancellation, and run actor remain bound to the non-empty stage notebook, cancellation authority, and persistence user. Replacing any authority or entering/leaving a stage while a database connection is held is a loud `StageBoundaryError`, never an optional retrieval failure. The answer-save cancellation checkpoint remains immediately before the atomic save; a completed save wins. Do not acquire an outer fan-out slot around a stage: only actual KG/chunk/element/PPR leaf calls retain their existing slots. Keep legacy `ReasoningRetriever.run` / mutable `ReasoningResult` for Report working copies until Report stages migrate. Streaming Ask invokes the startup-frozen `ask.completed_observer` host only after the durable answer, terminal job row, cancellation unregister, and browser `final` event. The three built-in observers preserve the existing sequential agent-profile → retrieval-experience (reasoning only) → search-profile behavior and I/O, with separate failure isolation and capability projections of notebook+actor / no identity / actor-only. They run before the stream sentinel, ordinary failures cannot reverse `done`, and post-terminal observers never inspect request cancellation. Synchronous POST Ask, MCP Ask, and direct compatibility paths keep their prior notification semantics; workflows import only the domain host ports, never the SDK, registry, or concrete host. A new production extension point, `ask.gap_consult` (reasoning Ask only — `ask_graph`, reports, and Knowhow row-completion never route through `_run_reasoning_stage`), triggers exactly once per run, inside this same orchestrator step: after the response-draft stage has returned and before persistence — its trigger reads the pre-draft retrieval facts (selected-source-graph activation and the retriever's own fail-open degradation included), but the consultation itself, its disclosure trace step and its suggestions are all landed on the response by core afterwards, so an injected `ResponseDraftStage` structurally sees no gap-derived signal in its envelope — when the run's terminal disclosure step still lists a confirmed-but-unexecuted direction or the evidence pool is thinner than the effort tier's own `ranked_final_floor`. Its egress surface is one bounded `GapConsultQuery` — a truncated question in wording the user has actually seen (the reviewed final question only when the clarification gate really ran, i.e. `needs_clarification` on the confirmed contract; an auto-confirmed run's `resolved_question` is an unreviewed model rewrite and is never sent — the raw question as typed goes instead) plus at most two truncated gap-direction labels, never the intent contract's composite `research_question` and never any notebook/actor/source identity — and its result is not evidence: core fills `AskResponse.gap_suggestions` only after the response-draft stage returns, never through `ResponseDraftInput`, so the answering model never sees it and it enters neither `anchors` nor `citations`. The frozen `GapConsultHost` runs a contribution's availability probe and its `consult` call together on one private daemon thread under a hard, request-latency-charged deadline (`ASK_GAP_CONSULT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`); anything short of a clean, on-time, well-shaped response is fail-open, cancellation excepted -- fail-open means the answer is unchanged and no error state is surfaced, **not** that the disclosure trace step disappears: a timeout, an exception, or a malformed result still records that the consultation happened and how many suggestions came back (`0`), because the reader is entitled to know a bounded egress attempt occurred even when it produced nothing (codex #584 R4, deliberately rejected -- see `ask_service.py::_consult_gap_sources`).
- The Ask post-completion extension point uses one deployment-owned cooperative deadline: an already-started synchronous callback finishes safely, but later contributions do not start after the deadline. These rails protect post-final delivery and cannot alter the persisted answer, citations, retrieval output, or terminal job state.
- Deep Report planning, generation, and core final audit cross immutable `backend/app/application/report_pipeline.py` envelopes. Planning and generation retain separate fresh retrieval runs; stage wrappers validate the exact scope, run kind/correlation/actor/cancellation, injected connection probe, and connection-clear boundary without acquiring an outer leaf slot or copying the mutable evidence graph. All-section retrieval, the single multi-section synthesis barrier, parallel drafting, final editor, claim ledger, citation remap, image batch, and zero-body failure behavior remain core-owned and in their original order. The final editor/auditor boundary must not rewrite section Markdown.
- A report may produce a `CommittedReport` ticket only when SQLite/PostgreSQL atomically updates the same row from `generating` to `done`; cancellation also CASes only a non-terminal row, so whichever terminal state wins is sticky. `report.completed_observer` runs only after the done write, generation gate, and source/retrieval/model contexts are released; cancellation registration remains active through that hook and is removed afterward, preserving the historical active-job window. Observers cannot rewrite prose, citations, references, or status. The built-in observer retains the existing agent-profile completion signal through one opaque at-most-once access. Report post-completion has a separate deployment-owned cooperative deadline; no hook may acquire a core connection, start retrieval/model work, or reverse a durable terminal state.
- Tests patch public HTTP contracts or explicit domain seams, never private aggregate helpers. No test may bind architecture to line counts, source positions, or total file/route/model counts. Workspace-state hook extraction and FastAPI lifespan/application lifecycle composition remain outstanding, separate debt.
- Backend uses FastAPI.
- DATABASE_URL selects the formal repository backend through one repository factory. Exactly one active repository backend is selected centrally from `DATABASE_URL`. SQLite and PostgreSQL are both available direct backends; SQLite remains the shipped default. Its default path is `.local/silicon_notebook.db`.
- Source files are stored under `SILICON_NOTEBOOK_STORAGE_DIR`, defaulting to `.local/storage`.
- Default CORS origins include local frontend ports `3000` and `3001`; preserve this unless the frontend dev flow changes. Override at deploy time with a comma-separated `SILICON_NOTEBOOK_CORS_ORIGINS` (backend `.env`). Note: that variable is wired via pydantic-settings `validation_alias` + `NoDecode` — plain `Field(env=...)` is silently ignored in pydantic v2, so any new env-overridable setting must use `validation_alias` too.
- Repository access goes through the composed repository boundary. The single factory constructs `SQLiteRepository` or `PostgresRepository` behind the same runtime boundary. `RepositoryFacade` is backend-neutral over an injected `RepositoryRuntime` bundle. Application services do not assemble product SQL, inspect dialects, or import the opposite adapter. Stores own product SQL and raw row selection; established application/query components may assemble domain/application projections such as `NotebookSummaryQuery.from_row`. Backend-neutral batch maintenance is declared by `BatchMaintenancePort` and implemented by both adapters; physical SQLite vector text-to-BLOB conversion stays on the separate SQLite-only port. Services own orchestration. Facade methods must remain explicit compatibility adapters or source-checked one-hop delegates whose actual targets match the ownership manifest. Dependencies point factory/wrapper → facade → runtime → services → stores; extracted services must never import the facade back. `app/services/sqlite_identity.py` and `app/services/sqlite_notebook_sharing.py` are compatibility re-export shims (no mixin inheritance); keep them and the legacy request-context, `_COPY_CHUNK`, and `_remap_json_ids` exports from `sqlite_repository.py` compatible. The facade's public surface has a per-method caller ledger (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-23-facade-retirement-ledger.md`, reproducible via the read-only `scripts/audit_facade_callers.py`) classifying every public member `keep`/`test-only`/`ambiguous`/`retire-now` by production/script/test caller counts; retirement proceeds batch by batch off that ledger, and each retirement must sync `scripts/architecture_boundary_baseline.json::facade_public_surface` alongside `scripts/generate_repository_contract_fixtures.py --rebaseline-surface`'s `facade_surface.json`/`ownership_manifest.py` output. `scripts/check_extended.sh` asserts daily (G2) that the facade has gained no new zero-caller (`retire-now`) seats, via `scripts/audit_facade_callers.py --assert-no-retire-now`.
- Notebook authorization predicates have ONE definition point per backend: `backend/app/repositories/{sqlite,postgres}/access_sql.py` (mirroring `mount_sql.py`; the two files are placeholder-style mirrors and must change together). `user_can_access_notebook` (write, owner-only), `user_can_admin_notebook` (management, owner ∪ an effective `role='admin'` grant edge), and `user_can_read_notebook` (read, owner ∪ read-only member ∪ an effective `notebook_grants` edge — `user`/`group`/`group_admins`/`everyone`, matched against that exact four-value whitelist so shadow-parked rows fail safe) derive from it, in a strict `write ⊆ management ⊆ read` containment chain, as does every owner-or-member clause embedded in Memory read/search SQL. The admin predicate's principal test deliberately reuses the **restricted three arms** (`user`/`group`/`group_admins`, `_restricted_principal_arms`) plus `role='admin'` and **excludes `everyone`**: reuse makes `management ⊆ read` constructive rather than test-covered, and excluding `everyone` is defense-in-depth — the shadow parks a conflicting row's `role` under a sentinel string, and a `(everyone,'',<sentinel>)` row momentarily read as `role='admin'` would hand management to the whole site (the read predicate is untouched — four arms, `role` uninvolved). The deliberately-kept three-step `FOR SHARE`/three-state sites in the memory stores are pinned by an allowlist and must be extended by hand whenever the read predicate widens. Read access implies mountability (`mount_sql.MOUNT_VALID_EXPR`), but a *restricted* grant (anything short of `everyone`) only mounts while the mounting notebook itself is unshared — that unshared gate blocks re-sharing a borrowed library onward, and `tier='base'`/`everyone` bases are exempt. Hand-written copies of the predicate shape outside `access_sql.py` are rejected by `backend/tests/test_access_sql_contract.py` (introspective cross-backend parity + placeholder-direction checks + inline-shape scan). API write endpoints declare a named capability through `app/api/deps.py::require_notebook_capability(...)` — nine names across the frozen `{owner, admin}` value domain. **P2-T2 flipped six of them to the admin predicate** (`sources:write`, `kg:write`, `knowhow:write`, `knowledge:write`, `catalog:write`, and `notebook:manage` = `PATCH /notebooks/{id}` + the three grant endpoints), so a group admin now manages content through the browser. That PATCH edits the notebook's whole **descriptive profile** (`NotebookUpdate`'s eight fields: `name`, `purpose`, `primary_domain`, `target_users`, `expected_questions`, `source_types`, `taxonomy`, `access_scope`), not just its name — "rename" was shorthand for the endpoint and must not be read as the field list. Safe because none of those fields participates in any authorization decision (access is decided only by the `access_sql.py` predicates plus `mount_sql.py`, which reference `created_by`/`tier`/`notebook_members`/`notebook_grants`; `access_scope` is descriptive prose, not an access-control column) and because `model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid")` keeps repository-owned lifecycle state (`status`, `tier`, `created_by`, `is_shared`) unwritable. `backend/tests/test_notebook_update_authorization_free.py` freezes the field set so adding one forces that question to be re-answered. **Three stay owner-only**: `notebook:delete` (deleting a whole library, un-revocable by the owner), `notebook:configure` (mount configuration + `share_token` link sharing — a P2-T2 review split, kept owner because they configure the owner's own retrieval scope and outward disposal and do not transfer with content management: a group admin who could edit mounts would enumerate the owner's never-shared private libraries through `GET /notebooks/{id}/mountable` and read them whole through the proxy endpoints, and link sharing would mint public links for the owner), and `reports:write` (no consumer since P1 — reports moved to `require_notebook_read` plus an in-body row-level `created_by` check; the name is reserved for P2/P3 group-admin bulk-management actions). The 73 endpoint declarations were untouched; only the `_CAPABILITY_LEVELS` values and the factory branch changed. Body-level checks that must resolve `notebook_id` from another id first go through `notebook_capability_allowed(capability, ...)` against the same table. New write endpoints must use the capability factory, never a bare owner guard; `backend/tests/test_notebook_capability_guard.py` rejects bare-guard regressions by AST identifier scan (with empty-scan protection). Group knowledge sharing (design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-17-group-knowledge-sharing-design_zh.md`) extended the read predicate and flipped those capability levels without touching endpoint declarations; the five off-table write-predicate consumers re-checked at that point are enumerated beside `_CAPABILITY_LEVELS` in `deps.py` (the Agent/MCP surface deliberately did not flip, `knowledge_routes.can_edit` and the source parse/delete and knowhow-transfer in-body checks follow the table automatically, and the frontend `NotebookSummary.can_manage_content` UI signal is a separate manual change). P2 also adds the member-contribution approval flow as `notebook_share_requests`, kept out of `notebook_grants` so the read predicate stays status-filter-free: a plain member requests sharing a library **they manage** into a group they are only a **member** of, a group admin approves by inserting the `(group, viewer)` edge in the same write transaction, the state machine is one-directional `pending → approved/rejected` with withdrawal a whole-row `DELETE`, `decided_at` is only ever SQL `NULL`/ISO (never `''`), `uq_share_requests_one_pending` caps one in-flight request per (library, group) and the create endpoint returns the existing pending row idempotently on conflict, and `status` is exact-matched against the three values, never `!=`. **Deep reports are the one registered exception to "endpoint declarations do not change"**: a member of a shared notebook may create their own report, and reports are private to their creator, so authorization there is not expressible as one notebook-level capability. All nine report write endpoints now declare `require_notebook_read` plus an in-body row-level `reports.created_by == current user` check (`report_routes.py::_own_report_or_404`, which every endpoint touching an existing report must call — pinned by an AST guard); list/export narrow by the same predicate in SQL, and "someone else's report" is the same 404 as "no such report". `reports:write` stays registered with no consumer, reserved for P2 group-admin management actions. The anonymous share page re-checks the creator's *live* read access on every request, so losing access kills the links that member issued: `GET /public/reports/{token}` calls `user_can_read_notebook(notebook_id, created_by)` with both ids passed explicitly (the anonymous router binds no request user and must never touch the `current_user` ContextVar, which falls back to the seeded administrator when unset), and a failure returns the **same 404 as an unknown token**, because a distinguishable response would report somebody's group membership to an anonymous caller. Restoring access revives the link. Administrative usage attribution follows the same creator rule: `list_user_usage` counts reports by `reports.created_by`, matching `questions`, the per-notebook breakdown, and the activity feed.
- Group administration adds its own boundaries beside that predicate. **Group visibility is 404, not 403** — group names are themselves probeable — with one deliberate exception at `POST /notebooks/{id}/grants`, where "no such group" (404) and "you do not administer that group" (403) are distinguishable, because reaching it already proves management rights on the library and the caller must already hold a 128-bit random group id. **Creating a group edge is a double condition** (management rights on the notebook *and* group-admin identity on the target group), and the group half is decided **inside the store's write transaction**: the edge grants a whole group read access the instant it lands, and a check-then-insert window is long enough for the group to be deleted or the caller demoted while the edge ships anyway. **Revocation is asymmetric** — the library's manager deletes any edge from the notebook side, a group admin deletes every edge pointing at their group from the group side, each needing only its own half. `user` principals keep using the share-token flow and `everyone` keeps using `POST /notebooks/{id}/tier`; neither goes through the grant endpoints, because two write entrances for one fact eventually leave one missing the other's validation. An invalid `?scope` is a 422, never a silent fall back to `mine`. **Administrators hold an operations bypass on the group dimension** (read any group detail, manage any group's members and edges without membership), mirroring the `notebooks.created_by` transfer bypass: the "keep one group admin" rule can still leave a zero-admin group under a concurrency window that no management endpoint could then recover, and `GET /groups?scope=all` would otherwise be a table whose every row 404s. The bypass does not cover self-service leave, does not fabricate `my_role`, and relaxes no notebook-dimension guard. Deleting a group clears the grant rows pointing at it in the same write transaction; because `principal_id` carries no foreign key, `scripts/merge_dbs.py::sweep_orphan_group_grants` sweeps the orphans a union merge can resurrect — after `GLOBAL_UNION` and before `foreign_key_check`, and matching only the `group`/`group_admins` principals, since `user`/`everyone` `principal_id`s do not point at `groups` at all and sweeping them would delete two classes of perfectly normal grants. At runtime `list_grants` labels an unresolvable edge `principal_kind="missing"` so a library owner can understand and delete it.
- Group sharing's full-stack parity surface must not regress. `GET /notebooks/{id}/mountable` returns `MountableNotebook` with `origin ∈ {base, mine, shared}`, projected by `MOUNT_ORIGIN_COLUMN` from columns `MOUNT_VALID_EXPR` already reads (zero extra queries), so the mount picker can group truthfully — once read access implies mountability, other people's libraries enter the candidate set and a `tier`-only split would file them under "my notebooks". Priority base → mine → shared keeps the pre-existing grouping byte-for-byte. That field belongs on a **new** model, never on `NotebookRef`, which is also `MountedBase`'s base class and the response model of a query that does not compute the flag. `NotebookSummary.granted_via` carries `{group_id, group_name, kind}` and `access` gains **no** new enum value (a group member is exactly the reader tier); **both the list and the detail path** fill it in, de-duplicated **membership-row-first**, so somebody who both joined by link and sits in a granted group has an empty `granted_via` and keeps a working "退出共享" button. Conversely a card with a non-empty `granted_via` **must hide** that button: it deletes only a `notebook_members` row and is a no-op against an authorization edge, so pressing it while the library stays listed is a guaranteed false failure; show "由组管理员管理" instead. Owner-side "已分享" (badge and `shared-by-me` overview) becomes a **union** of `notebooks.is_shared` and any group-pointing edge, with `SharedByMeItem.group_count`; rows with no link are not size-counted and their members are not queried, since `mode`/`size`/`members` all describe the link. The new read-only `GET /notebooks/{id}/share` makes opening the share dialog side-effect-free — it never mints a token and does not compute copy statistics when there is none — and the link is created only when the user explicitly asks (that `POST` stays idempotent). A borrowed edge disabled by the unshared gate gets its own copy and name visibility, decided by `MOUNT_GATE_CLOSED_EXPR` derived from the mount predicate rather than re-spelled at the consumer: the mounting owner still legitimately holds read access, so the real name stays visible and the message offers the un-share recovery path instead of the fixed "no longer public and not yours", which is simply untrue for a borrowed edge; the share dialog must also warn **before** sharing that borrowed reference libraries will pause participating in retrieval. Deep reports are open to read-only members (`canManageReports` is unconditionally true) under the row-level creator isolation above, and the pending-actions bell now resolves notebook names for non-owned libraries and evaluates its report half **outside** the owned-notebook gate — that half's predicate consumes only `created_by` and not a single notebook id, so inside the gate a member with no libraries of their own had a permanently-zero bell while their own report waited at `intent_ready`.
- `RepositoryRuntime` owns or references composed runtime state; `REPORT_CANCELLATIONS` remains the intentionally process-global canonical owner, and the runtime, report coordinator, and module compatibility functions share that same identity reference. Other mutable operational state is runtime-owned, and supported post-composition replacements must update every retained consumer. Synchronous Ask/report submission failures must mark the durable job/report failed, unregister its cancellation entry, and re-raise; preserve the successful worker order and existing Ask transaction checkpoints. Its composition is split by domain: `RepositoryRuntime.__init__` only calls the module-level `_build_*` domain constructors (plus its own two `threading.Lock()`s) in order and mounts each returned frozen bundle's fields on itself with one explicit line per seat. Call order is the dependency topology — a builder takes earlier bundles and never the runtime itself, so a cycle back into the composition root cannot be spelled; the only permitted runtime-bound inputs are narrow late-bound callables (the current-user accessor, the `ask_service` accessor and `_note_ask_completed`). Process-level side effects (scheduler validation, event logger, `kg_scheduler.initialize`) and the one persistence bundle keep their exact historical order; `backend/tests/test_repository_runtime_composition.py` freezes the mounted attribute set and both rules.
- Schema changes stay version-gated behind `SqliteMigrator` (append `_migration_N` + bump `SCHEMA_VERSION`); startup recovery/seed/admin-upgrade run every boot outside the version gate. Pre-refactor databases must keep loading: the frozen v9 fixture replay (`backend/tests/fixtures/repository_v9/`, `test_repository_v9_fixture.py`) and the backup-only real-database verifier `scripts/verify_repository_snapshot.py` are the remaining guards. The verifier uses exact per-version migration and stable-seed manifests, percent-encodes SQLite URI paths, never constructs the repository on an original database/storage path, and reports a retained temporary backup on cleanup failure without private row data. Original DB/WAL metadata and SHM existence/size are guarded; on a live WAL attachment only SHM mtime is exempt.
- Database-specific test coverage now targets the direct PostgreSQL backend only. Retired tests for the SQLite backend implementation, SQLite-to-PostgreSQL import/forward-shadow, and cross-backend parity must not be restored to the active suite.

The current schema version is 57. This is the SQLite schema version. The committed v9 compatibility fixture
upgrades through migrations v10–v57 and remains readable. Those migrations
cover compatibility and SQLite hot-path indexes (v10–v12), Memory/Agent and
Memory-derived source links/indexes (v13–v15), knowhow tables and cell code
(v16/v18), paper metadata (v17), source-linked assets (v19), and multi-domain
reference-library mounts plus promotion targets (v20), and the normalized
interactive-reformat anchor-membership expression index (v21); v22 adds durable
notebook-scoped KG build jobs; v23 added per-user latest model-service status;
v24 adds the kg_canonical_scratch table for the write-lock-slimming cluster-map
swap; v25 irreversibly scrubs stored per-user model credentials and legacy
status, then adds deployment-wide model-service health persistence keyed by
service ID; v26 adds knowhow table change history and named milestones; v27 adds
the sources.chunked_at completion marker so an extracted-but-chunkless source's
history is decidable (a legitimate zero-chunk parse versus an interrupted chunk
build); v28 adds the app_settings key/value table and the nullable
user_profiles.upload_document_limit column backing the per-notebook document
limit; v29 deterministically removes duplicate cluster membership rows and adds
the final unique membership guard; v30 adds the sources(notebook_id, file_hash)
index backing content-hash upload dedup and batch_ingest resume (previously a
full-table scan); v31 adds the inert, payload-free shadow_change_log and
shadow_capture_control internal tables. Run-scoped logical-key guards and
capture/freeze triggers are installed separately. Guards enforce uniqueness as
soon as they are installed; capture/freeze behavior remains disabled until the
run control state enables it; v32 adds reports.understanding_json for the
durable, confirmation-gated Deep Report question-understanding contract.
SQLite v33 adds covering knowledge-relations endpoint/id indexes for stable,
bounded lexical-relation keyset recall. SQLite v34 adds the indexed
`kg_relation_completion_state` generation watermark and `(source_id,id)` object
keyset index. SQLite v35 adds `ask_jobs.asked_at` for exact in-flight Ask
reconnection. SQLite v36 adds the three KG-quality-analysis precompute product
tables (kg_community_edges, kg_source_profiles and the kg_analysis_artifacts
product ledger); rebuild_communities rewrites all three wholesale in one
transaction and stamps every ledger row with the kg_mutation_seq it was built
at. None of the three carries a level column: the community layer's freshness
gate is not level-scoped, so the level a product set describes is recorded in
the ledger payload instead. PostgreSQL migration v14 mirrors v36. SQLite v37
adds the indexed `(source_id, element_type, created_at, id)` ordering on
`source_elements` for bounded, per-type collection enumeration
(formula/table/image/code_block listings). PostgreSQL migration v15 mirrors
v37. SQLite v38 and PostgreSQL migration v16 add the partial
`idx_sources_visible_identity` index on `(notebook_id, created_at, id)` for
bounded visible-source identity lookup while excluding hidden Memory/Knowhow
projection rows. SQLite v39 adds the command-catalog extraction tables: `catalog_jobs`
(one row per run, carrying the per-source `queued`/`running` partial unique
index that is the cross-process single-flight guard) and `catalog_candidates`
(one reviewable row per extracted or grounding-rejected entry, keyset-ordered
by a per-job `position`). `catalog_jobs.source_generation` records the source
element generation the run was created against.
`catalog_candidates.job_id` deliberately carries no
foreign key — the rows cascade from notebooks/sources directly, and an incoming
foreign key would make `catalog_jobs` a non-leaf table and leave the
single-column `source_id` guard with no static parking strategy for the forward
shadow. v39 also installs `idx_knowhow_tables_nb_title` on
`knowhow_tables(notebook_id, title, created_at, id)` — its only index on a
pre-existing table — so by-title target resolution is an index seek whose last
two columns already supply the `(created_at, id)` tie-break, instead of reading
every table row in the notebook inside the locked apply window. PostgreSQL
migration v17 mirrors v39. SQLite v40 / PostgreSQL migration v18 add immutable
`knowledge_source_facts` plus normalized `knowledge_source_fact_elements`.
The writer validates the current running extraction generation and cited
element ownership inside the global-KG transaction; the global-object link is
not a foreign key, so fusion cannot erase source truth. The read path remains
off until the later source-subgraph PRs.
SQLite v41 / PostgreSQL migration v19 add the restartable
`knowledge_source_fact_backfills` ledger for the explicit offline historical
projection. Backfill must rebuild the reverse source index once per notebook,
then read bounded source-first object pages and commit one short transaction per
page. Project only rows whose owner and every cited element are provably from
the same source; ambiguous legacy provenance is `incomplete`, never guessed.
Persist only cursor/counts/projection version/stable incomplete reason/separate
operational failure code/status. An explicit fact `projection_origin` is the
only live-vs-historical discriminator; never infer origin from a model-produced
local id prefix. Count current-generation live facts independently of the
continued existence of their fused global objects. Reuse a completed
reverse-index marker across resumed commands, and make the audit independently
reconcile effective KG generation, projection version, and persisted fact
counts. SQLite v42 / PostgreSQL migration v20 add the notebook-level
`source_index_backfills` execution ledger. Its KG-generation pin, keyset cursor,
total/scanned/written counters, terminal timestamp, and stable failure code are
committed with each reverse-index page; it never stores evidence or exception
text. An interrupted command resumes the last committed page, a completed
current marker is normalized to a completed ledger without rewriting rows, and
generation drift fails closed before the next invocation resets the notebook.
SQLite v43 / PostgreSQL migration v21 add revocable public-report sharing
tokens (`reports.share_token`/`shared_at`) with a partial unique index covering
only issued tokens. SQLite v44 / PostgreSQL migration v22 add
`chunks.question_indexed_at` and the source-owned `chunk_questions` generated-
question index; cascade/reparse cleanup and notebook-copy remapping preserve
the original-chunk ownership boundary. SQLite v45 / PostgreSQL migration v23
add the nullable `user_profiles.ui_mode` column backing the per-user interface
mode preference (`auto` default / `advanced`); readers fall back to `auto` when
the column or profile row is absent.
SQLite v46 / PostgreSQL migration v24 add `chunk_elements`, the element -> chunk
reverse index, its notebook-level execution ledger `chunk_element_backfills`,
and the `unified_kg_state.chunk_elements_indexed` marker that forks the read
path. The composite primary key `(notebook_id, element_id, chunk_id)` is the
seek index; the extra `chunk_id` index exists only to serve the cascade from
`chunks`. The migration creates empty tables — historical rows are projected
only by the explicit offline `backfill-chunk-elements` phase, whose ledger has
the same shape and the same fail-closed generation rule as
`source_index_backfills` and stores no chunk or exception text. Its completion
test is an exhausted cursor, never a scanned-versus-frozen-total comparison:
concurrent writes inflate the scanned count, so a total-based gate would flip
the marker with historical chunks still unprojected.
SQLite v47 / PostgreSQL migration v25 add `notebook_object_schemas`, keyed by
`(notebook_id, object_type)`, for owner-governed notebook-local graph schema
definitions. Global `object_schemas` remain the administrator-managed baseline;
an effective registry overlays same-name notebook rows, including local disable,
without changing the canonical four-type source-extraction contract. Proposed
rows are review-only and never shadow the effective registry before activation.
Writes lock/recheck both registries, including after model-backed induction, and
database merge preflight rejects conflicting same-name global definitions.
SQLite v48 / PostgreSQL migration v26 add the nullable `sources.agent_profile_id`
provenance column: NULL means a person added the row, and it is written on the
INSERT branch only, so content-hash dedupe that reuses an existing row keeps the
first writer's provenance and a notebook deep copy clears the column outright
(every copied source counts as user-added). `SourceSummary` and the source detail
models project it as the `agent_created` boolean, which is the gate the MCP
`delete_source` tool checks; the projection defaults to false when the column is
absent, so the gate fails closed.
SQLite v49 / PostgreSQL migration v27 add the three group-knowledge-sharing
tables `groups`, `group_members` and `notebook_grants`. `notebook_grants` holds
one row per live authorization edge — `(notebook_id, principal_type,
principal_id, role)` with `principal_type ∈ {user, group, group_admins,
everyone}` — and its `principal_id` is a **polymorphic** reference (user id,
group id, or the empty string for `everyone`) carrying deliberately no principal
foreign key and no CHECK constraint; enum validation lives in the application
layer. That column must stay `NOT NULL DEFAULT ''`: NULL does not participate in
unique comparison, so an `everyone` row would escape
`UNIQUE (notebook_id, principal_type, principal_id)` entirely, letting duplicate
grants accumulate and leaving revocation incomplete — and NOT NULL is also what
hands the forward shadow's parking column to `principal_type` (SENTINEL_TEXT).
For the same reason the `everyone` test must be the exact four-value match
`principal_type = 'everyone'` and must never be inferred from `principal_id`
(neither `IS NULL` nor `= ''`): parking temporarily writes a sentinel string
into a conflicting row's `principal_type`, and exact matching makes parked rows
fail safe. Notebook deep copy deliberately does **not** carry authorization
edges, following the `notebook_members` precedent — access-control state is not
knowledge, and the new owner re-grants it. Deleting a group clears the grant rows
pointing at it inside the same write transaction, since `principal_id` has no
foreign key to enforce that.

SQLite v50 / PostgreSQL migration v28 add `notebook_share_requests`, the
member-contribution approval-flow table — a sibling of `notebook_grants`,
deliberately kept out of the grant table so the decision predicate stays
status-filter-free. A plain member submits a request against a **library they
manage** toward a group they are only a **plain member** of (a group admin
sharing into a group they administer never touches this table, using the existing
grants endpoint); a group admin approves, inserting the `(group, viewer)` edge and
updating the row's status in one write transaction. The state machine is
one-directional `pending → approved/rejected`: withdrawal is a whole-row `DELETE`
by the requester (only while `pending`), never a third status, so
`decided_by`/`decided_at` stay purely the group admin's decision. `decided_at` may
only be written as SQL `NULL` or an ISO timestamp, **never the empty string** — it
is this table's only nullable time column entering the forward shadow, PostgreSQL's
`timestamptz` would type-error on `''` and poison replication, and it is
deliberately **not** registered in `POSTGRES_EMPTY_TIME_SENTINELS`. The partial
unique index `uq_share_requests_one_pending`
(`(notebook_id, group_id, status) WHERE status = 'pending'`, three-column so the
`status` column serves the shadow's SENTINEL_TEXT parking candidate without pinning
an inbound FK) caps one in-flight request per (library, group); the create endpoint
hitting it is **not a plain 409 but an idempotent return of the existing pending
row**. `status` consumers may only exact-match `pending`/`approved`/`rejected` and
never use `!=` as a "decided" test. Both FKs cascade, and notebook deep copy
carries no requests, following the grant-edge precedent.

SQLite v51 / PostgreSQL migration v29 add the two agent-understanding tables
`agent_notebook_profile` and `agent_profile_jobs`, keyed by primary key
`(notebook_id, owner_id, label)` and `(notebook_id, owner_id)` respectively —
the replication key equals the declared primary key on both, so the forward
shadow parks them by `REPLICATION_KEY` with no sentinel column and no
`_UNIQUE_PREDICATES` pin. `owner_id` follows the `notebook_grants.principal_id`
precedent: `NOT NULL DEFAULT ''` (`''` marks the shared base layer that every
member reads; a non-empty value marks that one member's personal overlay),
deliberately carrying no foreign key to `users` and no CHECK constraint on it
or on `label` (label values `corpus_shape`/`key_entities`/`corpus_gaps`/
`retrieval_notes`/`usage_gaps` are validated in the application layer only).
`agent_notebook_profile.history_json` is a bounded ring buffer of before/after
entries appended in the same write transaction as the block update — v1
intentionally reuses the block's own JSON history column instead of adding a
separate change-history table, since the interface only reads/edits/clears/
rebuilds current blocks and a queryable history table would add a full
manifest/copy-rank/parking entry for no reachable capability. Notebook deep
copy carries neither table: a copy starts with no consolidated understanding
of its own libraries or usage, and job rows are transient process state like
`catalog_jobs`. PostgreSQL migration v29 is the paired schema.

SQLite v52 / PostgreSQL migration v30 add conversation public sharing to
`conversations`: `share_token` (nullable, partial unique index
`idx_conversations_share_token WHERE share_token IS NOT NULL`, NULL-parking like
`notebooks.share_token`/`reports.share_token`), the read watermark
`shared_through_at` (a literal timestamp value, NOT a foreign key — storing an
answer id would go meaningless once that answer is deleted, so this stays the
always-valid fallback bound) plus `shared_through_id` (the id of that same
answer — load-bearing, not display-only: the public boundary keysets on this
answer's `(created_at, rowid/ordinal)` for an exact same-instant tie-break, and
falls back to the `shared_through_at` interval only when it no longer resolves). The token lives on the row rather than a side table (same
`_migration_43` report-token precedent; a deleted conversation takes its link
with it). **Deep copy needs no handling** — `_COPY_VALIDATED_TABLES` does not
include `conversations` at all, so these columns never travel with a copy and
there is nothing to clear; the migration comment records this so nobody adds a
redundant clear by analogy with the token siblings. This adds columns, not
tables and not FKs, so the forward shadow's fixed FK branch bound stays 12 row
slots and the table count is unchanged; the new partial unique index takes the
PG catalog from 108 to 109 unique surfaces.

SQLite v53 / PostgreSQL migration v31 add `agent_profile_jobs.claim_token`, the
consolidation chain's claim GENERATION (Agentic Memory P2). It is a
`TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT ''` column with no index, no unique surface and no
foreign key, minted afresh on every `claim` and carried as part of the
compare-and-swap by both `settle` and `write_block`. It closes the ABA left by
P1's status-only single-flight: a member removed and re-added gets a job row
with the same primary key and `runs` back at 0, so neither the key nor the run
counter could act as a generation, and a stale worker's settle used to land on
the replacement row (consuming the new run's snapshot and releasing a slot it
never held) while its writes passed a bare existence check. A timestamp was
rejected for the same reason `memory_revisions` rejected one — SQLite's clock
is second-granular, so a delete and recreate inside one second is invisible.
`settle` therefore returns three outcomes rather than a bool: `settled`, `gone`
(no row — only member removal deletes it, so the caller wipes the blocks this
run recreated) and `superseded` (a row, but a later claim's — the caller must
NOT wipe, because the newer generation may already have written its own
blocks). The distinguishing re-read happens inside the settling transaction;
outside it, a concurrent removal turns a `superseded` into a `gone`, i.e. turns
"leave the new run's work alone" into "delete it". `write_block` takes an
optional `claim_token` and, when non-empty, checks it inside the same write
transaction, raising `AgentProfileClaimSuperseded`; the empty default is the
user-facing edit path, which holds no claim. `sweep_stale_on_start` deliberately
ignores the token, which strands every in-flight worker's generation exactly as
intended. No new table, index or unique surface, so the forward-shadow
invariants stay at 80 business tables, 109 unique surfaces and a 12-row-slot
branch-counted bound.

SQLite v54 / PostgreSQL migration v32 add `retrieval_experiences` (Agentic
Memory P2), the deployment-GLOBAL retrieval-strategy experience library. One
entry says "in this shape of question, this retrieval action is / is not
worth reaching for", plus a short model-written rationale, a `support` count
of the runs behind it, and an `adopted` count of the times the model actually
picked that action after the entry was injected. The table carries no
`notebook_id`, no owner column, and no foreign key in either direction: it
stores general tactics for HOW to search, never anyone's content, so notebook
deep copy structurally cannot reach it (the same sentence that already covers
`groups`/`group_members`) and `scripts/merge_dbs.py` classifies it as a global
union table. Its primary key is a single content-addressed `TEXT` column — the
deterministic hash of (situation fingerprint, action) — which is what keeps
that union safe across independent deployments (an incrementing id would
silently drop rows on a primary-key collision) and, because the declared
replication key equals it verbatim, also what parks its one unique surface on
`REPLICATION_KEY` with no sentinel column. It creates no index — the row count
is hard-capped, and the only two read paths are a primary-key point lookup and
a bounded-return, unbounded-scan read (an index is registered as deferred to the next schema hop). This is deliberately the only store in the repository
with no tenancy predicate at all: isolation instead lives one layer up, in
what may become a row in the first place. The distillation input is projected
to `RunObservation` (`app/services/retrieval_experience_projection.py`) — a
frozen dataclass whose every reachable field is an `int`, a `bool`, or a
closed `Literal`, with no free-text field anywhere — so the model that writes
`rationale` has never seen a question, an answer, a document title, or an id.
A static privacy guard (`backend/tests/test_retrieval_experience_privacy_guard.py`)
scans both the projection module and the job module together for that
property, since moving a `question`-shaped read from one file to the other
would otherwise defeat a guard that only watched one of them. An entry never
influences retrieval scope: the closed THEN-side action vocabulary contains no
scope-shaped action, an entry carries no source/notebook/library field to
