Each result's envelope (metadata + coverage) is reserved before any item is admitted: that envelope is what discloses the cut, so it is never the thing dropped. A row stopped short reports `complete=False`, `truncated_reason="payload"`, and `returned_total` equal to what was delivered; the synthesis preview is rendered from `delivered_outcomes`, the view of what the cards actually carry, so the prompt's coverage header and the result card can never state different numbers for the same list. The preview's `inline_rows` allowance is likewise pooled per run and is SPLIT rather than handed out first-come-first-served — each outcome is reserved `max(1, inline_rows // n)` first and the remainder is passed along in outcome order — because a real first list usually holds at least the whole allowance, which would leave every later collection at `previewed 0` and answer a multi-collection question from one list. The closing stability check re-resolves the participant set through `participant_ids` (the same `resolve_participants` / `mount_sql.py` entry the opening call used) and compares the SET before recomputing the fingerprint/seq vector over the freshly resolved ids: a library mounted or unmounted mid-walk is invisible to a digest recomputed over the opening id list (an empty new base contributes no signal, and an unmounted base's signals are still there), and such a walk must report `concurrent_change`, never "all". Narrowing an element enumeration to one document is expressed by `enumerate.source_title` (internal source ids are never shown to the model) and resolved server-side against the map's own plan by exact trimmed/case-folded match, bounded; zero or multiple matches skip the action rather than widening it, and a plan longer than the resolver's cap is declined outright rather than resolved from its prefix — uniqueness is a property of the whole scope. `replace_elements` advances the source's `updated_at` in its own write transaction on BOTH backends, so the change signal flips atomically with the element generation and a just-parsed source is countable and listable immediately — the first-parse under-count window this feature previously disclosed is eliminated, not documented. (`sources.updated_at` is read in exactly one place, that signal query; nothing orders, displays or keys on it.) An explicitly named `source_id` is still queried directly rather than inferred from the plan. Page round trips per action are bounded and the bound is ENFORCED (`_Walk.bound_queries`, breach raises `EnumerationInvariantError` and fails open as one skipped action): `max_rows + max_pages` for elements — zero-count sources never enter the plan, and a planned source yields rows — and `participants + max_pages + the raw over-scan ceiling` for KG objects, which has no per-partition count to skip on and pays top-up reads for the status filter. Each partition's first page stays free of the page allowance deliberately; charging it would again make a wide-and-thin corpus unenumerable. Because these tools need no graph, reasoning Ask's "this notebook has no knowledge graph" early return now only fires when the scope ALSO holds no enumerable collection; the `kg_required` flag keeps its meaning either way.
- `app/services/outline_synthesis.py` plus the `update_outline` branch in `reasoning_retrieval.py` back a bounded, model-authored outline scratchpad and section-by-section synthesis (design doc §3.1, borrowed from DualGraph), gated by `outline_wiring_active(settings, limits)`, which reads `limits.effort == "exhaustive"` off `AskRetrievalLimits.effort` — never derived from budget numbers, since any `dataclasses.replace` of a budget field must not silently change which tier a call belongs to — AND `REASONING_OUTLINE_ENABLED` (default true). `update_outline` is the reflect loop's eleventh action id (`OUTLINE_ACTION`): the section structure is full-replacement, with at most 12 sections, two levels (a section may name one parent, itself parentless), 60-character titles, 8 evidence keys per section, and at most 6 ordinary calls per run; evidence on the same stable section id is a union with the old bindings, omission never deletes it, and `remove_evidence` is the only explicit removal mechanism. At the eight-key cap old bindings win; rejected new keys are named in the next bounded scratchpad so the model can explicitly free space and retry. Pending overflow does not freeze a later ordinary update while ordinary allowance remains: structural changes and all legal bindings in that full payload are applied normally. A terminal `sufficient`/stale correction is evidence-only and available only if a normal reflect step remains; the sixth ordinary update can expose the same one-shot qualification after its own allowance is exhausted. Correction submissions preserve every section id/title/parent and cannot execute retrieval; the one-shot cap qualification is consumed by its first submission even if rejected. `max_steps` is absolute, so last-step overflow is disclosed rather than adding step 51/50, and an already-triggered stale breaker is recorded before any in-budget terminal correction. Unresolved rejected keys are disclosed by a closing `outline_evidence_overflow_unresolved` trace step and never enter synthesis. A submission with no legal section is a skip that PRESERVES the previous outline rather than wiping it. Evidence-key legality is server-computed, not model-asserted: the legal set is the live candidate pool intersected with the run-monotonic set of keys actually rendered in a candidate-summary window plus the current outline's already-legal bindings. Thus a previously shown binding survives window movement, while a never-rendered middle-window id cannot pass by being guessed. Enumeration-listing item ids and source ids are deliberately excluded from that legal set (the model is never shown listing ids, and one document produces no synthesizable evidence slice). Every reflect round re-attaches the whole current outline because the section structure is full-replacement and reflect prompts carry no conversation history; server-side union protects bindings even when the model omits a key, and empty sections are named as retrieval-direction gaps. Outline revisions are stale-neutral by design: they never advance or reset the `no_progress`/stale-circuit-breaker account (alternating cosmetic-only outline edits must not dodge `reasoning_stale_limit`), while a binding round's own retrieval action still resets staleness normally. `_outline_nudge_note` appends one deterministic adoption-nudge line to the same reflect region (design doc §3.1.1) when the gate is on, the outline is still EMPTY, the run's bounded nudge allowance is unspent, and one server-held structural reason exists — a source roster this run already enumerated to `state == "complete"` at or above the item floor, or that many confirmed mandatory retrieval directions (counted off `run()`'s existing reviewed-direction list minus its leading whole-question seed, never by re-parsing the intent contract). Roster wording wins when both hold; an incomplete roster never counts, because an outline built from a half-listed roster is missing sections the model cannot yet see are missing. It costs zero queries and zero model calls, adds no action id, phrases itself as a ledger entry with an explicit opt-out, and its mutual exclusion with the scratchpad lives in that function's own empty-sections check rather than an `else` at the call site. Only a round that actually emits it adds `outline_nudged: true` to the existing reflect step's `detail` (an unconditional `False` would break the frozen-baseline detail-KEY set at lower tiers). At run close, `outline_synthesis.plan_outline_sections` resolves each section's evidence keys against the live `collected`/element/chunk maps; sections resolving to zero evidence are equivalent to empty sections. When ≥2 sections resolve non-empty and the run produced no collection listing (typed enumeration or structured whole-table batch — a listing run stays on the single-pass path, since listing previews and coverage disclosures enter only that path's synthesis context and sectioning it would write prose from a ranked sample while a complete listing sat unused), `AskService._answer_reasoning_sections` synthesizes once per section, each call passing only that section's resolved hits/elements/chunks into `_answer_reasoning(sectioned=True, key_offset=...)`: every context-assembly id range (chunk/element/KG/Memory/chain) is offset by `item.key_offset` (a 10,000-per-section stride) so no two sections' `[k]` numbers collide; the collection-map block, enumeration previews, private Memory, and query-time derived chains are never passed into a sectioned call — they are not legal binding targets, so a section could never have asked for them, and remain untouched on the fallback path; evidence refinement is skipped in sectioned mode (it is a per-assembly model call, so running it per section would turn k synthesis calls into 2k, and an 8-key slice never has a refinement-worthy middle to trim); and each section's `[k]` anchors are resolved against that section's own `id_map` before sections are merged — resolving the stitched-together answer against a merged map would let a marker naming another section's disjoint key range silently bind to that section's evidence instead of being discarded as the hallucination it is. `ReasoningResult.outline_evidence` carries every `collected` hit bound by the outline but pushed out of `top_hits` by the final rerank/`top_n` cap (quota-capped runs float that value to the selected set's floor, or 0.0 for an empty selection, rather than reusing a pre-rerank relevance that could inflate `grounded`); these extra hits are folded into the evidence-classification pool only when sectioned synthesis actually ran. Every section is classified against its own slice and anchors; the closing detail's `section_grounded` is a bounded list of section records (each with its own boolean and `evidence_level`), not the whole-answer flag, and ungrounded titles are listed separately. All grounded sections leave the ordinary global `classify_evidence` result unchanged; otherwise that result is capped at `overview` but never raised. Zero exactly-grounded sections can therefore remain `overview` when each section has relevant cited evidence but a conservative model report, rather than being forcibly mislabeled `inferred`. Any section whose synthesis fails (after its own `_answer_with_retry`) discards the entire partial product and falls back to ordinary single-shot synthesis; a successful fallback removes the sectioned attempt's `model_error` entries from the user-facing `_err_sink` while leaving `events.jsonl` intact. `_answer_with_retry` itself now applies the same rule on every Ask path: it records `mark = len(sink)` on entry (skipping the removal entirely when `_ASK_MODEL_ERRORS.get()` is `None`) and drops the `ask_answer` entries from `sink[mark:]` when a retry finally produces the answer (filtering by workload identity, not by position: today `synth()` records nothing else, but giving evidence refinement a `note_model_error` would make a positional truncation swallow it on a first-attempt success), so a recovered run does not display "本次回答可能不完整" over a complete answer; when both attempts fail EVERY entry survives, including the terminal empty-content `RuntimeError`, and entries recorded before `mark` by other workloads are never touched. The sectioned removal deliberately keeps its closed upper bound (`outline_err_mark:fallback_err_mark`) rather than becoming an open slice, so it can never swallow a fallback-path alarm that was meant to be kept. Each completed section emits its own lightweight `synthesis`-typed `TraceStep` (`section_index`/`section_total`/`section_title`) in addition to the run's closing `synthesis` step, which gains `outline_sections`/`outline_fallback`/`outline_skipped`/`section_grounded`/`ungrounded_sections` in its `detail` whenever outline planning ran — including when sectioning was bypassed for having fewer than two resolvable sections or for a listing run (`outline_sections` 0, `outline_fallback` false), so the skipped-section disclosure never vanishes with the bypass — and stays untouched when no outline exists, preserving the frozen-baseline detail-key set at every lower tier and the disabled state. Citation-shaped markers in model-authored section titles are stripped at the parse entry point (a heading marker in the merged answer would render as an unbindable citation or collide with another section's key range). `update_outline` itself emits an `outline`-typed trace step (front-end label "大纲"). v1 adds no new `AskResponse` field for the outline; it surfaces only through the trace and the answer's own `##`/`###` heading structure (scoped `.chat-answer .answer-markdown h2/h3`, distinct from the Deep Report heading scale). Deep Report's own report path (`report_engine.py`) reaches both mechanisms through the same gate, not a report-specific copy: `report_retrieval_limits(depth)` maps the report's own `depth` (1/2/4/8/16, threshold-based so any clamped in-between value snaps to the next lower named tier) onto `ask_retrieval_limits(effort)` and passes the result as `limits` into `ReasoningRetriever.run` for that section's deep-dive, while `max_steps` stays the report's own depth value rather than the tier's own step ceiling — so reaching `exhaustive` (depth 16) activates outline scratchpad + KG weak-support gap feedback inside that section exactly as it does in Ask, with zero report-specific wiring. The tier governs the two stages after `run()` as well: the per-direction top-up merge is re-clamped to the tier's `ranked_final_cap` and `answer_element_items` (by relevance descending; outline-bound objects are exempt, mirroring their position outside `top_hits` on the Ask side) instead of letting four confirmed directions multiply an overview report past its own ceiling, and the section writing context uses `kg_context_chars` for the KG partition and one shared `chunk_context_chars` for chunks plus direct elements (capped at `answer_element_items`) rather than the fixed `ANSWER_CONTEXT_BUDGET_CHARS`/`REPORT_SECTION_CHUNK_BUDGET` pair with a separate element allowance stacked on top — a tier honoured only inside `run()` is handed straight back by the two stages that consume its output. The finalized sub-outline is folded into a bounded "discovered structure" block (`outline_structure_block`, ≤12 lines/≤80 chars per line/≤1200 chars) that only augments that section's own `report_section_prompt` as a suggestion for organizing `###` sub-headings; it never writes back to `reports.outline_json`, the user-confirmed report outline. Collection enumeration tools remain unreachable on this path regardless of tier, since the report constructs its retriever without `collection_catalog`/`collection_enumeration`. The tier additionally scales the OUTLINE stage's zero-LLM probes and the deep-dive's call count: the coverage probe (per mandatory topic) and sufficiency probe (per drafted section) each execute a per-tier number of retrieval queries (values registered only in `docs/product-and-api*.md`; a report row without a depth keeps the historical width), probe output feeds only the STORM/judge coverage counts and never body evidence, repeated probe queries within one planning run are memoized (the memo never outlives the run and never caches failures, so transient errors keep their per-probe retry), and the planner writes a "检查各节证据充分性" progress state between "多视角规划大纲中" and "大纲就绪" so the post-STORM probe phase no longer reads as a minutes-long stall on large libraries, and the sufficiency judge's LLM refinement half runs only at deep and above — overview/standard keep the deterministic half (the coverage counts and verdicts the outline editor displays), byte-identical to the judge's existing fail-open output since the LLM half only ever refines downward. Each section's deep-dive seeds `ReasoningRetriever.run` with the section's user-confirmed retrieval directions via `intent_queries` — a reviewed direction set is authoritative and is never reinterpreted by a second model (the same principle as Ask), which removes one planning LLM call per section; the first seed is always the section's composite question (mirroring Ask, where the full authoritative question leads the confirmed seeds); directions the run SUCCESSFULLY executed (per its `attempted` ledger, minus entries carrying the sparse `failed` marker — a retrieval that blew up is not a retrieval that ran) are not re-fetched by the post-run KG merge, per-direction element top-up remains (the run does not fetch elements per direction), and directions the run's step budget could not fit — or whose in-run retrieval failed — are still executed by the post-run merge; the "every approved retrieval direction must execute" contract is honoured jointly through the `attempted` ledger, and the lower tiers' more conservative sufficiency verdicts are registered in the contract docs. Stacked on top: after each *accepted* `update_outline` apply, `RetrievalPort.weak_support_relations` probes the canonical KG neighborhood of the objects just bound as evidence and feeds back weakly-corroborated relations (design doc §3.3, PR-4) — gated by `REASONING_OUTLINE_KG_GAP_ENABLED` (default true) layered on the outline gate itself, with `kg_gap_probed_seeds` deduping so a pure key-swap/retitle apply issues zero queries. Both read primitives (`weak_support_relation_rows`, `relation_endpoint_name_rows`) walk primary-key prefixes and `idx_clusters_member`, never `concept_clusters.canonical_id IN (...)` (unindexed on both backends); the threshold and the rendered count are `source_count` (distinct corroborating documents), not the raw relation-row `support_count`, since alias normalization routinely folds several single-document relations into one canonical edge. The probe is fail-open (an exception records a `kg_gap_unavailable` skip step rather than failing the run) and a terminal overflow-repair round neither renders nor consumes the queued hint, since that round's own scratchpad text forbids retrieval. The `outline` trace step's `kg_gap_candidates` integer counts candidates newly queued by this apply for the next round's scratchpad, not candidates actually rendered.
- Every overflow-correction submission is evidence-only: its ordered `(section id, title, parent)` structure must equal the current outline, regardless of whether the correction was triggered by `sufficient`, max steps, stale, or the sixth ordinary update. Once the ordinary six-update allowance is exhausted, receipt of the first correction submission consumes the one-time qualification before structural validation, so a malformed attempt cannot retry until it succeeds.
- Rejected overflow keys are persistent server state, not per-update diagnostics. Carry each key until it is accepted, explicitly abandoned by naming that pending key in `remove_evidence`, or removed with its whole section; freeing an old slot while omitting the pending new key must still end with `outline_evidence_overflow_unresolved`. A rejected structure-changing *correction* remains unresolved, but ordinary allowance rounds are never silently converted into correction mode just because pending keys exist. Complete pending state is bounded at exactly 56 keys per section (six ordinary updates plus one correction, eight keys each), while each prompt renders only the first eight and a remaining count; resolving/abandoning that batch reveals the next, so the scratchpad keeps its documented constant window.
- Reading a mounted reference library's source detail, elements, or image assets goes through active-notebook proxy reads, never a direct cross-library request. `GET /notebooks/{active}/sources/{id}`, `GET /notebooks/{active}/sources/{id}/elements` and `GET /notebooks/{active}/assets/{asset_id}` authorize on the active notebook (`require_notebook_read`) and then resolve the target strictly inside that notebook's VALID participant set — the resource declares its own owning notebook, anything outside the set is 404 with no existence disclosed, and the set is re-evaluated per request so a demoted/transferred/mid-copy library or an unmounted edge loses access immediately. This is the same contract as the Ask-citation graph focus above: mounting a public base never grants direct membership in it, so the bare owner-or-member `GET /sources/{id}` pair stays exactly as strict as before, and writes (re-parse, delete) are deliberately NOT proxied — the source-detail window renders a reference-library source read-only. Proxied reads also disclose less than the owner-or-member endpoint: the detail model drops `file_path` and the raw `error_message` (both can carry server-side absolute paths) in favour of a `parse_failed` boolean; a cross-library source whose type is a hidden synthetic projection row (`memory`/`knowhow`, which the collection map deliberately counts) is refused outright so a citation jump can never unfold one; and a cross-library asset is served `Cache-Control: no-store` so an unmount takes effect immediately instead of being masked by a day of browser cache. The participant lookup short-circuits when the resource already belongs to the active notebook, so the same-notebook path (one request per rendered image) pays no extra query; participant resolution itself remains the single mount-predicate definition in `repositories/*/mount_sql.py`.
- Streaming Ask (`/ask/stream`) must remain progress-first: emit an immediate `started` event with the detached `job_id` and durable `conversation_id`, stream each agent trace step as it is recorded, then emit one final `AskResponse`. The ids let the frontend publish and reopen an in-flight first-turn session before any answer exists. A transport disconnect only stops delivery to that client; the worker continues and may persist its answer. Only the explicit interrupt path calls `POST /notebooks/{id}/ask/jobs/{job_id}/cancel`, sets the cancellation event, and stops the worker before saving a cancelled response. If interrupt is clicked before `started` is readable, the client must keep reading through `started`, cancel by the returned job id, and only then abort the transport; aborting first strands the detached worker. The normal `/ask` endpoint remains the non-streaming compatibility path.
- Streaming Ask must also emit one content-free blank NDJSON heartbeat every 5 seconds while the delivery queue is idle, and return `Cache-Control: no-cache, no-transform` plus `X-Accel-Buffering: no`. Existing clients ignore blank lines. This protects long model/reflection/synthesis gaps from reverse-proxy **idle** timeouts without inventing progress or changing disconnect/cancellation semantics; a deployment's absolute total-request ceiling remains an operator-owned proxy setting.
- Model JSON remains strict-first. Only `reasoning_agent` and `ask_answer` may use the shared conservative repair seam, controlled by `MODEL_JSON_REPAIR_MODE=off|shadow|on` (default `on`): repair is limited to complete object-shaped syntax faults such as missing quotes/commas, expected top-level keys and unambiguous boolean/enum example types are enforced, and every nonempty repaired string value must still occur verbatim in the raw response. Never repair truncation, arrays/scalars, unknown top-level keys, non-finite values, or rewritten strings. `shadow` records repairability but preserves strict rejection; events contain only workload/support id, stable status, and stable reason. Valid JSON stays byte-for-byte unchanged, JSON booleans are accepted only as actual booleans, and a failed `ReasoningRetriever` must degrade to a terminal persisted Ask answer rather than dereference an absent result.
- Unified-KG rebuild is explicit/observable. Opening the Knowledge Graph overlay fetches the current graph + `/unified-kg/status` and offers refresh when dirty; it must not block on rebuild.
- Cross-document concept-merge candidates must be bounded and reviewable. LLM merge review operates on small pending candidate batches, never the entire concept set at once. The pending queue has canonical-component-pair semantics: retain one deterministic highest-score representative per displayed pair, persist stable seed identities, and project every rejected/deferred seed pair through confirmed unions as a component-level cannot-link so another member seed cannot resurrect the same visible pair. One manual decision atomically settles every legacy duplicate row for that displayed pair to the latest status; lock the complete row set in deterministic order, and treat the decision as a confirmed reversal if any sibling was confirmed. Rebuild must delete its stale pending generation, then re-read and apply live decisions in the same refresh transaction before publishing replacement rows. Reject/defer of a wholly pending pair changes no current cluster or retrieval product and therefore must not invalidate, mark the unified KG dirty, or launch a rebuild; reversing a pair with any already-confirmed row is the exception because its union may be materialized, so that path must invalidate and dirty. Bump `CLUSTER_ALGO_VERSION` whenever this clustering/queue semantics changes so deployed notebooks cannot skip the corrected rebuild through the content-version gate.
- Re-running parse/extraction preserves the source row and original file, replaces source elements/chunks and their embeddings, and normally removes extraction runs plus source-derived knowledge before writing rebuilt state. The explicit batch `kg --retry-partial` path is the exception: it retains the prior source graph/history until a complete replacement is ready, then swaps graph rows transactionally.
- Deleting a source uses the same source-derived cleanup, then deletes the source row (cascading source-owned records) and local file. Do not claim unrelated artifact cleanup that the repository does not perform.

## Python Environment

Run all backend work with an isolated Python environment that has
`backend/requirements.txt` installed. Activate it — or point `PYTHON_BIN` at its
interpreter — so the helper scripts (`scripts/dev.sh`, `scripts/backend.sh`) and the
root `package.json` use it; they fall back to `python3` when `PYTHON_BIN` is unset.

Keep **machine-specific** details — absolute interpreter paths, which local port a
service happens to occupy, and similar per-developer facts — in that machine's local
config / memory, **not** in committed files (this file, the READMEs, etc.). Committed
docs describe the generic procedure only; `README.md` → "Deployment" is the canonical
setup runbook.

## Backend Commands

Run backend commands with the active environment's interpreter:

```bash
cd backend
python -m uvicorn app.main:app --host 127.0.0.1 --port 8000
```

For dependency checks or installs, use the same interpreter:

```bash
python -m pip install -r backend/requirements.txt
```

Set `PYTHON_BIN` for the helper scripts that support it:

```bash
PYTHON_BIN=/path/to/python bash scripts/check.sh
```

## Frontend/UI

`scripts/dev.sh` starts the backend and the Next.js frontend from `frontend/`. It requires `frontend/node_modules`; run `npm install` in `frontend/` first if missing. The normal mainline is:

```bash
cd frontend
npm run dev
```

`object_type` display names have one source of truth: the backend `OBJECT_TYPE_LABELS` in `app/domain/extraction_profiles.py` (`app/services/extraction_profiles.py` is a re-export shim), shipped to the client as `KnowledgeTypeCount.label`. Any call site that can reach that API label (the Knowledge browser) must use it, so user-defined object types render their proper Chinese name too. Call sites that only hold an `object_type` string (citation popover, knowledge-graph canvas and side panel) use the built-in front-end table `KG_TYPE_LABELS` in `frontend/app/kg-type-model.ts`; `kg-type-mark.tsx` consumes and re-exports the model for shared rendering. The table stays character-for-character identical to the backend constant; `scripts/check_object_type_labels_contract.py` is a hard gate in `scripts/check.sh` and fails the build on drift, so a label change on either side must land on both. Do not reintroduce a Title-Case fallback — unknown or custom types are displayed verbatim as their `object_type`. Both tables, and any other map keyed by `object_type`, must be read with `Object.hasOwn(...)` rather than bare indexing or `map[type] ?? fallback`: `constructor` and `__proto__` resolve through the prototype chain to inherited functions/objects, which are truthy, so `??` never takes over and downstream reads silently become `undefined` (this produced `NaN` node coordinates in the graph layout).

Errors shown to a user are always Chinese, and the translation happens only in the frontend — `frontend/app/errors.ts` is the single source. Backend `detail` strings stay as they are and are never rewritten to please the UI: they are the contract for MCP agents, logs, and triage. The layer is **deny by default, and trust comes from provenance rather than from the shape of the text**. This is the one rule to keep straight, because the obvious alternative is wrong: a backend `detail` that happens to be Chinese, short, and single-line is *not* evidence that it was written for a user. Roughly forty call sites raise `HTTPException(detail=str(exc))`, and their output is structurally indistinguishable from the twenty that carry deliberate user copy — an earlier version of this module trusted "4xx and contains a CJK character" and consequently displayed `403 访问被拒绝 — nginx/1.25 request id=req-1 upstream=10.0.0.7:8000`, internal address included.

So the backend declares provenance explicitly. `user_error(status, message)` in `backend/app/api/deps.py` raises an `HTTPException` carrying the header `X-User-Message: 1`, and only a response bearing that header may have its `detail` shown verbatim. The marker is a **header** precisely so that `detail`'s JSON type stays untouched for MCP and logs. Anything raised as a bare `HTTPException` — every `detail=str(exc)`, every f-string wrapping an exception — is generalized to a status-code message on the client, with the original text going only to `console.error`. Two consequences are easy to get wrong: the header must be listed in the CORS `expose_headers` in `app/main.py` or a cross-origin deployment silently loses every backend message (same-origin development, backend tests, and frontend tests with mock `Response` objects are all green while production is broken), and a new 4xx whose `detail` is a Chinese literal must use `user_error` — `backend/tests/test_user_error.py` AST-scans for that and fails the build otherwise.

Trust is checked before shape, and shape is still checked second. `humanizeHttpError(status, detail, trusted)` passes `detail` through only when `trusted` (read from the header by `readHttpError`) and `status < 500` and `isDisplayableUserText()` agrees the string looks like a sentence — not multi-line, not tag-bearing, not brace-bearing, at most 200 characters. That second gate no longer decides trust; it catches the case where `user_error` was applied to a string that had an exception spliced into it. `trusted` defaults to `false`, so forgetting the argument fails closed. 5xx is generalized regardless of the marker: `user_error` is for client-correctable 4xx, and a 5xx means the server broke. The **diagnostic** channel is unchanged — raw body, collapsed to one line and truncated once, to `console.error` only.

Everything that is caught rather than fetched goes through `toUserMessage(error, fallback)`, and that function **only recognizes a brand, never a shape**. `throwHumanizedHttpError` and `humanizedError(message)` stamp the errors this module has translated; `toUserMessage` returns a stamped message verbatim — preserving the 401/403/404/409 distinction instead of flattening every failure into one sentence — and replaces everything else with the caller's fallback, writing the discarded original to `console.error`. This matters because the largest failure class never reaches `throwHumanizedHttpError` at all: when `fetch` itself rejects there is no `Response` to read, and backend error strings frequently arrive as JSON fields rather than status codes — the ask stream's `error` event, a persisted `ask_job.error`, a report's `error`, a source's `error_message`, a readiness snapshot's `error`, `model_errors[].message`. The backend writes `f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"` into all of those, which is for logs and MCP, not for a person, and a shape-based rule let the Chinese-looking ones (`RuntimeError: 模型调用失败 upstream timeout`) straight through. The brand is a `Symbol.for(...)` rather than a module-local `Symbol` or an `instanceof` class check, because Next.js bundles the same module into both the server and client graphs and the module-local forms would produce two non-equal identities and thus false negatives; forgery is not a concern, since the values being screened are strings and JSON carries no symbols. Values that must not be rendered but must still be traceable go to `logDiagnostic(tag, value)`, which shares the HTTP path's truncation limit — never call it (or `toUserMessage`) during render, or it repeats on every re-render; log at an I/O boundary or in a `useEffect` keyed on the value.

Do not hand-roll an error branch. `frontend/tests/guards/errors-guard.test.mjs` recursively scans `frontend/app` and `frontend/features` and enforces three shapes: no `new Error(...)` whose argument interpolates `.status`; — because that first rule cannot see `setStatusText(\`服务异常：${err.message}\`)` — **no reads of `.message` at all** outside `errors.ts` unless the semantic site is reviewed; and, because that second rule could not see the backend's *diagnostic* fields either, **no reads of `.error` or `.error_message`** unless reviewed (`console.error` is exempt, being the channel those strings belong in). Reviewed entries are keyed by module path + qualified scope + access kind + target and carry a reasoned count; source positions and snippets are diagnostics only. Entries are limited to cases where the value is not raw exception text or is not displayed: a sentinel comparison, a condition, a state field already written by `toUserMessage`, or a value taken solely to hand to `logDiagnostic`. The guard also pins positively that `reportError`, the ask stream's `error` event, `job.error`, and `extractErrorMessage` (a thin alias of `toUserMessage` shared by ~20 knowhow call sites) still route through the layer.

**Anomaly severity tiers.** Small anomaly indicators attached to a source (parse failure, extraction gaps, missing paper metadata) are classified by *consequence*, not cause, into three severities: `integrity` (stored knowledge may be wrong or silently incomplete and usually cannot self-heal — red pill, `AlertOctagon`), `retrieval` (knowledge is intact but retrieval is degraded — usually one action fixes it — amber pill, `AlertTriangle`), and `info` (in-progress or expected state, not an anomaly at all — neutral grey, red/amber forbidden). `frontend/app/anomaly-severity.ts` (`sourceAnomalies()`) is the single classification entry point and `frontend/app/anomaly-badge.tsx` (`AnomalyBadge`) is the single render path, styled by the `.anomaly-badge--*` classes in `globals.css` off the `--danger`/`--danger-bg`/`--danger-border`, `--warning`/`--warning-bg`/`--warning-border` tokens plus `--warning-solid`. `--warning` is text-only (deliberately darkened to clear AA contrast on `--warning-bg`); `--warning-solid` keeps the original brighter amber for solid fills that predate this tiering (progress-bar segments, legend dots) — new anomaly copy never introduces a fourth amber. The answer-level "unbuilt retrieval index" banner is the same `retrieval` tier by consequence but is *not* source-attached: it does not flow through `sourceAnomalies`/`AnomalyBadge`, rendering instead as a dedicated `.answer-index-degraded` element that merely shares the retrieval-tier tokens. Any new anomaly small-print must add a mapping in `anomaly-severity.ts` and render through `AnomalyBadge`; a hand-rolled inline `style={{color: ...}}`, a bare `⚠`/`⛔` glyph, or a fresh ad-hoc color token is exactly the pre-tiering form this replaced and must not reappear — `frontend/tests/guards/anomaly-guard.test.mjs` is the regression gate, scoped to the tokens/patterns unique to the removed forms (it deliberately leaves the unrelated, still-legitimate `var(--color-warn, ...)` fallback used by the analytics modal and the retrieval-index status hint untouched). Compact rows (the source list) surface only the actionable tiers — `sourceAnomalies(source).filter(a => a.severity !== "info")` — so `info` (e.g. "待补全", "非论文") shows only in the source detail view, keeping narrow-screen rows from overflowing with badges that need no action.

**长任务按钮的忙碌态.** 凡是点一下就会触发长任务的按钮——排后台 job(体检修复的补齐向量/重新解析、KG 构建、补上关联、重新合并、检索索引构建)、同步等完的重活(来源重新解析、查重)、或大体积传输(上传文件)——点完必须**立刻**变成不可点,并把文案换成**按该动作语义**写的进行态(「补齐向量」→「补齐中…」、「重新解析」→「解析中…」、「重建索引」→「重建中…」),不用笼统的「处理中」。纯图标按钮没有文案可换,改用 `.busy-spin` 转圈图标并同步更新 `title`/`aria-label`。这不是锦上添花:`backfill-vectors`、`reparse` 这些端点后端**没有单飞守卫**,POST 立即返回而修复在后台跑,按钮不变化用户就会反复点,每点一次后端就再排一份同样的活。两种形态都合法——① `disabled={busy}` + 文案切换;② 忙碌时整排 CTA 换成「取消」或干脆不渲染(「索引与构建」正常态用的就是这种)——但**必须二选一**,不能什么都不做。忙碌位的解除要**按证据**而非按时间,且证据要按**修复的形状**选,不能一刀切(唯一真源是 `frontend/app/checkup-view.ts` 的 `repairRelease` / `isRepairing`,纯函数、`checkup-view.test.mjs` 有单测):一轮只修一批的(`reparse` 的样本上界 ≤20 篇)存「触发那一刻该组的 count」,count 一变就恢复可点,让用户接着修下一批;一次修全库的(`backfill_vectors`)**不能**用 count —— 看板 H4/H5 的口径排除活跃租约,job 逐源补齐时 count 就一路递减,拿它解锁会在 job 还剩大半时放行、又排一次全库补齐,正是 `backend/app/services/checkup.py` 里 H4/H5 注释点名要防的「并发 backfill 重复模型调用」;它只在该组从体检结果里消失时解除。忙碌位还必须随体检结果收敛(摘掉已不在结果里的分组键),否则旧条目会被后来重新出现的同名问题继承、把新卡片锁死。有界轮询窗口关闭与切库是共用兜底,绝不让按钮卡在禁用态。**按笔记本单飞的后台任务(「补上关联」「重新合并」)的忙碌位必须存「哪个库在忙」而不是一个裸布尔**:任务在服务端按 notebook 单飞,用户点完 A 完全可以切到 B 再点一次,裸布尔会让 B 的按钮跟着变灰、让 A 的轮询在 B 上重拉图谱并清掉 B 的展开节点、回到 A 又因为位已被清而永远等不到那次刷新。判据与解除同样抽成纯函数(`frontend/features/kg-maintenance/notebook-busy-set.ts` 的 `busyForNotebook` / `claimNotebookSlot` / `releaseNotebookClaim`——两个动作共用同一份实现,`kg-relink-status.ts` 只是按历史命名再导出一遍;`kg-relink-status.test.mjs` / `kg-rebuild-status.test.mjs` 单测),解除必须是「只清自己那一格」的函数式更新,不能无条件置空。**服务端也把这两件事放进同一个 per-notebook 单飞槽**(重新合并整表重写 `concept_clusters` 与板块划分,补上关联只是把关系边追加进这张聚类正在读的同一张图——两者共槽是因为并发不只是多花一份钱,而是一方会在另一方还在读的输入上发布结果,不是因为两者做的是同一件事),所以 409 必须点名真正占着槽的那个动作,而各自的 status 端点对**另一种**任务一律如实回 `idle`——否则一条轮询会替它没发起过的任务空等到上限。轮询另需**尝试上限**:服务端只在进程内记这件事,任务卡死时 status 会一直如实回报 running,没有上限按钮就永远解锁不了;超限走中性文案(任务可能仍在跑,不能说它失败了)。已知取舍(刻意保留、有反向护栏用例钉住):backfill 全失败时 H4/H5 不归零,按钮会锁到窗口结束(≤10 分钟)——因为 backfill 没有持久 job 状态,而提前放行的代价(重复付费模型调用)高于等待。同理,**补齐真的完成到 H4/H5 计数归零之间还可能再多等至多 30 秒**:那两个数走的是带 TTL 的进程内 memo(见上面体检那条),忙碌位因此可能比实际修复完成晚一个 TTL 才解除——同样是刻意接受的,提前放行的代价仍然更高。`disabled` 写了还不够:`.sort-button` / `.icon-button` / `.ghost-button` 都显式写死了 background/color,浏览器默认的 `:disabled` 变灰对它们无效,`globals.css` 里那条共用 `:disabled { opacity: .55 }` 是让禁用**看得见**的必要条件。`frontend/tests/guards/long-task-button-guard.test.mjs` 是回归门:它按 `onClick` 绑定的源码文本(不是行号)认出各长任务入口,断言每个都带非平凡的 `disabled`(`disabled={false}` 这类恒假值同样报红),并断言 `CHECKUP_FIX` 与 `CHECKUP_FIX_BUSY` 两张表逐键配对、进行态文案与静态文案逐条不同。守卫刻意不覆盖走形态②的那几颗(正常态的 `runScaleIndexOp("rebuild")`、看板里的 `startKgBuild`/`startKgRebuild`、以及看板里那颗 `confirmRefreshUnifiedKg`——它与知识图谱视图里同名的那颗 onClick 源码逐字相同、分不开,而两颗走的是**两种**合法形态,加进去只会把形态②判成违规;后者的忙碌位改由 `kg-rebuild-wiring-guard.test.mjs` 按源码语义钉住),也不覆盖**新增**的长任务入口——后者的兜底是本节约定与人工评审,不是这份测试。

**Markdown 单波浪线不是删除线.** 渲染**模型产出文本**的 GFM 插件真源只有 `frontend/app/markdown-gfm.ts` 的 `remarkGfmPlugin`——`[remarkGfm, { singleTilde: false }]`,由 Ask 回答/Memory 卡片(`answer-markdown.tsx`)、深度报告(`report-view.tsx`)、报告公开分享页(`app/r/[token]/page.tsx`)与问答会话公开分享页(`app/c/[token]/page.tsx`)共用,调用方不得再传裸 `remarkGfm`。理由是部署环境实测到的静默损坏:remark-gfm 4.0.1 的删除线扩展默认 `singleTilde: true`(github.com 网页版行为,GFM 规范本身并不允许),于是**单个** `~` 就是删除线定界符。中文技术答案里区间 `7~5nm`/`80~90%`/`0.8~1.1V`/`2~3 周` 与约等于 `~5W`/`~3GHz` 密度极高,同段出现两个词内 `~` 就配成一对,中间整段正文被 `<del>` 划掉(实测「工艺节点为 7~5nm,良率约 80~90%,因此需要评估。」→ 划掉「~5nm,良率约 80~」);没有任何报错,只是答案里凭空多出一条中划线。关掉后单个 `~` 恢复字面量,而规范的 `~~删除线~~` 逐字不变。**唯一豁免是 knowhow 格子预览**(`knowhow-cell-editor.tsx` 仍用裸 `remarkGfm`):它与 `backend/app/services/knowhow/md_normalize.py` 是一对孪生实现,两侧的跨行强调配对都按「单 `~` 是删除线」判定「规整会不会拆断渲染」,单方面改渲染器会让那套护栏与实际渲染口径分叉(方向变保守、不损坏数据,但论证失真),要连孪生逻辑一起改,是独立一件事。回归门 `frontend/tests/guards/markdown-single-tilde-guard.test.mjs` 三条:任何 `<ReactMarkdown>` 的 `remarkPlugins` 不得出现裸 `remarkGfm`(或未带 `{ singleTilde: false }` 的元组)、真源自己必须写死该选项、除真源与豁免外生产代码不得直接 import `remark-gfm`(否则新渲染面可以不经 JSX 绕过前两条);三个模型文本渲染面必须都被扫到,重命名/搬走时守卫要响而不是静默缩水。行为侧另有 `frontend/tests/component/answer-markdown-tilde.component.test.tsx` 走真组件真管线。

**Graded-effort控件.** 产品里有两个「五档强度」选择:深度报告的「研究深度」与逐步推理的「检索档位」。它们档名相同(概览/标准/深入/详尽/穷尽),因此必须是**同一个控件**——`frontend/app/effort-picker.tsx` 的 `EffortPicker` 是唯一实现:一个 chip(前缀词 + 当前档名)加向上弹出的 popover(标题 + 当前档名 + 均布圆点滑块 + 该档一句中性说明),点外部/Esc 收起,转入禁用态自动收起。调用方只提供 `{id, label, hint}` 档位表、当前值与 `onChange`;`compact` 只压 chip 尺寸以嵌进问答输入控制行,popover 两处完全一致。档位的**数值阈值不上屏**:界面只表达档名与那句说明,精确上限是 `docs/product-and-api*.md` 的契约表(以及 `frontend/app/ask-retrieval-effort.ts` ⇄ `backend/app/core/ask_retrieval_policy.py` 的双向锁定),不再以 title/折叠块铺在控件上。popover 默认右对齐控件,但打开时会量一次并夹回最近的水平裁剪祖先(问答那侧是 `.workspace-panel { overflow: hidden }`)与视口的交集内,resize 时重算。这不是防御性写法:模式标签折行会把 chip 推到行首,右对齐的 232px popover 实测溢出面板 90px 被裁;而 `right: 0` 与 `left: 0` 各自只在一段宽度里成立(前者在面板 < ~700px 失效,后者在 ~700–930px 失效),所以只能量了再夹。夹取算术在 `frontend/app/effort-picker-logic.ts`(纯函数,`effort-picker-logic.test.mjs` 单测四个分支),量尺寸与写样式留在组件里(`effort-picker.component.test.tsx` 桩掉 rect 验接线)。宽松时首选位不动,宽屏观感与报告那侧保持一致。新增第三个强度选择要复用 `EffortPicker` 而不是再造一套平铺 chip;`frontend/tests/unit/effort-picker.test.mjs` 是回归门,除了检查两个调用点都消费共享控件,还断言 `<input type="range">` 只存在于 `effort-picker.tsx`——把滑块复制回任一调用点(即便共享控件仍在)就报红。

**群组界面的版式.** 现行群组管理入口是独立页 `frontend/app/groups-page.tsx`；旧 `frontend/app/groups-panel.tsx` 不再路由，只保留为兼容遗留。独立页与 notebook 内分享界面的「共享给群组」一节 (`frontend/app/notebook-group-share.tsx`) 复用 `globals.css` 的系统色板、边框、圆角、排版和基础 `.group-*` 语义。桌面版采用左侧群组导航与右侧工作区，窄屏按现有断点自然折叠；Notebook、成员、申请、设置四区不得靠临时内联样式拼装。旧组件仍遵守以下四条兼容不变量:

1. **行的横向布局由 `.group-row` 自己给出,不靠调用点的内联样式**。这两处的行原来是块级的 `.checklist-row` 加内联 `style={{ alignItems: "center", gap: 8 }}` —— 这两个属性在块级盒子上一个字都不生效,于是「组名 + 分类 + 人数 + 角色 + 按钮」连成一条没有间距的文字(真机形态:「notebook项目1 人组管理员 已展开」)。`.checklist-row` 本身不动:它另有几处按块级文字行使用的调用点(上传弹窗、分享预览的来源清单),给它加 `display:flex` 会改掉那些。行内的整句说明用 `.group-row-note`(可换行),短元数据用 `.group-row-meta`(`nowrap`,「3 人」这类不该被折断)。
2. **只读标签用 `.group-chip`,不用 `.new-pill`**。后者是 42px 高的实心黑主按钮;拿它渲染分类/状态标签,会让一个点不动的标签比旁边真正能点的按钮还重。危险动作(删除群组)仍用 `.new-pill.danger-pill`,它确实是按钮。
3. **详情是「清单里被展开的那一条」,不是清单之后的又一节**。它包在 `.group-detail` 卡里、有自己的标题栏(组名是 `h3`,不是 `.section-title` —— 那个类带 `text-transform: uppercase`,会把用户起的组名整块大写成「NOTEBOOK」),清单里对应的那一行同时标 `.is-active`,展开后把卡带进视口(`scrollIntoView` 在 jsdom 里是 `undefined` 而不是 no-op,调用前必须探测)。清单里那颗按钮的可见文案仍是「查看／已展开」,但无障碍名字带上组名(一屏里它会重复很多次),再点一次收起。
4. **行内动作比页面主 CTA 小一号**(34px,对齐 `.catalog-card-actions` 的既有做法):42px 会把每一行撑成两倍高,而这些都是局部动作。

`frontend/tests/guards/group-layout-guard.test.mjs` 是回归门(按 JSX 结构与 CSS 规则体判定,不含行号),覆盖上面四条里可形态化的部分:`.group-row` 仍是 flex、两个文件都不再用 `.checklist-row`/内联 `alignItems`、`.new-pill` 只出现在 `<button>` 上、`groups-panel.tsx` 不再出现 `.section-title`。它**不**检查具体间距数值(那是设计取舍,不是不变量)。

**只读/群组共享库的顶栏身份行.** `ReaderNotebookBadge` 那一行由 `globals.css` 的 `.reader-badge-row` 排版,在固定 72px 顶栏下**恒不换行**。「恒单行」的**前提**就是那个固定高度:≤760px 时 `.workspace-header` 本来就是 `height: auto`,前提不成立,那里必须放开换行并让标题 `flex-basis: 100%` 独占一行——不换行反而会把标题挤到 43px(实测,320px 视口,「返回主页」+ 徽章 +「退出共享」三者相加),26px 字号连一个半字都放不下。守卫把这两件事绑在一起:基础规则必须 nowrap,窄屏块里必须同时有 auto 高度与 wrap。**紧凑桌面宽度(≤1200px)不在顶栏渲染「退出共享」**:它固定 ~98px 且不缩,是那一段里把标题挤没的那一个(1000px 视口实测 37px→105px,与群组形态持平);能力没丢——同一动作在笔记本卡片菜单里一直都在。刻意不改 `.workspace-title` 的 flex-basis:那会改变**所有**工作区(含 owner)的顶栏左右分配,并让 800px 上的工具栏开始横向滚动,为一个只读形态的边角付全局代价。它此前用 `.tag-row`(`flex-wrap: wrap`),而 `.workspace-header` 是固定 72px 单行——标题 + 徽章 + 一句长说明换成三行、在 72px 里垂直居中,标题那一行被推到可视区**之上**(静态量过:内容高 141px vs 容器 72px,标题 top 在 header top 之上),于是群组共享库点进去整份库名一个像素都看不见,而说明文字漏到标题栏外面盖住下方内容。三条约定:①标题用 `.reader-badge-title`——`width: auto` / `max-width: none` 显式解开 `.notebook-title-input` 的 `width: 100%`(那个值在 owner 顶栏里是对的,它在那儿独占整行),加 `min-width: 0` 与省略号,保证被压缩的是标题而不是徽章;②身份标注是**状态**不是主操作,用轻量的 `.reader-badge-chip`,不拿 42px 实心黑主按钮 `.new-pill` 冒充(它摆在 26px 库名旁边会把主角本身盖过去);③身份解释只进 tooltip,顶栏不为它占一行——「要停止访问请联系组管理员撤销共享」这类指引尤其不留:群组共享本来就没有自助退出(那正是 `!granted` 才渲染「退出共享」的原因),把一条按不动的操作指引常驻在标题旁既占地方又帮不上忙(用户裁决 2026-08-20)。回归门 `frontend/tests/guards/reader-badge-layout-guard.test.mjs` 在 CSS 里钉「不换行 + 可省略」(jsdom 没有排版引擎,量不到那 141px),`frontend/tests/component/notebook-reader-actions.component.test.tsx` 在真渲染里钉结构前提(库名在、容器是 `.reader-badge-row` 而不是 `.tag-row`、行内没有 `.tool-hint`)。

**访问权变动之后必须连当前工作区一起对账.** 独立群组页从工作区顶栏也打得开,而退出群组、被移出群组、组被删除、共享被撤销都可能把用户正站在里面的那本库从脚下抽走。只重取 `notebooks` 清单是不够的:工作区渲染自 `currentNotebook` 这份**另一个** state,清单变了它一动不动,人会继续待在一本自己已经读不到的库里,整屏毫无反应,只能手动刷新。`page.tsx` 的 `reconcileOpenNotebook(remaining)` 是唯一实现,「退出只读共享」(`handleLeaveShared`)与独立群组页的 `onChanged`(`refreshAfterAccessChange`)共用它——分开写会让其中一条在后续改动里悄悄退化回「只刷列表」。判据的**主语**是 `activeNotebookIdRef`(此刻真正装载着哪一本),不是渲染期从 state 抄下来的 `currentNotebookIdRef`——后者在一次切库落地之前还指着上一本库,「弹窗还在飞时用户关掉它、点开另一本」这条时序里会让对账读到刚被撤销的旧库,反过来把用户自己发起的导航作废掉(世代闸挡不住:那次导航发起在世代快照之前,两边相等)。`null` 恰好同时表示「导航在飞」与「人在集合页」,两种都是「无库可对账」,直接返回。**「还在清单里」≠「什么都没变」**:撤掉一条边之后访问权还在,但档位可能降了(组管理员被移出那个组、只剩另一个组的只读边,`can_manage_content`/`granted_via` 都变),所以库还在时必须调既有的 `refreshActiveNotebook()` 刷一次详情——工作区渲染自 `currentNotebook` 这份独立 state,不刷就会继续亮着一整屏写入口而每次写都在 API 上被拒;刻意不拿列表行去合并(列表投影里 `document_limit` 是 0 哨兵、`paper_meta_missing` 恒 null,合进详情会把它们改坏),也刻意不先比字段再决定要不要刷。判据的**谓语**是「重取回来的清单里还有没有它」而不是「刚才那个动作针对的是不是它」:同一本库可以经多条路进来(另一个群组、直接成员、`everyone` 授权边),退掉其中一条并不必然失去访问,而清单是服务端算过权限的那一份,照它走既不误踢也不漏踢。兜底导航**自己**失败(瞬时抖动)时必须退回集合页并说明一句:`openNotebook` 一进门就把 `activeNotebookIdRef` 置成 null,而屏幕上还留着那本读不到的库,就地抛出去会让之后每一次复核都在「无库可对账」上直接返回,人被永久钉在陈旧工作区里、只能手动导航才出得来。落点沿用既有那套:优先跳到自己的第一本库,一本都没有才回集合页,并 `replaceState` 顶掉旧的 `#notebook=<id>`,否则那条历史条目还活着、按返回键会撞上一本已经 403 的库。重取清单另有一道**发布闸**:比的是 `notebookListPublishedRef`(已发布那次的发起序号),**不是**「发起序号还等不等于最新」——后者是「发起即占位」,一次**失败**的复核自己什么都不发布,却把并发的那次成功加载一起作废掉,初次加载因此可能永远停在空清单上;失败的请求不推进水位,所以它谁也吞不掉。发起序号 `notebookListSeqRef` 仍在,但只用来回答另一个问题「期间有没有更晚发起的请求」(有就把对账让给它,并因为它可能是只刷列表的 `loadNotebookCollection` 而重来一次),**每一个** `setNotebooks` 的路径都要过——`loadNotebookCollection` 把 `listNotebooks()` 与更慢的 health/config 放在同一个 `Promise.all` 里,它的清单可以在撤销之前取回、却被慢请求拖到撤销之后才落地,把已撤销的卡片复活;输掉竞态的那次访问权复核会**重来一次**(只一次),因为赢家只刷列表、不做访问权对账:两次复核会叠在一起(切回标签页一次、弹窗里的动作又一次),而先发的那次可以后回,旧响应会用 `setNotebooks` 把撤销前的快照盖回去——工作区已经正确跳走了,列表里那本读不到的库却又活过来。`navEpoch` 挡的是导航、挡不住这个,是两件事。因此 `handleLeaveShared` 必须调同一个收口(`refreshAfterAccessChange`),不得自己再抄一份重取——抄一份就会漏掉其中一道闸。**远端撤销另算**:别人把你移出群组、删掉群组或撤销共享时本浏览器收不到任何事件,没有推送通道,只能在标签页**重新可见**时复用同一条对账路径复核(节流 `ACCESS_REVALIDATE_MIN_INTERVAL_MS`;`listNotebooks()` 抛出时整条对账不执行,一次网络抖动绝不能把人赶出他还有权限的库)。这是尽力而为**不是保证**:一直停在前台不动的标签页仍要等下一次交互撞 403,面向用户的文档必须按这个口径写,不能承诺「当场」(codex #529 R3)。回归门 `frontend/tests/guards/group-sharing-guard.test.mjs`。

## 界面词汇表 (User-Facing Vocabulary)

Copy shown to users — JSX text, `label`/`title`/`placeholder`/`aria-label`, toasts, errors, table headers — uses only the "interface word", never the internal implementation term. **界面词 ≠ 内部词**: `projection`/`tier`/`canonical`/`chunk`/`KG` and friends keep their original names in code, types, comments, and the architecture docs; only strings rendered to the user get rewritten.

| 内部 / 黑话（界面文案里不得出现） | 界面词 |
|---|---|
| 基准库 / 基准语料 / 底层库 (base) / 权威参考层 | 公共知识库 |
| 个人层 | 个人知识库 |
| notebook / Notebook（散文中） | 笔记本 |
| 建图 / 构建·建立知识图谱（作动作） | 整理（知识图谱） |
| 入图 / 未入图 | 已分析 / 待分析 / 已分析·无知识 |
| 抽取 / 补抽 / 重抽 | 分析 / 分析新增 / 全部重新分析 |
| 向量检索索引 / CSR 图 / ANN / 暴力检索 | 索引（整句重写，如「建立快速查找结构」；小库说「直接搜索已够快」） |
| TopN / Top-N | 最相关的若干条（档位说明一律只讲效果，不讲取数方式） |
| chunk / chunks | 段 |
| 节点 / 知识节点（散文，非图谱技术上下文） | 知识对象 / 知识条目（**不可统一降格为「概念」**，见下） |
| 边 / 关系边（散文） | 关联 |
| 投影 / 投影产物 / 重建投影（knowhow） | 同步 / 重新同步 |
| LLM 预审 / 预审 | 自动判重 |
| 去重 | 合并重复 |
| 晋升（用户侧）：动作 / 状态 / 队列 | 贡献到公共知识库 / 已收录 / 内容审核 |
| 孤立节点 / 补连边 | 没建立关联的内容 / 补上关联 |
| 边审 / 边审查队列 | 关系审核 / 关系审核队列 |
| Memory（残留英文散文） | 记忆 |
| schema（散文） | 内容类型 / 抽取字段（图谱对象类型/字段管理功能的界面名放行为「图谱 Schema」，见下） |
| deprecated（toast 直出） | 已弃用 |
| community / 社区 | 主题板块 |
| canonical 簇 / cluster | 合并后的知识对象 |
| outlier source | 关联稀疏的来源 |
| centrality | 核心度（批 1 未渲染，见下） |
| 画像 / 底座画像（agent_notebook_profile 共享层） / 巡固（consolidation job） | AI 对这个库的理解 |
| 画像 / 覆盖层画像（agent_notebook_profile 个人层） | 我的检索心得 |
| 观察队列（observation, agent_observations，Agentic Memory P3） | Agent 记录 |
| 回答风格偏好（search profile, user_profiles.search_profile_json，Agentic Memory P3） | 我的回答偏好 |
| consult_memory（模型主动拉取检索经验的 reflect 动作／trace 步，Agentic Memory P4） | 回想 |

**图谱质量分析视图（批 1）的四行补记**：「合并后的知识对象」同时覆盖内部计量单位代号
`canonical`（按节点数）与 `clusters`（按簇数）——两者在这份报告里数的是同一种东西，映射到
同一个界面词。「核心度」对应 `communities.centrality`；批 1 没有把它渲染到任何界面元素上
（图谱质量分析视图头部只展示对象构成、收敛率、主题板块地图、关联稀疏的来源、板块俯瞰
图），此处只是提前登记界面词，不代表用户现在能在界面上看到这个数字。另有一组**产物新鲜
度口径代号**（`basis`，见 `frontend/app/kg-analysis-model.ts` 的 `BASIS_LABEL`，同屏并列
数字靠它分辨为什么对不上，设计 §3.3）：`usable_live` → 「整理当时的实时口径」、
`community_snapshot` → 「上次主题板块划分」、`unified_rebuild_snapshot` → 「上次整理时
的规模」。

**「概念」不是图谱对象的统称。** 图谱是**对象级**的：内置 `concept` / `claim` / `formula` / `procedure` 四型（真源 `extraction_profiles.OBJECT_TYPE_LABELS`），外加 knowhow 表以列名生成的自定义 `object_type`。「概念」只是其中**一种**类型的界面名（`概念 Concept`），把计数、引用锚点、入图提示统称为「概念」等于把其余类型降格，用户按图索骥时对不上。统称一律用**知识对象**（强调它在图谱里是个可定位的东西）或**知识条目**（强调它是一条知识内容），按语境择一。knowhow 侧尤其注意：`概念` 在那里另有所指（anchor 分组，见 `knowhow-matrix-drawer.tsx` 的徽章），复用会撞义。

**「图谱 Schema」是 schema 行放行的唯一复合短语。** 图谱对象类型与字段管理功能（原顶层导航「内容类型」，现已并入知识图谱视图的头部入口，成员可查看、owner 可维护当前笔记本定义、管理员可另管全局基线）的界面名，经产品决策（2026-07-23）定为「图谱 Schema」——EDA / 芯片场景下用户反而熟悉 "Schema" 一词。守卫 `check_ui_vocabulary.py` 的 `SANCTIONED_UI` 只放行「图谱 Schema」这一个短语（匹配前像剥 `${…}` 插值一样先剥掉），**裸 `schema` / 裸 `Schema`（不带「图谱」前缀）仍照抓**；`backend/tests/test_ui_vocabulary_guard.py::test_图谱Schema放行但裸schema仍抓` 钉住这个放行的窄度。这是全表唯一保留英文单词做界面词的例外。

**刻意保留、不要误杀**：**知识图谱**（用户词典里的词，只杀 KG / 建图 / 入图 等缩写变体）、**索引**（书后索引式心智模型，只杀 CSR / ANN / 暴力检索 修饰）、**「知识库」作 Knowledge tab 名**（`workspace-model.ts` 的 `CHAT_MODES`；lint 分不清 tab 名与误用，故不进黑名单）、**裸「节点」/「边」**（图谱视图里画出来的就是节点与边，属表中说的「图谱技术上下文」；且「边」与旁边 / 边框 / 边距同形，lint 判不了——故黑名单只收无歧义的复合形态：孤立节点 / 补连边 / 关系边 / 边审。散文里的裸用靠人工对照本表把关）。

`scripts/check_ui_vocabulary.py`（挂在 `scripts/check.sh`）执行上表，命中即失败。它的**作用域跟着信任边界走，不跟着目录走**：既扫 `frontend/app` 的渲染文本，也扫后端 `user_error(status, "…")` 的消息字面量——`api/deps.py` 只给这批 4xx `detail` 打 `X-User-Message: 1`，而前端 `errors.ts` 是 deny-by-default、见到这个标记就把 detail **原样上屏**；打标记等于声明「这是给人看的文案」，那就同样受本表约束（曾经按目录划作用域，于是「仅管理员可设置基准库」「仅管理员可管理晋升队列」四条 403 一路上屏而守卫全绿）。裸 `HTTPException(detail=str(exc))` 刻意不在扫描面内——它永远不上屏，detail 是诊断 / MCP 契约，那条分界由 `backend/tests/test_user_error.py` 守。它是**词黑名单而非语义检查**——只在含中文的单元里匹配，且剥离注释 / 标识符 / `${…}`·`{…}` 与 f-string 插值，故 `id: "chunk"`、`currentNotebook` 等不会误报；也因此不声称全覆盖，新增界面文案仍须人工对照上表把关。
## No Docker In First Version

Do not add Docker or Docker Compose as the default first-version workflow.

Local beta should run directly on a local Python environment and the local Next.js frontend.

Docker can be introduced later only when the user asks for deployment packaging or when the project moves beyond the local beta workflow.

## Dependency Policy

- Treat the project's isolated Python environment (with `backend/requirements.txt` installed) as canonical; activate it or set `PYTHON_BIN`.
- Keep backend dependencies compatible with Python 3.13+. The SQLite write lock's fairness
  depends on CPython 3.13's `PyMutex`-backed `threading.Lock` handoff (see
  `backend/app/repositories/sqlite/database.py`); on older interpreters the lock silently
  reverts to barging and writer starvation returns with no error.
- Default backend requirements should not require PostgreSQL, pgvector, or SQLAlchemy while SQLite is the local baseline.
- Keep dependency setup reproducible from `backend/requirements.txt`.
- Ask for approval before installing new packages when network or environment mutation is required.

## Current Backend Baseline

The backend has been exercised on:

```text
Python 3.13.11
fastapi
uvicorn
pydantic
openai
python-multipart
python-docx      # DOCX 兜底最后一环
mammoth          # DOCX 兜底首选：Word 样式 → 语义化 HTML
python-pptx      # PPTX 兜底首选：形状/表格/图表标题/备注
pypdf
pymupdf4llm
markdown-it-py   # 结构化 Markdown 解析
numpy            # float32 向量矩阵检索
openpyxl         # XLSX 解析
xlrd             # 旧版二进制 .xls 解析（MinerU 不支持该容器，无优先分支，唯一读取器）
```

If any dependency is missing, install `backend/requirements.txt` into the active environment.

## LLM Configuration

Chat, embedding, and rerank services are deployment-owned. `MODEL_SERVICES_CONFIG` points to a TOML file whose `[services]` entries declare protocol, endpoint, model, `api_key_env`, the physical service's sole capacity setting `max_concurrency`, and an optional chat-only fixed `top_p`; `[bindings]` maps every stable workload id from `model_registry.py` to a compatible service. A configured `top_p` overrides per-call defaults and must be included in the effective request/cache identity. Secrets live only in `.env` variables referenced by `api_key_env`. Do not restore role-specific endpoint/model/key variables or any user-owned model setting.

The process watches a non-empty `MODEL_SERVICES_CONFIG` and hot-reloads changed TOML. The watcher must observe the same changed file signature twice, parse it, and verify that the post-read signature still matches before atomic publish, so an in-place truncate/write save cannot publish a moving snapshot; explicit forced reload skips the two-observation delay but retains the post-read check. An empty/comment-only configured TOML is rejected during reload rather than interpreted as offline mode: clear the setting and restart for that explicit transition. Invalid, missing, or half-written replacements keep the last valid configuration, and stable invalid versions emit a bounded credential-safe diagnostic. New calls resolve the new binding/runtime generation; already-submitted work completes on its original generation. Model status reads the live registry rather than retaining the startup snapshot. Do not make business services cache a raw physical client or bypass this reload boundary.

`RuntimeModelProvider` is the process-owned access boundary. Business code requests a stable workload adapter from it; the provider resolves workload → physical service, submits through that service's one `ServiceScheduler`, then and only then invokes the reviewed raw transport. No application service, repository, batch phase, probe, or compatibility property may construct or expose a raw chat/embed/rerank client, call a provider transport directly, or introduce a second semaphore/executor as a nominal global cap.

Per physical service, `ServiceScheduler` enforces `max_concurrency`, a total queue cap of `10 × max_concurrency`, a per-actor queued cap of `2 × max_concurrency`, fixed priority dispatch `interactive:report:background = 8:2:1`, and round-robin actors inside each priority lane. Queue deadlines are 30 seconds for interactive work, 300 seconds for report work, and 1800 seconds for background work. One fatal provider result or three consecutive transient results open the service breaker for 30 seconds; half-open admits exactly one probe. Separate service ids have separate capacity, queues, and breakers.

The supported production topology is one backend process (`uvicorn --workers 1`). All model services remain URL-based; the backend neither loads nor starts inference models. MinerU is a separate parser adapter and retains its supported remote `http`, isolated `cli`, and PyMuPDF4LLM `off` modes (pypdf last resort). An empty `MODEL_SERVICES_CONFIG` intentionally enables deterministic/offline fallbacks; a non-empty invalid configuration fails startup.

`EMBED_DIM` must match the bound embedding model. Timeouts, retries, output budgets, embedding batch size, KG source-job concurrency, adaptive window sizing, and local ANN/CPU thread counts are workload/compute tuning only; none may override `max_concurrency`.

Legacy deployment migration is owned by `scripts/migrate_legacy_model_env.py`, and release packaging must include and execute-test it with the bundled `.venv/bin/python`. It must remain dry-run by default. `--apply` must back up the input and any replaced output, atomically write a credential-free TOML, rewrite only managed model assignments, preserve unrelated `.env` content, and never print secrets. It may replace an existing output without `--force` only when that file is byte-identical to the checked-in example. Keep its role fallbacks and physical-service deduplication aligned with the retired configuration behavior, including every former rewrite-client workload, derive bindings from the registry's stable workload ids, and validate generated output through `SystemModelServiceRegistry`. Capacity inference is only a migration starting point and may be explicitly overridden per legacy role; runtime must still have exactly one `max_concurrency` on each physical TOML service. Native DashScope rerank remains a supported migration target for old `RERANK_API_STYLE=dashscope` deployments.

### Model Service Status and Diagnostics

- `GET /api/model-services/status` is read-only and must never probe upstream services. It returns sanitized system service identity, workload bindings, configured capacity, running/queued counts, breaker state, and last-known health. It must never expose endpoints, credentials, provider payloads, source/prompt/response text, IPs, or raw exceptions.
- The Model Services UI is configuration-free. Ordinary users can inspect status only. Admins may explicitly probe one configured service with `POST /api/admin/model-services/{service_id}/test` or all configured services with `POST /api/admin/model-services/test-all`; status reads and page loads never auto-probe.
- Every scheduled model call receives a safe `support_id`. Client-visible model failures identify a sanitized service/model label plus that id; users give the id to maintenance staff, who correlate it with server logs and service status. Logs may retain operational provider diagnostics but must never log credentials or full private prompt/response bodies through scheduler/status events.
- Health observations are deployment-service-scoped and written monotonically by occurrence time. Manual probe and observed runtime outcomes share the same service identity/fingerprint guard, so stale completions cannot overwrite a replacement configuration; on an exact occurrence tie, observed failure wins over manual success.
- Schema v24 irreversibly rewrites every stored `user_profiles.model_settings` value to `{}`, deletes legacy per-user model health rows, and stores current health by deployment service id. Personal model-settings APIs, draft tests, save/edit controls, and configuration pages are retired and must not reappear.

## Logging / Observability

Structured logs go to `.local/logs/*.jsonl` (gitignored) plus brief console lines, via a single `EventLogger` (`backend/app/core/event_logging.py`). Add observability at the existing chokepoints, not at each call site:

- `requests.jsonl` — HTTP middleware in `app/main.py` (method/path/status/latency/`request_id`; slow calls flagged via `SLOW_REQUEST_MS`; `X-Request-Id` response header).
- `events.jsonl` — source pipeline in `SQLiteRepository.process_source` / `_set_source_status` (per-stage timings + status transitions + failure stack).
- `llm.jsonl` — `LLMInteractionLogger` wrapping `OpenAICompatibleClient` (`app/core/llm.py`); chat detailed, embeddings summarized, errors recorded.

Rules: reuse `EventLogger` for any new structured log (it handles JSONL append + console + never raising); never log raw embedding vectors; chat prompt/response are truncated to `LLM_LOG_MAX_CHARS`. `/dev/logs`'s **Model calls** tab is the original single-purpose LLM-record viewer, unchanged, backed by `/api/debug/logs/...`; that API is opt-in because full LLM records may contain prompt/response text from private sources, and stays gated behind `DEBUG_LOGS_ENABLED=true`. Config env vars: `LLM_LOG_ENABLED`, `LLM_LOG_PATH`, `LLM_LOG_MAX_CHARS`, `EVENT_LOG_ENABLED`, `EVENT_LOG_DIR`, `SLOW_REQUEST_MS`, `DEBUG_LOGS_ENABLED`, `USER_ACTIVITY_VIEW_ENABLED` — keep `.env.example` aligned.

`/dev/logs` defaults to a second, separate tab, **Activity**, that answers "what did this user do" rather than "what did the model do": asks, sources, and reports for one user merged into a single newest-first timeline. Both tabs share one top scope bar (viewed user, date range). Activity is three columns — Scope (the viewed user's own notebooks with source/ask/report counts, each expandable to that notebook's source list; selecting a notebook filters the stream, selecting a source only opens Detail, it does not filter the stream), Activity stream (the merged feed, `(created_at DESC, id DESC)` keyset-paginated, default page 50 capped at 200, a native date picker plus "全部时间"/all-time driving a half-open `[since, until)` window), and Detail (full Q&A plus reasoning trace for an ask, reusing the existing read-only Ask-panel renderers; parse status and derived diagnostics for a source). It is served by three reads in `admin_routes.py` — `GET /admin/users/{user_id}/activity`, `GET /admin/users/{user_id}/notebooks/{notebook_id}/sources`, `GET /admin/users/{user_id}/asks/{job_id}` — gated by self-or-admin (mirroring `debug_logs._resolve_owner`'s owner-or-admin check) plus a dedicated deploy-time switch, `USER_ACTIVITY_VIEW_ENABLED` (default **true**, since Activity is the default tab — gating it behind the default-off `DEBUG_LOGS_ENABLED` would 404 the default tab on every ordinary deployment); independent of `DEBUG_LOGS_ENABLED`, which continues to gate only `/api/debug/logs/...` behind the **Model calls** tab. `GET /system/config`'s `SystemConfiguration` response carries this switch as `user_activity_view_enabled` — the only way the frontend can learn a deploy-time value — and `/dev/logs` hides the Activity tab and defaults `view` to `llm` (normalizing away a `?view=activity` deep link too) when it reads `false`; a missing field (older backend) is treated as `true` rather than hiding an available tab. `GET /admin/users/{user_id}/notebooks` (reused by the Scope column to list the viewed user's notebooks) is likewise self-or-admin, not admin-only — a normal user viewing their own activity must be able to read their own notebook list. All three activity types are owner-only (the viewed user's own, non-copying notebooks) — the same convention as the admin usage overview's owner-only expanded inventory, even though that page's aggregate totals include joined shared-notebook submissions — and hidden synthetic sources (`memory`/`knowhow`) are excluded from both the source list and the stream. `list_user_notebooks`'s per-notebook report count is likewise scoped to `created_by = user_id` (mirroring the `questions` aggregate's existing predicate and the Activity stream's own report entries): a report created by another writable member of a shared notebook no longer counts toward the owner's Scope-column header, which previously disagreed with the stream (that entry never appeared there). `since`/`until`/the pagination cursor all resolve through one shared `parse_activity_instant` helper (`app/core/activity_time.py`) used by both database backends: a malformed bound raises rather than silently widening the window; SQLite compares on an absolute instant via `julianday()` (mixed naive/offset-aware `created_at` text — a naive value is read as UTC, matching how SQLite's own date functions treat naive strings); PostgreSQL compares natively on `timestamptz`. A source activity entry never carries the raw `error_message` (mirrors `ScopedSourceDetail`'s existing redaction, exposing a derived `parse_failed` boolean instead). `SourceSummary` gained an additive `display_title` field — paper title preferred, the one `source_display_title` implementation citation cards already use — leaving `title` untouched, so the existing Sources sidebar has zero regression. A report activity entry's duration reuses the Deep Report rule verbatim (`generation_started_at → updated_at`); unfinished reports show only their creation time and reports missing the start stamp show no duration. Deliberately not built yet, as a P2/P3 sequencing choice rather than a silent gap: correlating a model call to the ask/report that triggered it (needs a new write-side `_log_context` `ContextVar` threaded through `EventLogger`/`LLMInteractionLogger`, and cannot be backfilled onto history recorded before it ships); surfacing `events.jsonl`'s `ask_stage`/`pipeline` per-stage timings in ask/source Detail; clicking a source narrowing the Activity stream instead of only opening Detail; paginating a notebook's source list past its first page. Design rationale: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-04-user-activity-log-view-design_zh.md`.

Production DFX is a binding internal-infrastructure contract. The supported production shape is Ubuntu 24.04 started from the repository with `npm run start` and one Uvicorn worker. Production start installs `backend/requirements.txt` into `PYTHON_BIN`'s environment and recreates frontend dependencies from `package-lock.json` with `npm ci` before building; only explicitly prebuilt deployments may use `SKIP_INSTALL=1`. It rejects occupied ports from listener rows even when their PIDs are hidden, launches terminal-independent background frontend/backend processes, and exits immediately without probing either service; operators own the subsequent `/api/ready` and frontend checks. Interruptions before the launcher hands off both children send SIGTERM to both direct children together, wait one bounded grace window, SIGKILL survivors, and reap both; `npm run stop` owns normal shutdown after handoff. Operators run `python3 scripts/diag.py incident` **during** a visible hang, retrying with `--pid <backend-pid>` only when automatic discovery cannot select the already-running worker. The unified dispatcher has exactly seven commands: `incident`, `slow`, `latency`, `locks`, `open`, `db`, and `base-recall`; bare invocation remains `slow`. Existing standalone engine scripts and their old operator/cron paths must keep working, but do not reintroduce retired commands.

The default `incident` result must remain one copyable UTF-8 block no larger than 32 KiB, use one deadline capped at 10 seconds, and rank no more than three evidence-backed hypotheses while preserving explicit missing/degraded evidence instead of inventing a cause. The runtime atomically refreshes `.local/diagnostics/runtime.json` every two seconds; a heartbeat older than six seconds is stale. `SIGUSR1` is a non-terminating all-Python-thread faulthandler dump with no local-variable capture. `.local/diagnostics/thread-dumps.log` retention remains bounded to 8 MiB after successful capture, and temporary DB snapshots remain bounded beneath `.local/diagnostics/db-snapshots/`.

The runtime diagnostics directory must be owner-controlled mode `0700`; `runtime.json` and `thread-dumps.log` must be owner-owned, single-link regular files with mode `0600`. Open and replacement operations stay descriptor-relative to a validated pinned directory. Any unsafe existing artifact or directory-path replacement must fail closed and increment diagnostics degradation without following links, blocking on special files, or truncating an attacker-selected target.

Runtime snapshots and copyable reports are metadata-only. Never capture, persist, or print request bodies, user-controlled raw filenames, source text, Ask questions/answers, prompts/model messages, Memory/Knowhow content, or authorization headers, cookies, tokens, credentials, secrets, raw process command lines, or local variables; never persist or print SQL text or SQL parameters. Exact opaque notebook identifiers may exist only in the machine-local runtime snapshot for read-only correlation; reports pseudonymize notebook/request/job identifiers consistently and omit every other raw opaque id. Even sanitized output must be reviewed before sharing outside the trusted team.

All normal-path diagnostics wrappers are best-effort, exception-safe, and semantically invisible: instrumentation failures must never escape into the request/job/repository call, acquire the SQLite write lock, alter transaction behavior, or persist per-SQL events. All six commands stay Python-standard-library-only and must never import `app`. `base-recall` must use only the bounded, `O_NOATIME`-pinned, identity-validated DB/WAL copy owned by the diagnostics workspace; it must never construct a repository, run application retrieval, migrate, or open the source database through SQLite. Its report is fixed-field metadata with pseudonymous IDs and the same 32 KiB privacy bound. All six commands are read-only with respect to product data and must never execute delete/write SQL, checkpoint, vacuum, analyze, reindex, migrate, restart/terminate a process, or auto-remediate. Diagnostics may mutate only their own bounded `.local/diagnostics/` lock, heartbeat, dump, and temporary-snapshot artifacts, and must require neither root nor third-party packages for incident capture.

Historical readers must continue to cover and deduplicate legacy `<channel>.jsonl`, daily `<channel>-YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl`, daily gzip `<channel>-YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl.gz`, and one-level per-user directories for the `requests`, `events`, and `llm` channels, with byte/time/record bounds and malformed/truncated accounting. Any diagnostics change must update `README.md`, `README_zh.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `architecture.md`, and `scripts/README.md` together; internal observability remains exempt from `fangan_done.md` unless it implements a separate product-spec feature.

## Verification

Run:

```bash
bash scripts/check.sh
```

`scripts/check.sh` is the level-1 edit-time and PR/push offline gate. It runs three bounded
lanes concurrently: the stable backend pytest suite with default 12 backend pytest workers
(override with `BACKEND_PYTEST_WORKERS`), syntax/smoke/contract/harness
checks, and frontend tests plus the typechecking production build and the
package typecheck. Node's test runner and Vitest are each capped at four
workers so they do not oversubscribe the backend critical path. `next build`
must keep TypeScript errors fatal (`ignoreBuildErrors` stays unset) and stays
the fail-closed typecheck for production code, but Next's build-time type
checker silently drops every diagnostic in `*.test.*`/`*.spec.*` files and
`__tests__`/`__mocks__` directories (the ignoreRegex in
`next/dist/lib/typescript/runTypeCheck.js`, verified on Next 15.5), so a type
error that only exists under `frontend/tests/**` never fails the build. The
frontend lane therefore runs `npm run lint` (`tsc --noEmit`) after the build —
after, so it type-checks the freshly regenerated `.next/types` instead of a
stale tree — as the one pass that sees those files; with `incremental` the
warm re-check costs under a second (~5s cold), so the duplicate parse the lane
once avoided is no longer a budget concern. The level-1 backend lane excludes
the `slow` real-index/performance marker, the `graph_index_contract` cold graph/index
contracts, the `architecture_contract_heavy` subset (>2s per test) of the repository-wide
semantic source scans, and the separately authoritative PostgreSQL tree. The remaining,
cheap `architecture_contract` tests (<=2s each; see
`backend/tests/conftest.py::_ARCHITECTURE_CONTRACT_HEAVY_TESTS` for the exact heavy
allowlist) run in level 1 on every PR/push. Run
`scripts/check_extended.sh` for level 2: it reuses level 1 and adds the exact
backend complement. `.github/workflows/daily-extended.yml` runs level 2 once
daily at 18:17 UTC (02:17 Asia/Shanghai) and on manual dispatch; ordinary PRs
and pushes run level 1. Level 0 is change-focused test selection, and level 3
is the independent PostgreSQL integration gate.
Each lane owns a process group;
interrupting or terminating the controller must terminate and reap every
pytest/npm/Next.js descendant. Acceptance on the current Apple Silicon
development machine uses:

Codex-only execution rule: Codex must request outside-sandbox execution for
`scripts/check.sh` on the first attempt. Do not first run it in the sandbox:
backend lifecycle coverage binds loopback ports and controls subprocesses, so
sandbox failures add noise rather than useful signal. Codex must also request
outside-sandbox execution directly for GitHub network operations (`git fetch`,
`git push`, and `gh auth/repo/pr`); ordinary local read-only Git inspection remains
inside the sandbox.

Codex review sessions (`codex exec review`): do NOT re-run `scripts/check.sh`
or the frontend build/vitest lanes while reviewing a diff. The review sandbox
cannot write under `frontend/node_modules`, so those lanes fail with EPERM
regardless of code correctness, and the submitter already runs the full local
gate and posts its result on the PR. Review by reading the diff; when a claim
needs verification, prefer targeted read-only checks (focused pytest files,
contract scripts). This is a review-scope rule only — it does not relax any
gate for implementation sessions, where the full `scripts/check.sh` remains
mandatory before a PR.

```bash
PYTHON_BIN=/opt/homebrew/Caskroom/miniconda/base/bin/python bash scripts/check.sh
```

The Apple Silicon warm gate hard target is at most 60 seconds; CI lane timings are observational only, so this measured local target is not a portable timeout
assertion for every host.

Test-speed optimizations must preserve the same assertions and production defaults. The level-1 standard and level-2 extended backend marker expressions must remain exact complements, and PostgreSQL remains owned by `scripts/check_postgres.sh`; no committed test may become unreachable. Repository-wide syntax/contract discovery should parse each production file at most once per pytest process; tests of cache/container policy should use the policy object directly instead of constructing unrelated database/index artifacts; and lifecycle scripts may expose private `_SCRIPT_TEST_*` timing controls for tests while their unset production polling and timeout values remain unchanged. The autouse isolation fixture derives unique paths from each worker's existing pytest base temp rather than requesting a fresh `tmp_path` directory for every test. Concurrency tests use events/barriers for positive ordering and fairness assertions rather than depending on fixed sleeps or thread wake-up order; real-process lifecycle modules use dedicated xdist groups.
Ordinary SQLite repository tests copy a current empty schema built once per pytest worker instead of replaying the immutable migration ladder for every database. Every copy remains a separate mutable file. Tests that own migration, upgrade, or repository-snapshot behavior must stay in `_REAL_SQLITE_MIGRATION_MODULES` and execute the real ladder. Repository-heavy tests may replace only the default password-hash cost through the autouse fixture; authentication helpers still test the production default directly, and snapshot modules that compare persisted credential fields must stay in `_REAL_PASSWORD_HASH_MODULES`.
Tests that start delayed process-global work must also cancel pending work and wait for active work to quiesce in shared teardown before repository/database fixtures close; file-local cleanup is insufficient when an HTTP route and the test can resolve different repository objects. A barrier is valid only when every submitted participant belongs to that one rendezvous generation; when work is queued in waves, drive the synchronous subject from a controller thread and release it with events after observing the intended capacity.
Size-independent boundary branches may lower only the test-local threshold while separately asserting the shipped production floor. Tests that inspect multiple views of one immutable artifact share a single real build, and arithmetic/observability-only branch tests use the smallest owned seam instead of rebuilding that artifact; adjacent integration coverage must still build, open, and query the real artifact.

### GitHub Actions CI

- `.github/workflows/ci.yml` is a read-only wrapper around the level-1
  `scripts/check.sh`; `.github/workflows/daily-extended.yml` wraps only the
  level-2 `scripts/check_extended.sh`. Never duplicate test roots or frontend
  commands in either workflow.
- `CI / level-1-standard` runs on pull requests to `master`, pushes to `master`, and
  manual dispatches on `ubuntu-24.04` with Python 3.13, Node.js 22, and four
  backend pytest workers.
- `CI / level-1-frontend-node26` runs the frontend lane (`npm test`) and the
  production build on the current Node.js major, on the same triggers. It exists
  because the documented floor is "Node.js ≥ 20" while `level-1-standard` pins 22:
  Node ≥ 24 ships built-in Web Storage globals whose getters return `undefined`
  without `--localstorage-file`, and vitest's jsdom environment lets them shadow
  jsdom's own — every component test that reads `localStorage` fails on a
  developer's machine while CI stays green. `frontend/test-support/setup.ts`
  restores real jsdom storage (and the matching `Storage` class, so
  `vi.spyOn(Storage.prototype, …)` still intercepts) only when the built-ins are
  undefined, so Node 22 behavior is byte-identical. The lane also runs the build,
  which is what caught that fix's first attempt pulling in untyped `jsdom`.
- `Daily Extended Gate / level-2-extended` has exactly one daily cron
  (`17 18 * * *`) plus manual dispatch. Do not add PR/push triggers to it.
- `CI / level-3-postgres-integration` remains the separately authoritative
  PostgreSQL adapter integration check.
- Keep model/deployment secrets out of this workflow. Package-manager caches
  may contain downloads only; do not cache `node_modules`, virtualenvs,
  databases, or `.local` application state.
- CI must build `hnswlib` portably with `HNSWLIB_NO_NATIVE=1` and
  `pip --no-cache-dir`. Its source build otherwise uses `-march=native`, so a
  cached local wheel is unsafe to restore on a hosted runner with different
  CPU features and can terminate tests with `SIGILL`. Deployment wheelhouses
  may optimize only for their explicitly declared target CPU.
- The 20-minute hosted-runner timeout is not the local 60-second warm-gate
  target. Do not make the check required until stable green PR and post-merge
  runs have been observed and the user explicitly approves branch-protection
  changes.
- Every filesystem, data, and dependency path used by a CI-executed test is
  repository-relative and independent of the process cwd. Committed fixtures
  are located from `Path(__file__)`-anchored repository paths; tests must not
  embed developer checkout paths, depend on `HOME`, or read
  repository-external source documents.
- Any third-party package imported during test startup is a direct declared
  dependency in `backend/requirements.txt`; a clean CI install uses that file
  and `frontend/package-lock.json`. A developer's preinstalled package is never
  evidence that CI can install the gate.
- The byte-semantically frozen OpenAPI contract requires exact framework pins:
  FastAPI `0.135.3` and Pydantic `2.12.4`. Upgrade either pin only together with
  an intentional OpenAPI contract regeneration and a clean-environment full
  gate; do not replace these pins with open-ended minimum versions.
- Hosted-runner lane timings are observational. The under-60-second acceptance
  target applies to the verified Apple Silicon Homebrew warm gate, not a cold
  GitHub runner.
- Developer-only gold-generation/build/validation scripts that consume
  repository-external PDF parse output remain outside `scripts/check.sh`; this
  exception never applies to committed tests.
